In Kātyāyana's Sarvānukramaṇī, the difference between Āpra and Āprī is stated (this difference is given at the end of the Āprī-sūkta, that is, of Sūkta 1-142), showing that Ṛks addressed to the deity Narāśaṃsa are known as Āpra mantras, and Ṛks addressed to the deity Tanūnapāt as Āprī mantras.

In the Bṛhaddevatā:

द्वितीये द्वादशर्चॆ तु प्रत्यृचं यास्तु देवताः । स्तूयंते ह्यग्निना सार्धं तासां नामानि मे शृणु ॥ प्रथमायां स्तुतश्चेध्मो द्वितीयां तनूनपात् । नराशंसस्त्रीयायां चतुर्थ्यां स्तूयते त्वीणः ॥ बर्हिरेव तु पंचम्यां द्वारो देव्यस्ततोऽन्यया ॥ नक्तोषासा तु सप्तम्यामष्टम्यां संस्तुत् सह । देव्याविति तु होतारौ नवम्यामृचि संस्तुताः ॥ तिस्रो देव्यो दशम्यां तु ज्ञेयस्त्वष्टैव तु स्तुतः । (*dvitīye dvādaśarcè tu pratyṛcaṃ yāstu devatāḥ | stūyaṃte hyagninā sārdhaṃ tāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu ‖ prathamāyāṃ stutaścedhmo dvitīyāṃ tanūnapāt | narāśaṃsastrīyāyāṃ caturthyāṃ stūyate tvīṇaḥ ‖ barhireva tu paṃcamyāṃ dvāro devyastato 'nyayā ‖ naktoṣāsā tu saptamyāmaṣṭamyāṃ saṃstut saha | devyāviti tu hotārau navamyāmṛci saṃstutāḥ ‖ tisro devyo daśamyāṃ tu jñeyastvaṣṭaiva tu stutaḥ |*)

### Printed p. 227
एकादश्यां तु सूक्तस्य स्तुतं विद्याद्वनस्पतिं ॥ द्वादश्यां तु स्तुता देवीर्विद्यात्स्वाहाकृतीरिति । सूक्तेऽस्मिन्प्रत्यृचं यास्तु देवताः परिकीर्तिताः ॥ ता एव सर्वास्वाप्रीषु द्वितीया तु विकल्पते । (*ekādaśyāṃ tu sūktasya stutaṃ vidyādvanaspatiṃ ‖ dvādaśyāṃ tu stutā devīrvidyātsvāhākṛtīriti | sūkte 'sminpratyṛcaṃ yāstu devatāḥ parikīrtitāḥ ‖ tā eva sarvāsvāprīṣu dvitīyā tu vikalpate |*) (Bṛ. De. 2-146 to 151)

Thus, naming all the deities beginning with Idhma, it states the order of the sūkta and that in each Ṛk a separate deity is addressed.

Speaking of how many Āprī-sūktas there are, the Nirukta authors say:

इतीमान्येकादशाप्रीसूक्तानि । तेषां वासिष्ठमात्रेयं वाध्यश्वं गार्त्समदमिति नाराशंसवंति ; मैधातिथं दैर्घतमसं प्रैषिकमित्युभयवंत्यतोऽन्यानि तनूनपात्वंति तनूनपात्वंति । (*itīmānyekādaśāprīsūktāni | teṣāṃ vāsiṣṭhamātreyaṃ vādhyaśvaṃ gārtsamadamiti nārāśaṃsavaṃti ; maidhātithaṃ dairghatamasaṃ praiṣikamityubhayavaṃtyato 'nyāni tanūnapātvaṃti tanūnapātvaṃti |*) (Ni. 8-22)

Among these sūktas, the hymns seen by Vasiṣṭha, Atri, Vadhryaśva and Gṛtsamada are devoted to Narāśaṃsa. Those seen by the seers Medhātithi and Dīrghatamas, and also the Praiṣa-sūkta, are addressed to both deities, Tanūnapāt and Narāśaṃsa. The remaining four sūktas are devoted solely to the deity Tanūnapāt. In all (counting the Praiṣa-sūkta) there are eleven Āprī-sūktas. The same view is also given in the Bṛhaddevatā:

प्रैषैः सहाप्रीसूक्तानि तान्येकादश संति च । यजूंषि प्रैषसूक्तं वा दशैतानीतराणि तु ॥ सौत्रामण्यानि तु त्रीणि प्राजापत्याश्वमेधिके । पुरुषस्य तु यन्मेधे यजुःस्वेव तु तानि षट् ॥ अत्रैव प्रैषसूक्तं स्यान्नयजुष्वाद्रियेत तत् । तेषां प्रैषगतं सूक्तं यच्च दीर्घतमा जगौ ॥ मेधातिथौ यदुक्तं च त्रीण्यपोभयवंति तु । ऋषौ गृत्समदे यच्च नाध्य श्वे च यमच्यते ॥ नराशंसवदत्रेश्च ददर्श च यदौर्वशः । तनूनपादगस्त्यश्च जमदग्निश्च यज्जगौ ॥ विश्वामित्र ऋषिर्यच्च जगौ वै काश्यपोऽसितः । मेधातिथेर्ऋचां यास्तु प्रोक्ता द्वादश देवताः ॥ संपद्यंते यथाग्निं ताः संपदं तां निबोधत । (*praiṣaiḥ sahāprīsūktāni tānyekādaśa saṃti ca | yajūṃṣi praiṣasūktaṃ vā daśaitānītarāṇi tu ‖ sautrāmaṇyāni tu trīṇi prājāpatyāśvamedhike | puruṣasya tu yanmedhe yajuḥsveva tu tāni ṣaṭ ‖ atraiva praiṣasūktaṃ syānnayajuṣvādriyeta tat | teṣāṃ praiṣagataṃ sūktaṃ yacca dīrghatamā jagau ‖ medhātithau yaduktaṃ ca trīṇyapobhayavaṃti tu | ṛṣau gṛtsamade yacca nādhya śve ca yamacyate ‖ narāśaṃsavadatreśca dadarśa ca yadaurvaśaḥ | tanūnapādagastyaśca jamadagniśca yajjagau ‖ viśvāmitra ṛṣiryacca jagau vai kāśyapo 'sitaḥ | medhātitherṛcāṃ yāstu proktā dvādaśa devatāḥ ‖ saṃpadyaṃte yathāgniṃ tāḥ saṃpadaṃ tāṃ nibodhata |*) (Bṛ. De. 2-152 to 157)

### Printed p. 228
In these statements it is said that if the Praiṣa-sūkta is included there are eleven Āprī-sūktas in all, and otherwise ten. And, following the Nirukta statement itself, the order is given of the sūktas that are addressed to deities such as Narāśaṃsa and Tanūnapāt and that were composed by seers such as Medhātithi.

The Praiṣa-sūkta is well ordered, and gives a definite picture by gathering together the essence of all the Āprī-sūktas. It collects together all the individual epithets of the deities addressed in the Āprī-sūktas and sets out separately the nature and duty of each deity. In addressing each deity, it first utters a fixed portion of mantra, होता यक्षत् । (*hotā yakṣat |*), and then indicates the respective deities. By first examining the deity, the deity's epithet and the duty described in the Praiṣa-sūkta, one can afterwards understand the agreement or the differences of opinion regarding what the purpose of such Āprī-sūktas with these deities is, what their characteristics are, and what benefit comes from reciting them.

Table (number — deity — epithets):
- 1. अग्निः (समित्) (*agniḥ (samit)*)—समिधा, सुसमिधा, समिद्धः (*samidhā, susamidhā, samiddhaḥ*)
- 2. अग्निः (तनूनपात्) (*agniḥ (tanūnapāt)*)—अदितेः गर्भं भुवनस्य गोपां (*aditeḥ garbhaṃ bhuvanasya gopāṃ*)
- 3. अग्निः (नराशंसः) (*agniḥ (narāśaṃsaḥ)*)—नृशस्त्रं नृः प्रणेत्रं (*nṛśastraṃ nṛḥ praṇetraṃ*)
- 4. अग्निः (इळः) (*agniḥ (iḷaḥ)*)—ईळितो देवः, हव्यवाट्, अमूरः (*īḷito devaḥ, havyavāṭ, amūraḥ*)
- 5. बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—सुष्टरीम, ऊर्णम्रद, देवेभ्यः, स्नासस्थं (*suṣṭarīma, ūrṇamrada, devebhyaḥ, snāsasthaṃ*)
- 6. देवीर्द्वारः (*devīrdvāraḥ*)—ऋष्वाः, कवश्यः, कोषधावनीः (*ṛṣvāḥ, kavaśyaḥ, koṣadhāvanīḥ*)
- 7. उषासानक्ता (*uṣāsānaktā*)—*bṛhatī, supeśasā patibhyaḥ yoniṃ kṛṇavāste saṃsmayamāne*
- 8. दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*)—मंद्रा, पोतारा, कवी, प्रचेतसा (*maṃdrā, potārā, kavī, pracetasā*)
- 9. तिस्रो देवीः (*tisro devīḥ*)—अपसां अपस्तमाः देवीः (*apasāṃ apastamāḥ devīḥ*)
- 10. त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—*aciṣṭaḥ, apākaḥ, retodhāḥ, viśravas, yaśodhāḥ, pururūpaḥ, akāmakarśana*
- 11. वनस्पतिः (*vanaspatiḥ*)—*dhiyo jyeṣṭhā, svadāna svadhitiḥ, devebhyaḥ ṛtuthā havyavāṭ, yajñasya, āyuḥ kalpamānaḥ*
- 12. अग्निः (स्याहा) (*agniḥ (syāhā)*)—आज्यस्य मेदसः स्वाहा (*ājyasya medasaḥ svāhā*)

In this manner the Praiṣa-sūkta, gathering the main points of all the Āprī-sūktas, has given a well-ordered picture. The modes of composition of these Āprī-sūktas, their styles, and in the sacrificial work…

### Printed p. 229
In this way the Praiṣa-sūkta has gathered the main points of all the Āprī-sūktas and given an orderly picture. The manner in which these Āprī-sūktas are composed, their styles, and their uses in the sacrificial work, as well as their poetic character and the opinions that modern scholars have expressed about them, will be stated at the end of this sūkta (1-142), after its explanation is complete.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समि॑द्धो अग्न॒ आ न॑ह दे॒वा अ॒द्य य॒तस्रु॑चे ।  
> तंतुं॑ तनुष्व पू॒र्व्यं सु॒तसो॑माय दा॒शुषे॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संइद्दः । अग्ने । आ । वह । देवान् । अद्य । यतस्रुचे ।  
> तंतुं । तनुष्व । पूर्व्यं । सुत्यसोमाय । दाशुषे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने समिद्धः सम्यग्दीपित एतन्नामकस्त्वमद्यास्मिन्यागदिने देवान् हविषां
> भोक्तानग्न्यादीना वह।अस्मद्यज्ञं प्रति प्रापय । यतस्रुचे यागायोद्यतस्रुचे
> यजमानाय तदर्थं । आवाह्य च सुतसोमाय यागार्थमभिषुतसोमाय
> दाशुषे हविर्दत्तवते । यद्यपीदानीमेवाहूयंते देवाः तथापि पूर्वानुष्ठानापेक्षया भाव्यपेक्षया ना दाशुष इत्युच्यते । हविर्दात्रे वा यजमानाय
> तदर्थं पूर्व्यं पूर्णेभ्यो रेवेभ्यो हितं पूर्वकालीनं वा तंतुं यज्ञं
> तनुष्स्व । विस्तारय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, you who are named Samiddha, "well kindled": on this day of the sacrifice bring the gods, the enjoyers of the oblations, such as Agni and the others, and make them reach our sacrifice. Bring them for the sacrificer who has raised the ladle (यतस्रुचे (*yatasruce*)) for the rite, and who has pressed the Soma (सुतसोमाय (*sutasomāya*)) for the sacrifice, and who has given the oblation (दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)). Although the gods are being invited only now, he is called "giver" with regard to the earlier performance, or in view of what will come. Or else दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*) means the sacrificer who gives the oblation. For him extend (तनुष्व (*tanuṣva*), "spread") the sacrifice (तंतुं (*taṃtuṃ*), the thread), which is पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*): beneficial to the former ones, or belonging to former times. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- समिद्धः (*samiddhaḥ*)—(well blazing) you who are renowned as Samiddha
- अद्य (*adya*)—at this time of the sacrifice
- यतस्रुचे (*yatasruce*)—for the sacrificer who holds the ladle for the sacrifice
- देवा (*devā*)—the gods (who receive the oblation)
- आ वह (*ā vaha*)—bring and unite
- सुतसोमाय (*sutasomāya*)—for the one who has pressed the Soma juice
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—and offers the oblation, the sacrificer
- पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)—ancient
- तंतु (*taṃtu*)—the sacrifice
- तनुष्प (*tanuṣpa*)—extend and bring to completion

### Printed p. 230

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, you who are well blazing and renowned as Samiddha: at this time of the sacrifice bring and unite the gods, who receive the oblation, for the sacrificer who stands ready with the ladle for the sacrifice. And for the sacrificer who has pressed the Soma juice and offers the oblation, extend and bring to completion the ancient sacrifice.

##### Printed English

Agni, who is Samiddha, bring the gods to-day (to the worhipper). whose ladle is uplifted : extend (the merit of) former sacrifice to the giver (of the Soma libation) is poured forth

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

समिद्धः (*samiddhaḥ*)—सम्यग्नीपितः एतन्नामकः त्वं (*samyagnīpitaḥ etannāmakaḥ tvaṃ*). Sāyaṇa explains this as "you, renowned by the name Samiddha". It is addressed to Samiddha Agni, who holds the first place in the Āprī-sūkta.

इध्मो यः सर्वमेवाग्निरयं हीध्मः समिध्यते । ध्यातेनैव तत्कृतं रूपं ध्मातो हीध्मः समिध्यते ॥ (*idhmo yaḥ sarvamevāgnirayaṃ hīdhmaḥ samidhyate | dhyātenaiva tatkṛtaṃ rūpaṃ dhmāto hīdhmaḥ samidhyate ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-158)

Here identity is stated between the fuel-wood इध्म (*idhma*) and the all-pervading Agni. The word इध्म (*idhma*) is derived from the root ध्या (*dhyā*). The author of the Bṛhaddevatā gives this etymology of the words इध्म (*idhma*) and समिद्ध (*samiddha*): the name is because it blazes after being blown upon. This etymology follows the explanation of the Nirukta author. In the Nirukta too, concerning the first deity of the Āprī-sūkta, it says:

शासामिध्मः प्रथमागामी भवति । इद्मः समिंधनात् । (*śāsāmidhmaḥ prathamāgāmī bhavati | idmaḥ samiṃdhanāt |*) (Ni. 8-5)

In the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa too, at the beginning of the Praiṣa-sūkta:

होता यक्षदग्निग्ं समिधा सुषमिधा समिद्धं नाभा प्रथिव्याः संगथे वामस्य । (*hotā yakṣadagnigṃ samidhā suṣamidhā samiddhaṃ nābhā prathivyāḥ saṃgathe vāmasya |*) (Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-1)

Thus the priests are told to perform the sacrifice to Agni who is called Samidh and who is accompanied by the first Prayāja deity.

### Printed p. 231
One more matter must be noted here. The designation "Idhma" as a name does not occur in the Āprī-sūktas, and this name is not in the Praiṣa-sūkta either. Only in the Sarvānukramaṇī is it indicated:

सुसमिद्ध इतीध्म समिद्धो ना अग्नि...........इति प्रत्यृचं देवताः । (*susamiddha itīdhma samiddho nā agni...........iti pratyṛcaṃ devatāḥ |*)

Since this word is not used in this context in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa either, it is clear that Śaunaka and Kātyāyana, in using it, followed the Nirukta author. Moreover, the epithet Samiddha or Susamiddha is used in seven sūktas, and the epithet Samidh in the remaining three sūktas, Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-4-1; 7-2-1; 10-60-1. The first duty of this Agni called Samiddha is to invite the gods. In the opening Ṛks of the sūktas 1-13-1; 1-142-1; 3-4-1; 10-110-1, the prayer सुसमिद्धो न आ वह देवा अग्ने हविष्मते (*susamiddho na ā vaha devā agne haviṣmate*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-13-1) indicates Agni's duty; and not only that, he is also prayed to carry the oblations and the rest.

Whether this deity is Agni or not, there has been a difference of opinion from the beginning. The Nirukta author cites this difference and then gives his own view. He states the views of the respective sages, यज्ञेध्म इति कात्थक्यः (*yajñedhma iti kātthakyaḥ*) (Ni. 8-5); अग्निरिति शाकपूणिः (*agniriti śākapūṇiḥ*) (8-5), and then his own view: आग्नेया इति तु स्थितिः (*āgneyā iti tu sthitiḥ*) (Ni. 8-22). In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa,

समिधो यजति । प्राणा वै समिधः । प्राणा हीदं सर्वं समिंधते यदिदं किंच प्राणानेव तत्प्रीणाति प्राणान्यजमाने दधाति । (*samidho yajati | prāṇā vai samidhaḥ | prāṇā hīdaṃ sarvaṃ samiṃdhate yadidaṃ kiṃca prāṇāneva tatprīṇāti prāṇānyajamāne dadhāti |*) (Ai. Brā. 2-4)

by these sentences identity between Samidh and Prāṇa is stated; even so, on the strength of many other authorities it may be said that this deity must be Agni.

तंतुं (*taṃtuṃ*)—Here the word तंतु (*taṃtu*) is explained as "sacrifice". It is called तंतु (*taṃtu*) because it is threaded through without break, and because it grows and spreads, made up of many kinds of parts. The epithet तंतु (*taṃtu*) applies to all things that have these two qualities. For example:

तंतुं तन्नन्रजसो भानुमन्विहि ज्योतिष्मतः पथो रक्ष धिया कृतान् । अनुल्बणं वयत जोगुवामपो मनुर्भव जनया दैव्यं जनं । (*taṃtuṃ tannanrajaso bhānumanvihi jyotiṣmataḥ patho rakṣa dhiyā kṛtān | anulbaṇaṃ vayata joguvāmapo manurbhava janayā daivyaṃ janaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-53-6)

Having cited this passage, in the other Brāhmaṇas,

### Printed p. 232
तंतुं तन्नन्रजसो भानुमन्विहीति प्राजापत्यां शंसति प्रजा वै तंतुः प्रजा मेवास्मा एतत्संतनोति । (*taṃtuṃ tannanrajaso bhānumanvihīti prājāpatyāṃ śaṃsati prajā vai taṃtuḥ prajā mevāsmā etatsaṃtanoti |*) (Ai. Brā. 3-28)

This sentence shows that sacrifice, light and the continuation of progeny are all denoted by the word तंतु (*taṃtu*), which supports the meaning given above.

##### Grammar

- समिद्धः—सम् उपसर्गः । इंइंधी दीप्तौ धातुः । निष्ठा इति क्त प्रत्ययः । अनिदितां हल इति उपधानकारलोपः । झषस्त (पा. सू. ८-२-४०) इति प्रत्ययस्य धत्वम् । पूर्वस्य जस्त्वम् । गतिरनंतरः (पा. सू. ६-२-४९) इति गतेः (सम्) प्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- वह—वह प्रापणे धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति ।
- यतस्रुचे—यता स्रुक येन सः यतस्रुक् । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्यापूर्वपदम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति । चतुर्थीएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- तनुष्प—तनु विस्तारे धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिज्जतिजः इति तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- पूर्व्यम्—पूर्वैकृतमिनयौ च (पा. सू. ४-४-१३३) इति पूर्वशब्दात् यप्रत्ययः । यस्येति च इति अकारलोपः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति । द्वितीया एकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- सुतसोमाय—षुञ् अभिषवे धातुः । कर्मणि क्त प्रत्ययः । बहुव्रीहिसमासः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- दाशुषे—दाश्य दाने धातुः । दाश्वान् साह्वान् (पा. सू. ६-१-१२) इति क्वसुप्रत्ययांतः निपातितः । चतुर्थी एकवचने परे भसंज्ञात्वात् वसोः संप्रसारणम् इति संप्रसारणम् । संप्रसारणाच्च इति पूर्वरूपम् । शासिवसिघसीनां च (पा. सू. ८-३-६०) इति प्रत्ययसकारस्य षत्वम् ।

### Printed p. 233
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> घृ॒तवं॑त॒मुप॑ मासि॒ मधु॑मंतं तनूनपात् ।  
> य॒ज्ञं विप्र॑स्य॒ माव॑तः शशमा॒नस्य॑ दा॒शुषः॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> घृतवंतं । उप । मासि । मधुमंतं । तनूनपात् ।  
> यज्ञं । विप्रस्य । मावतः । शशमानस्य । दाशुषः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे तनूनपात् । तनूनपादाज्यमिति कात्कृक्यपक्षे तन्नो गावो भोगानां
> तासु जायमानत्वात् ताभ्य उत्पन्नं क्षीरं क्षीरात्सर्पिरित्याज्यस्य नप्तृत्वं । तद्वान्यज्ञः । अग्निपक्ष आपस्तन्न उच्यंते । ताभ्य ओषधिवनस्पतयः । तेभ्योऽग्निरिति
> नप्तृत्वं । अरणीभ्यामिति भावः । एतन्नामक हे अग्ने विप्रस्य मेधाविनः शशमानस्य शंसमानस्य त्वां स्तुवतो दाशुषो हविर्दत्तवतो मावतो मत्सदृशस्य
> यजमानस्य ॥ युष्मदस्मद्भ्यां छंदसि सादृश्य इति मतुप् । आ सर्वनाम्न इत्यात्वं ॥
> धृतवंतं क्षरदाज्योपेतं मधुमंतं माधुर्योपेतं सोमादिमधुरद्रव्येण
> तद्वंतं यज्ञमुपोपेत्य मासि । निर्मिमाषे । मितो भवसि वा । संपूर्तिपर्यंतं
> तिष्ठसीत्यर्थः ॥ मा माने । आदादिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*). On the view of Kātthakya, तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) is the ghee (आज्य (*ājya*)): *tanvaḥ* are cows, the means of enjoyment, and since milk is born from them, and ghee from milk, ghee is their "grandson" (नप्तृत्वं (*naptṛtvaṃ*)); the sacrifice possesses it. On the Agni view, the waters are called *tanvaḥ*; from them come the plants and trees, and from them Agni, hence the grandson-relation; the sense is: from the two kindling-sticks. O Agni of this name, of the wise (विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)) sacrificer, who praises you (शशमानस्य (*śaśamānasya*)), who has given oblation (दाशुषः (*dāśuṣaḥ*)), and who is like me (मावतः (*māvataḥ*), "resembling me"): मावतः (*māvataḥ*) is formed with matup in the sense of similarity, by the rule that pronouns in the Veda take such a form, with lengthening to ā. Come near to the sacrifice that is ghee-possessing (धृतवंतं (*dhṛtavaṃtaṃ*), with dripping ghee) and honey-possessing (मधुमंतं (*madhumaṃtaṃ*), with sweetness, with Soma and other sweet substances), and मासि (*māsi*): you are measured out, or you are limited; the meaning is that you remain until completion. The root मा (*mā*) is in the sense of measuring, of the Adādi class.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*)—O Agni named "Tanūnapāt"
- विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)—of the wise one
- शशमानस्य (*śaśamānasya*)—who praises (you)
- दाशुषः (*dāśuṣaḥ*)—who offers the oblation
- मावतः (*māvataḥ*)—who is, like me, (full of faith)
- of the sacrificer
- धृतवंतं (*dhṛtavaṃtaṃ*)—filled with ghee
- मधुमंतं (*madhumaṃtaṃ*)—and furnished with sweet substances such as Soma
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—to the sacrifice
- उप (*upa*)—graciously
- मासि (*māsi*)—remain until the sacrifice is complete

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, famed as "Tanūnapāt": to the sacrifice of the sacrificer who is wise, who worships you, who offers the oblation, and who is full of faith like me, a sacrifice filled with ghee

### Printed p. 234
and furnished with Soma and other sweet substances, graciously come, and remain here until the completion of the sacrifice.

##### Printed English

Tanunapat, be present at this well-flavoured and butter-fed sacrifice, (the offering) of a pious offerer (of oblations) ; glcrifying you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the word तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*), Sāyaṇa, on the basis of the Nirukta, gives two kinds of meaning. First: तनूनपात् आज्यं इति कात्क्यपक्षे तन्नो गावो भोगानां तासु जायमानत्वात् । ताभ्यः उत्पन्नं क्षीरं क्षीरात्सर्पिः इति आज्यस्य नप्तृत्वं । तद्वान् यज्ञः । (*tanūnapāt ājyaṃ iti kātkyapakṣe tanno gāvo bhogānāṃ tāsu jāyamānatvāt | tābhyaḥ utpannaṃ kṣīraṃ kṣīrātsarpiḥ iti ājyasya naptṛtvaṃ | tadvān yajñaḥ |*) If the meaning of Kātkya, that तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) is ghee, is accepted, then the word तनु (*tanu*) means cow, and since ghee arises from the milk produced from it, तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) means ghee; or he has explained it as the sacrifice accompanied by ghee. In this connection, from the Nirukta author's sentence नपादित्यनंतरायाः प्रजाया नामधेयं निर्णिततमा भवति (*napādityanaṃtarāyāḥ prajāyā nāmadheyaṃ nirṇitatamā bhavati*) (Ni. 8-5), the word नपात् (*napāt*) means grandson, so here too the meaning is the ghee produced from the milk born of the cow. Second: अग्नि पक्षे अपस्तन्व उच्यंते । ताभ्यः ओषधिवनस्पतियः, तेभ्योSग्निरिति नप्तृत्वं । अरणीभ्यां इति भावः । (*agni pakṣe apastanva ucyaṃte | tābhyaḥ oṣadhivanaspatiyaḥ, tebhyoSgniriti naptṛtvaṃ | araṇībhyāṃ iti bhāvaḥ |*) The word तनु (*tanu*) means water; from it the plants and trees (the kindling-sticks) arise and from them Agni is born, hence the name तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*). Thus two kinds of meaning are given. In the Bṛhaddevatā:

अयं तनूनपादग्नि रसौ हि तननात्तनुः । ततस्तु मध्यमो जज्ञे स्थानेयं मध्यमात्ततः ॥ अनंतरां प्रजामाहुर्नपादिति कृपण्यवः । नपादमुष्यचैवायं अग्निस्तेन तनूनपात् ॥ (*ayaṃ tanūnapādagni rasau hi tananāttanuḥ | tatastu madhyamo jajñe sthāneyaṃ madhyamāttataḥ ‖ anaṃtarāṃ prajāmāhurnapāditi kṛpaṇyavaḥ | napādamuṣyacaivāyaṃ agnistena tanūnapāt ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-76 to 27) **NOTE**

तनूनपादयं त्वेव नाम्ना यच्छत्वसौ तनुं । नपादिति प्रजा माहुरमुतोऽस्य च संभवं । (*tanūnapādayaṃ tveva nāmnā yacchatvasau tanuṃ | napāditi prajā māhuramuto 'sya ca saṃbhavaṃ |*) (Bṛ. De. 3-1)

This terrestrial Agni is the nephew/grandson (नपात् (*napāt*)) of the Agni of the divine world, in this way: the divine Agni, being endowed with the power of spreading (तननात् (*tananāt*)), is called by the synonym तनु (*tanu*). From him arises the Agni of the atmosphere, in the form of lightning, and from that this terrestrial Agni; hence the terrestrial Agni has the name तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*); and also because, spreading out from his own body, he came into being in this form

### Printed p. 235

(The sentence from the previous page continues:) it has been explained that the name Tanūnapāt arises in this way. The relation between this form of Agni and the Agni in the form of Narāśaṃsa, and the difference between them, will be explained in the Special Topics of the next Ṛk.

- मासि (*māsi*)—निर्मिमीषे मितो भवसि वा । संपूर्तिपर्यंतं तिष्ठसीति वा । (*nirmimīṣe mito bhavasi vā | saṃpūrtiparyaṃtaṃ tiṣṭhasīti vā |*) The meaning is: remain until the sacrifice is completely accomplished.
- विप्रस्य (*viprasya*)—the word विप्र (*vipra*) is read among the names for the wise (Ni. 3-19). Of the sacrificer, who is wise and a praiser.
- मावतः (*māvataḥ*)—मत्सदृशस्य यजमानस्य । (*matsadṛśasya yajamānasya |*) The meaning is: of a sacrificer who is endowed with faith just as I am.

##### Grammar

- धृतवंतम् — धृतमस्यास्ति इति धृतवान् । तम् । तदस्यास्ति इति सूत्रेण मतुप् प्रत्ययः । पित्त्वात् अनुदात्तो भवति ।
- मासि — मा माने इति धातुः । अदादिः । लट् मध्यमपुरुषैकवचने सिप् प्रत्ययः । अदिप्रभृतिभ्यः शपः इति शसि लुक् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति । **NOTE**
- तनूनपंत् — आमंत्रितस्य च (पा. सू. ८-१-१९) इति निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- यज्ञम् — यज देवपूजासंगतिकरणदानेषु इति धातुः । यजयाच—(पा. स. ३-३-९०) इति नज् प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति । द्वितीयाएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- मावतः — युष्मदस्मदोः सादृश्ये वतुप् वाच्यः (पा. सू. ५-१-६५-२) इति सादृश्यार्थे प्रतीयमाने अस्मद् शब्दात् वतुप् प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययोत्तरपदयोश्च (पा. सू. ७-२-९८) इति प्रकृतेः मादेशः । आ सर्वनाम्नः (पा. सू. ६-३-९१) इति वतुप् निमित्तम् आत्वम् । वतुप् पित्त्वात् अनुदात्तो भवति ।
- शशमानस्य — शंसमान इति भवति स्थले शशमान इति रूपं भवति । चित्त्वात् चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति । षष्ठीएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- दाशुषः — पूर्वस्मिन् मंत्रे व्याख्यातम् । षष्ठीएकवचनांतरूपम् ।

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शुचिः॑ पाव॒को अद्भु॑तो॒ मध्वा॑ य॒ज्ञं मि॑मिक्षति ।  
> नरा॒शंस॒स्त्रिरा दि॒वो दे॒वो दे॒वेषु॑ य॒ज्जियः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुचिः । पावकः । अद्भुतः । मध्वा । यज्ञं । मिमिक्षति ।  
> नराशंसः । त्रिः । आ । दिवः । देवः । देवेषु । यज्जियः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देवेषु मध्यॆ शुचिः शुद्धो दीप्तो वा पावकोऽन्नस्यापूतस्य शोधयिताद्भुतोऽभूत इव अद्यक्षणे भविता । आश्चर्यभूत इत्यर्थः । देवो द्योतन
> शीलो यज्ञियो यज्ञ संपादनार्हः ईदृशो नराशंसो नरैः कर्मनेतृभिर्ऋत्विग्निः शंसनीयोऽग्निर्दिपो द्युलोकादागत्य मध्वा मधुरेण रसेन यज्ञ
> मस्मदीयं त्रिरा मिमिक्षति । त्रिवारं समंतात्सिंचति । स्वदयतीत्यर्थः । यद्वा ।
> मधुरेण फलेन त्रिरा मिमिक्षति । योजयतीत्यर्थः । नराशंसो यज्ञ इति कात्थक्यपक्षे यज्ञाभिमानी देवो द्योतमानो द्युलोकादागत्यानुष्ठीयमानं
> यज्ञं त्रिरा मिमिक्षति । आयं नराशंसो देवस्त्रिरा ॥ उपसर्गवशाज्योग्यक्रियाध्याहारः ॥ त्रिवारमागच्छति । त्रिराहरति वा फलं । अवशिष्टं पूर्ववन्नेयं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Among the gods, Agni is शुचिः (*śuciḥ*), pure or shining, and पावकः (*pāvakaḥ*), the purifier of unpurified food. He is अद्भुतः (*adbhutaḥ*): as though he had not been, he comes into being in this very moment; that is, he is a wonder. He is देवः (*devaḥ*), of a shining nature, and यज्ञियः (*yajñiyaḥ*), fit to accomplish the sacrifice. Such is Narāśaṃsa, Agni, to be praised by men, that is, by the priests who lead the rite. Coming from the heavenly world, with sweet juice he त्रिरा मिमिक्षति (*trirā mimikṣati*) our sacrifice: he sprinkles it all round three times, that is, he makes it sweet. Or else: with sweet fruit he joins it three times, that is, he unites it. On the view of Kātthakya, that Narāśaṃsa is the sacrifice, the deity presiding over the sacrifice, shining, comes from the heavenly world and thrice mixes the sacrifice being performed. Or: this god Narāśaṃsa त्रिरा (*trirā*): by force of the preverb a suitable verb is to be supplied; he comes three times, or he brings the fruit three times. The rest is to be understood as before.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—among the gods
- शुचिः (*śuciḥ*)—the pure one
- पावकः (*pāvakaḥ*)—the one who purifies (the food)
- अद्भुतः (*adbhutaḥ*)—the wonderful one
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—the shining one
- यज्ञियः (*yajñiyaḥ*)—fit for accomplishing the sacrifice
- नराशंसः (*narāśaṃsaḥ*)—Agni, famed as Narāśaṃsa (the one praised by the priests)
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—having come from the heavenly world
- मध्वा (*madhvā*)—with sweet juice or with fruit
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the sacrifice
- त्रिः (*triḥ*)—three times
- आ मिमिक्षति (*ā mimikṣati*)—joins all around, or brings the fruit also in threefold repetition at all three sacrificial times

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, who among the gods is pure, purifies the food, is wonderful, shining, fit for accomplishing the sacrifice, and famed as Narāśaṃsa, comes from the heavenly world and joins this sacrifice of ours three times with sweet fruit.

### Printed p. 237

##### Printed English

The pure, purifying, wonderful Narashansa, an adorable god among gods, (having come) from heaven, thrice mixes the sacrifice with the sweet (juice of the Soma).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नराशंसः (*narāśaṃsaḥ*)—नरैः शंसनीयः (अग्निः) । (*naraiḥ śaṃsanīyaḥ (agniḥ) |*) "Agni who is praised by men": this is a name of Agni by its meaning. Or नराशंसोऽग्निर्यज्ञो वा (*narāśaṃso 'gniryajño vā*): according to another view Narāśaṃsa means either Agni or the sacrifice. A passage of the Nirukta supports both these opinions.

नराशंसो यज्ञ इति कात्थक्यः। नरा अस्मिन्नासीनाः शंसंति । अग्निरिति शाकपूणिः। नरैः प्रशस्यो भवति । (*narāśaṃso yajña iti kātthakyaḥ| narā asminnāsīnāḥ śaṃsaṃti | agniriti śākapūṇiḥ| naraiḥ praśasyo bhavati |*) (Ni. 8-7). Because men sit in the sacrifice and praise, the sacrifice has the name Narāśaṃsa, says Kātthakya; because Agni is to be praised by men he has this name, says Śākapūṇi. Having indicated these two opinions, the author of the Nirukta then cites the Ṛk

नराशंसस्य महिमानमेषामुप स्तोषाम यजतस्य यज्ञैः । ये सुकृतवः शुचयो धियंधाः स्वदंति देवा उभयानि हव्या ॥ (*narāśaṃsasya mahimānameṣāmupa stoṣāma yajatasya yajñaiḥ | ye sukṛtavaḥ śucayo dhiyaṃdhāḥ svadaṃti devā ubhayāni havyā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-2-2).

In this Ṛk the meaning "Agni" alone is proper. Among the deities enumerated in the Nighaṇṭu, the deity Narāśaṃsa is understood to be one of the forms of Agni. The authors of the Bṛhaddevatā say the same in the verse

नराशंसस्तितश्चैनमेनमेवाश्रितस्त्वस्थळः । बहिर्द्वारश्च देव्योग्निमेनमेव तु संश्रिताः ॥ (*narāśaṃsastitaścainamenamevāśritastvasthaḷaḥ | bahirdvāraśca devyognimenameva tu saṃśritāḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 1-107),

in which they say that Narāśaṃsa, like the other deities mentioned there, is contained within Agni. And

पृथक्त्वेन समासैस्तु यज्ञे यच्छस्यते नृभिः । स्तुवंत्याप्रीषु तेनेमं नराशंसं तु कारवः ॥ (*pṛthaktvena samāsaistu yajñe yacchasyate nṛbhiḥ | stuvaṃtyāprīṣu tenemaṃ narāśaṃsaṃ tu kāravaḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-28):

because he is praised separately by all the men gathered at the sacrifice, the poets praise him (Agni) in the Āprī hymns by the name Narāśaṃsa. Saying this, they too have shown the opinion stated by the authors of the Nirukta.

### Printed p. 238

नराशंसमिहैके तु अग्निमाहुरथेतरे । नराः शंसंति सर्वेऽस्मिन्नासीना इति नाध्वरे ॥ एतमेवाहुरन्येऽग्निं नराशंसोd्ध्वरे ह्ययं । नरैः प्रशस्य आसीनैराहुश्चैवर्त्विजो नरः ॥ (*narāśaṃsamihaike tu agnimāhurathetare | narāḥ śaṃsaṃti sarve 'sminnāsīnā iti nādhvare ‖ etamevāhuranye 'gniṃ narāśaṃsoddhvare hyayaṃ | naraiḥ praśasya āsīnairāhuścaivartvijo naraḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 3-2 to 3) **NOTE**

Some say Narāśaṃsa is Agni. Because men gather in the sacrifice and praise, others call him the sacrifice. According to still others, because he is praised by the men gathered at the sacrifice, Narāśaṃsa is Agni himself. The third is a reconciliation of the first and second opinions. Along with these two meanings Sāyaṇa has also given a third. When explaining Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-18-9 in the bhāṣya he says

नराशंसमेतन्नामकं देवविशेषं यद्वा अवयवार्थव्युत्पत्ता सदसस्पतिदेवतापरोऽयं शब्दः । अत्र अग्निवत् सदसस्पतेरपि नरैः शस्यमानत्वात् नराशंसस्त्वं । एतमेवाभिप्रायं हृदि निधाय ब्राह्मणमेवमाम्मायते—प्रजा वै नरो वाक् शंसः । (*narāśaṃsametannāmakaṃ devaviśeṣaṃ yadvā avayavārthavyutpattā sadasaspatidevatāparo 'yaṃ śabdaḥ | atra agnivat sadasaspaterapi naraiḥ śasyamānatvāt narāśaṃsastvaṃ | etamevābhiprāyaṃ hṛdi nidhāya brāhmaṇamevamāmmāyate—prajā vai naro vāk śaṃsaḥ |*) (Ai. Brā. 6-27).

Narāśaṃsa may mean a particular deity of this name, or Sadaspati. This meaning may hold both because Sadaspati too is praised by men and because the passage cited from the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa suggests this idea; thus the third meaning has been shown. But it would not be wrong to say that throughout the Ṛksaṃhitā the word is a synonym of Agni.

In Ṛks such as

नराशंसः प्रति धामान्यंजन् तिस्रो दिवः प्रति मह्ना स्वर्चिः । घृतपुषा मनसा हव्यमुंदन्नूर्धन्यज्ञस्य समनक्तु देवान् ॥ (*narāśaṃsaḥ prati dhāmānyaṃjan tisro divaḥ prati mahnā svarciḥ | ghṛtapuṣā manasā havyamuṃdannūrdhanyajñasya samanaktu devān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-3-2)

and in the present Ṛk, "Agni" is the meaning. In all ten Āprī hymns Narāśaṃsa is addressed six times, and the deity named Tanūnapāt six times. In two hymns of the first Maṇḍala both are addressed together. How the divergence of view about these two deities grew, and how the scholars of the śāstras have discussed this matter, deserves examination.

If one looks chiefly at the rule of the prayāja principle, invoking these two deities is not strictly necessary. For in every Ṛk different deities must be addressed, and

### Printed p. 239

these two deities are portions of Agni; since Agni was invoked in the first Ṛk, they are as good as addressed too. The scholar Haug (the translator of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa) has called them two deities distinct from Agni, but that opinion is not sound. Another point worth noting is this: the word Tanūnapāt stands in the vocative case throughout the Āprī hymns, while the word Narāśaṃsa stands in various cases; and the deity Tanūnapāt has only the epithets kavi and sujihva, whereas Narāśaṃsa has epithets such as priya, śuci, deva, devatama. Because of this, according to some scholars of the side, Narāśaṃsa, being known to the poets and, as a form of the earthly Agni, present before them, is the one near at hand; while Tanūnapāt, being less near, not visible to direct perception, is one who merely, heeding the prayer of the sacrificers, carries the oblations to the gods. In these opinions fancy is mixed with truth.

With the help of madhu **NOTE**, to fill the sacrifice itself with sweetness is the chief duty of these two deities. By epithets such as प्रतिधामान्यंजन् (*pratidhāmānyaṃjan*) (2-3-2), Narāśaṃsa **NOTE** is described as one who lights up the sacrificial ground; and as described in Ṛks such as

तनूनसात्पथ ऋतस्य यानान्मध्वा समंजन्त्वधया सुजिह्व । मन्मानि धीभिरुत यज्ञमृंधन् देवत्रा च कृणुह्यध्वरं नः । (*tanūnasātpatha ṛtasya yānānmadhvā samaṃjantvadhayā sujihva | manmāni dhībhiruta yajñamṛṃdhan devatrā ca kṛṇuhyadhvaraṃ naḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-2),

Tanūnapāt is one who makes the path of the Ṛta sweet; thus the nature of each has been stated. These special natures are described thus in the Praiṣa hymn:

होता यक्षत्तनूनपातमदितेर्गर्भं भुवनस्य गोपां । मध्वाद्य देवॊ देवेभ्यो देवयानान्पथो अनक्तु वेत्वाज्यस्य होतरज । (*hotā yakṣattanūnapātamaditergarbhaṃ bhuvanasya gopāṃ | madhvādya devò devebhyo devayānānpatho anaktu vetvājyasya hotaraja |*) (Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-2)

होता यक्षन्नराशग्ंसं नृशस्त्रं नृग् प्रणेत्रं । गोभिर्वपावान्तास्त्यद्वीरैः शक्तीवान्रथैः प्रथमयावा हिरण्यैश्चंद्री वेत्वाज्यस्य होतरज । (*hotā yakṣannarāśagṃsaṃ nṛśastraṃ nṛg praṇetraṃ | gobhirvapāvāntāstyadvīraiḥ śaktīvānrathaiḥ prathamayāvā hiraṇyaiścaṃdrī vetvājyasya hotaraja |*) (Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-2)

In these passages Tanūnapāt is described as the son of Aditi and one who lights the path of the gods; Narāśaṃsa as प्रथमयावा (*prathamayāvā*) (अग्रयावा (*agrayāvā*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-70.2), that is, as one who, as the representative of the gods, comes first toward men, and for that very reason as the leader of men. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa,

### Printed p. 240

तनूनपातं यजति प्राणो वै तनूनपात्स हि तन्नः पाति प्राणमेव तत्प्रीणाति प्राणं यजमाने दधाति । (*tanūnapātaṃ yajati prāṇo vai tanūnapātsa hi tannaḥ pāti prāṇameva tatprīṇāti prāṇaṃ yajamāne dadhāti |*) (Ai. Brā. 2-4)

by this passage the identity of Tanūnapāt with the vital breath (prāṇa) is stated; and

नराशंसं यजति प्रजा वै नरो वाक्कंसः प्रजां चैव तद्वाचं च प्रीणाति प्रजां च वाचं च यजमाने दधाति । (*narāśaṃsaṃ yajati prajā vai naro vākkaṃsaḥ prajāṃ caiva tadvācaṃ ca prīṇāti prajāṃ ca vācaṃ ca yajamāne dadhāti |*) (Ai. Brā. 2-4)

by this passage the identity of Narāśaṃsa with offspring (prajā) is stated. Examining such a variety of opinions, the scholar Haug has said:

"It clearly follows from this distinction between the invocation of these two deities—both rcpresenting a particular kind of Agni that certain gotras regarded Tanunapet as their tutelary deity or rather as one of their deified ancestors."

##### Grammar

- पावकः — पावयति इति पावकः । कर्तरि ङ्बुल् प्रत्ययः । अचोञ्छति इति धातोः इकः वृद्धिः । युवोरनाकौ (पा. सू. ७-१-१) इति प्रत्ययस्य अनादेशः । उंभादिषु अंतर्भूतत्वात् अंतोदात्तो भवति ।
- मध्वा — मधु शब्द आद्युदात्तः । तस्मात् तृतीयाएकवचने परे आगमानुशासनमनित्यम् इति वचनात् सुमागमो न भवति । जसादिषु छंदसि वा वचनम् इति वचनात् नाभावोऽपि न भवति । तदा यणादेशः ।
- मिमिक्षति — मिह सेचने इति धातुः । धातोः कर्मणः समान—(पा. सू. ३-१-७) इति सन् । सन्यजोः (पा सू. ६-१-११) इति धातोः द्वित्वम् । अभ्यासे आदिहल् शेषः । होढः इति धातुहकारस्य ढत्वम् । षडोः कः सि (पा. सू. ८-२-४१) इति ढकारस्य कत्वम् । ककारं निमित्तीकृत्य प्रत्ययसकारस्य षत्वम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति । **NOTE**
- नराशंसम् — नर शब्दः नृ नये इति धातोः अप् प्रत्ययेन सिद्धः । शंसंति अस्मिन् इति शंसः । हलश्च (पा. सू. ३-३-१२१) इति घञ् प्रत्ययः । नराणां शंसः नराशंसः तम् । उभे वनस्पत्यादिषु युगपत् (पा. सू. ६-२-१४०) इति पूर्वोत्तरपदयोः प्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- त्रिः — द्वित्रिचतुर्भ्यः सुच् (पा. सू. ५-४-१८) इति क्रियाभ्यावृत्तौ गम्यमानायां सुच् प्रत्ययः । सकारस्य रुत्वं विसर्गः ।

### Printed p. 241

##### Grammar

- दिवः — ऊडिदंपदादि—(पा. सू. ६-१-१७१) इति विभक्ते उदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- यज्ञियः — यज्ञाय अर्पः यज्ञियः । यज्ञर्त्विग्भ्यां घखञ् (पा.सू. ७-१-७१) इति घ प्रत्ययः । आयनेयानी—(पा. सू. ६-१-२) इति तस्य इयादेशः । यस्येति च इति अकारलोपः । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरेण इकार उदात्तो भवति ।

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ई॒ळि॒तो अ॑ग्न॒ आ न॒हेंद्रं॑ चि॒त्रमि॒प प्रि॒यं ।  
> इ॒यं हि त्वा॑ म॒तिर्ममाच्छा॑ सुजिद्व व॒च्यते॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ईळितः । अग्ने । आ । वह । इंद्रं । चित्रं । इह । प्रियं ।  
> इयं । हि । त्वा । मतिः । मम । अच्छ । सुजिह्व । वच्यते ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे आग्ने ईळितः सर्वैः स्तुत्य इण्नामकस्त्वमिहास्मिन्यज्ञे चित्रं चायनीयं
> प्रियं प्रीणयितारमस्मान् हविः स्वीकर्तुं ना प्रीतियुक्तमिंद्रमा वह । आह्वय ।
> हे सुजिह्व धृतपानेन शोभनज्वाल इयं मम मतिर्मननसाधना स्तुतिरूपा वाक्
> त्वा त्वामच्छाभिमुख्येन प्राप्तं वच्यते । मतिरेवास्मदीयोच्यते ॥ हिः पूरणार्थः ।
> वचेर्यकि संप्रसारणाच्चेति पूर्वरूपस्य ना छंदसीति विकल्पितत्वादभावे यण् ॥ अत्र
> पुरोडाशादीदभिमानियज्ञ परतॆया वा व्याख्येयं । एवमुत्तरत्र यज्ञांगैर्यज्ञसंबंधिदेवताभिश्च यज्ञ एवोच्यत इति तत्तरत्वेन व्याख्येयं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Agni, you who are praised (īḷita) by all and known by the name Iḷa, in this sacrifice bring (ā vaha, 'call') Indra, who is wonderful (citra, 'to be gathered, desirable'), dear (priya, one who gladdens us), and well-disposed, to accept the oblation. O Sujihva, you of fine flames through the drinking of ghee, this my mati, my speech in the form of praise, the instrument of reflection, is addressed (vacyate) toward you, facing you." It is only our own mati that is spoken. The particle हिः (*hiḥ*) is a filler. Sāyaṇa adds a note on the vowel-substitution (yaṇ) in वच्यते (*vacyate*). He then says that here the sacrifice is to be explained as referring to the deities of the sacrifice, such as the puroḍāśa cake; likewise in what follows, the sacrifice alone is meant by its parts and by the deities connected with it, and the verse is to be explained accordingly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- ईळितः (*īḷitaḥ*)—you who, praised by all, are known as Iḷa
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—the wonderful one
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—the beloved one
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- आ वह (*ā vaha*)—bring (him) along
- सुजिह्व (*sujihva*)—O Agni, you of excellent flame (through drinking ghee)
- इयं हि मम मतिः (*iyaṃ hi mama matiḥ*)—this speech of mine, in the form of praise, accompanied by meditation
- त्वा अच्छ (*tvā accha*)—toward you
- वच्यते (*vacyate*)—is recited

### Printed p. 242

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, you who, praised by all, are famed as Iḷa, bring to this our sacrifice Indra, the wonderful and beloved. O Agni, whose flames blaze up through the drinking of ghee, this my speech, in the form of praise and accompanied by meditation, is recited facing you.

##### Printed English

Agni, who is Ilita, bring hither Indra, the wonderful, the beloved; this my praise is recited, bright-tongued, before you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ईळितः सर्वैः स्तुत्यः इळ् नामकः त्वं (*īḷitaḥ sarvaiḥ stutyaḥ iḷ nāmakaḥ tvaṃ*) — it is explained as Agni, who is praised by all and famed by the name Iḷa. ईळॆ ईट्टेः स्तुतिकर्मणः ; इंथते र्वा (*īḷè īṭṭeḥ stutikarmaṇaḥ ; iṃthate rvā*): it may be formed either from the root īḍ, meaning praise, or from the root indh, meaning to blaze. (Ni. 8-8). Having shown this derivation of the word, the Nirukta-writers quote Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-3 and explain the right meaning of the word.**NOTE**

First, like the word samidh, this word too seems to have no separate indication as a deity, but to be used only as an epithet of Agni. Since Agni's help and support must be complete at the time of performing the sacrifice, he is by nature worthy of praise. At the beginning Agni must be (1) kindled. Afterwards, (2) under the name Tanūnapāt or Narāśaṃsa, he must make the oblations of the sacrifice full of honey. Then he must bring the gods to the sacrifice. For this work he must indeed be worthy of praise (īḍya). Therefore in this setting he is (3) īḷita, "prayed to", by the sacrificers.

In the Āprī hymns this word, derived from the root īḍ, is used of Agni in nine places in all: in eight as an epithet meaning "one who is praised" or "worthy of praise", and in one as an epithet showing Agni's function as messenger. And in the Āprī verse composed by Viśvāmitra,
प्र दीधितिर्विश्ववारा जिगाति होतारमिळः प्रथमं यजध्यै । (*pra dīdhitirviśvavārā jigāti hotāramiḷaḥ prathamaṃ yajadhyai |*)
अच्छा नमोभिर्वृषभं वंदध्यै स देवान्यक्षदिषितो यजीयान् । (*acchā namobhirvṛṣabhaṃ vaṃdadhyai sa devānyakṣadiṣito yajīyān |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-4-2)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-4-3)
only here does it mean food in the form of oblation. Even so, it is related to Agni, the chief deity of that verse. This meaning differs from the tradition of the Āprī hymn. But in the Praiṣa hymn it is only a synonym of Agni.

### Printed p. 243

होतायक्षदग्निमिड ईडितो देवो देवाग्ं आवक्षद्दूतो हव्यनाडमूरः । उपेमं यज्ञमुपेमां देवो देवहूतिमवतु नेत्वाज्यस्य होतरज । (*hotāyakṣadagnimiḍa īḍito devo devāgṃ āvakṣaddūto havyanāḍamūraḥ | upemaṃ yajñamupemāṃ devo devahūtimavatu netvājyasya hotaraja |*)
(Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-1)
On this passage the commentator says इडेत्येतन्नामकमग्निं तृतीयप्रयाजदेवं (*iḍetyetannāmakamagniṃ tṛtīyaprayājadevaṃ*), thus giving it as a synonym of Agni. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa:
इळो यजत्यन्नं वा इळोऽन्नमेव तत्प्रीणात्यन्नं यजमाने दधाति । (*iḷo yajatyannaṃ vā iḷo 'nnameva tatprīṇātyannaṃ yajamāne dadhāti |*)
(Ai. Brā. 2-4)
It says that iḷa is the food in the form of oblation to be offered in the sacrifice, and that as a result of offering this food the sacrificer too obtains food.

As explained above, the connection that Yāska shows between this word and the root indh is not clearly understood. Here Yāska does not state Kātkaśya's view. In the Bṛhaddevatā:
इळस्त्वषिकृतं रूपं ईडेश्व स्तुतिकर्मणः । इळावांस्तेन वोक्तोग्निरिडिना वृद्धिकर्मणा ॥ (*iḷastvaṣikṛtaṃ rūpaṃ īḍeśva stutikarmaṇaḥ | iḷāvāṃstena voktogniriḍinā vṛddhikarmaṇā ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-4)
By these sentences it is shown that this word is of the seers (ārṣeya) and a synonym of Agni, and that the word ilāvān is an epithet of Agni.**NOTE**
गोमा अग्नेऽविमा आश्वी यज्ञो नृवत्सखा सदमिदप्रमृष्यः । इळाना एषो असुर प्रजावान्दीर्घो रयिः पृथुबुध्नः सभावान् । (*gomā agne 'vimā āśvī yajño nṛvatsakhā sadamidapramṛṣyaḥ | iḷānā eṣo asura prajāvāndīrgho rayiḥ pṛthubudhnaḥ sabhāvān |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-2-5)
In this verse the word इळार्वा (*iḷārvā*) is an epithet of Agni, so the Bṛhaddevatā points out that item.**NOTE**

Although such differences of tradition appear in the explanations, it can be said on the whole that it is a synonym of the deity Agni, holding the fourth place in the Āprī hymn.

मतिः—मनसाधना स्तुतिरूपा नाक् (*matiḥ—manasādhanā stutirūpā nāk*) — it means speech that is meditative and in the form of praise.

##### Grammar

- ईळितः — ईड स्तुत् धातुः । कर्मणि निष्ठा इति क्त प्रत्ययः । अर्धधातुकस्लेड्वलाडेः इति इडागमः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति ।

### Printed p. 244

##### Grammar

- वह — वह प्रापणे धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुषैकवचने वह इति रूपं भवति । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- चित्रं — चिञ् चयने धातुः । अमिचिमिदिशसिभ्यः क्रः (उ. सू. ४-६०३) इति क्र प्रत्ययः । कित्त्वात् गुणनिषेधो भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति ।
- मतिः — मनु अवबोधने धातुः । स्त्रियां क्तिन् (पा. सू. ३-३-९४) इति क्तिन् प्रत्ययः । मंत्रेवृषेषपचमन-(पा.सू. ३-३-९६) इति क्तिन् उदात्तो भवति ।
- अच्छा — निपातस्य च (पा. सू. ६-३-१३९) इति संहितायां दीर्घो भवति ।
- सुजिह्व — शोभनाः जिह्वाः यस्य सः सुजिह्वः । गोस्त्रियोरुपसर्जनस्य इति समासे ह्रस्वो भवति । संबुद्धौ अमंत्रितस्य च (पा. सू. ८-१-१९) इति निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- हि — पादपूरणे चवैतुहि इति अत्र हि इति पादपूरणे आगतः ।
- वच्यते — वच परिभाषणे धातुः । कर्मणि लट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचने त प्रत्ययः । टेः एत्वं । सार्वधातुके यक् (पा. सू. ३-१-६७) इति यक् । संप्रसारणे प्राप्ते वा छंदसि इति संप्रसारणं न भवति । हि इत्यस्य संबंधत्वात् हिच (पा. सू. ८-१-३४) इति निघातस्वरप्रतिषेधो भवति । अदुपदेशात् परस्मिन् लसार्वधातुकस्य आगतत्वात् तत् अनुदात्तं भवति तदा यक्स्वरः अवशिष्यते ।**NOTE**

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्त्र॒णा॒नासो॑ य॒तस्रु॑चो ब॒र्हिर्य॒ज्ञे स्व॑ध्व॒रे ।  
> वृं॒जे दे॒वव्य॑चस्तम॒मिंद्रा॑य॒ शर्म॑ स॒प्रथः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्त्रणानासः । यतस्रुचः । बर्हिः । यज्ञे । सुअध्वरे ।  
> वृंजे । देवव्यचःतमं । इंद्राय । शर्म । सप्रथः ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 245

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स्वध्वरे शोभनप्रायणीयाद्यनांतरयागवति यज्ञे सोमयागे बर्हिर्दर्भान् स्तृणानास आस्तरंतो यतस्रुचो नियमितजुह्वादिपात्रा ऋत्विजो देवव्यचस्तममतिशयेन देवगामिनं सप्रथः सर्वतो विस्तीर्णं शर्म सुखसाधनं गृहमिंद्राय वृंजे । विवर्जयंति । संपादयंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the well-conducted (su-adhvara) sacrifice, the Soma sacrifice, which has fine preparatory rites such as the prāyaṇīya and the rites that follow, the priests (ṛtvij) strew the barhis, the darbha grass, and hold ladles and other vessels in check (yatasruc). For Indra they वृंजे (*vṛṃje*), that is, set apart, meaning prepare, a dwelling (śarma), a means of comfort, that is most god-frequented (devavyacastama, most reaching to the gods) and spread out on every side (sapratha).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्वध्वरे (*svadhvare*)—in this (sacrifice) accompanied by excellent, introductory rites such as the prāyaṇīya
- यज्ञी (*yajñī*)—in this Soma sacrifice**NOTE**
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—the seats of darbha
- स्तृणानासः (*stṛṇānāsaḥ*)—strewing
- यतस्रुचः (*yatasrucaḥ*)—the priests, who hold the ladles and other vessels for the sacrifice
- देवव्यचस्तमं (*devavyacastamaṃ*)—that which, calling the gods, worships them exceedingly
- सप्रथः (*saprathaḥ*)—that which is extended
- शर्म (*śarma*)—that which is a means of comfort, the sacrificial house
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- वृंजे (*vṛṃje*)—they prepare

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In this Soma sacrifice, accompanied by excellent introductory rites such as the prāyaṇīya, the priests have strewn the darbha seats and hold the ladles and other vessels for the sacrifice. And for Indra they prepare the sacrificial house, which calls the gods and again and again worships them exceedingly, which is extended, and which is a means of comfort.

##### Printed English

The priests, bearing ladles, are strewing the sacred grass in this holy sacrifice, to prepare a god-frequented and well-spread abode for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—This implement has a more concrete form than the notions of idhma and Iḷa. Barhis means simply a darbha seat. The doors are given a divine aspect (देवीः द्वारः (*devīḥ dvāraḥ*)), but nothing of the kind is done for barhis. Only the sense of a seat for the gods is suggested. Moreover, the doors are prayed to open of themselves, without other help, and make a way for the gods to come. Lifeless doors cannot open of themselves and make a wide path unless they have divine power; but for barhis there is no attribution of this power. The priests must spread it. Not only in this verse but in the other verses of the Āprī hymns too, it is stated clearly that the barhis is to be strewn only with the help of the priests and others. For example—

### Printed p. 246

प्राचीनं बर्हिरोजसा सहस्रवीरमस्तृणन् । यत्रादित्या विराजथ । (*prācīnaṃ barhirojasā sahasravīramastṛṇan | yatrādityā virājatha |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-188-4)
In this verse likewise, and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-5-4, बर्हिः प्राचीनमोजसा पवमानः स्तृणन् हरिः । (*barhiḥ prācīnamojasā pavamānaḥ stṛṇan hariḥ |*), there is the description that the priests strew the barhis. In the same way, in the verses of the other Āprī hymns too, the barhis is said to be an earthly and lifeless thing, yet a place that is a sacred seat for the gods. Only in the Praiṣa hymn:
होता यक्षद्बर्हिः सुष्टरीनोर्णम्रदा अस्मिन्यज्ञे वि च प्र च प्रथताग्ं स्वासस्थं देवेभ्यः । एमेनदद्य वसवो रुद्रा आदित्याः सदंतु प्रियमिंद्रस्यास्तु वेत्वाज्यस्य होतरज ॥ (*hotā yakṣadbarhiḥ suṣṭarīnorṇamradā asminyajñe vi ca pra ca prathatāgṃ svāsasthaṃ devebhyaḥ | emenadadya vasavo rudrā ādityāḥ sadaṃtu priyamiṃdrasyāstu vetvājyasya hotaraja ‖*)
(Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-1)
with बर्हिर्नामकं चतुर्थप्रयाजदेवमग्निं (*barhirnāmakaṃ caturthaprayājadevamagniṃ*), it is said to be a seat that is a synonym of Agni and of divine nature. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa,
बर्हिर्यजति, पशवो वै बर्हिः पशूनेव तत्प्रीणाति पशून्यजमाने दधाति । (*barhiryajati, paśavo vai barhiḥ paśūneva tatprīṇāti paśūnyajamāne dadhāti |*)
(Ai. Brā. 2-4)
it is explained as the animal that grazes on grass and is covered with grass. But it is seen that this meaning has not the slightest scope.**NOTE**

The Nirukta-writers say बर्हिः परिबर्हणात् (*barhiḥ paribarhaṇāt*): because it grows quickly, darbha is named barhis. (Ni. 8-8). In the Bṛhaddevatā,
बर्हिरेवायमग्निस्तु सर्वं हि परिब्बंहितं । अन्नेन यद्भुतो ना सन्निध्मेन परिबृंहितः ॥ (*barhirevāyamagnistu sarvaṃ hi paribbaṃhitaṃ | annena yadbhuto nā sannidhmena paribṛṃhitaḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-5)
it states identity: since all food is pervaded by him, and since after the oblation he is filled with fuel, Agni himself is in the form of barhis.

Although in many matters the authors of the Bṛhaddevatā follow the sentences of the Nirukta, in this context they show a slight difference: what the Nirukta-writers call barhis, simply a seat for the gods, they explain as a seat of divine nature and a synonym of Agni.

स्तृणानासः (*stṛṇānāsaḥ*) (printed स्प्राणासरि (*sprāṇāsari*))—आस्तरंत (*āstaraṃta*): those who strew.**NOTE**
सप्रथः (*saprathaḥ*)—सप्रथाः सर्वतः पृथुः (*saprathāḥ sarvataḥ pṛthuḥ*): it means spread out broadly all around, as the Nirukta-writers explain (Ni. 6-8).

### Printed p. 247

##### Grammar

- स्त्रणानासः — स्त्र-ञ् अच्छादने इति धातुः । क्र्यादिः । लडर्थे शानच् प्रत्ययः । क्र्यादिभ्यः श्ना (पा. सू ३-१-८) इति श्ना विकरणम् । प्वादीनां ह्रस्वः (पा. सू. ७-३-८०) इति धातोर्ह्रस्वः । ह्रस्वऋकारस्य परत्वात् णत्वम् । प्रत्ययस्य चित्त्वादंतोदात्तो भवति । जस्परे आज्जसेरसुक् (पा. सू. ६-१-५०) इति तस्य असुक् आगमः । सकारस्य रुत्वविसर्गौ । **NOTE**
- यतस्रुचः — यताः स्रुचः यैस्ते यतस्रुचः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- स्वध्वरे — शोभनः अध्वरः यस्मिन् सः स्वध्वरः । नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) इति उत्तरपदस्य अंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति । सप्तमीएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- वृज्जि — वृजी वर्जने इति धातुः । अदादिः । इदित्त्वान्नुमागमः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचने व्यत्ययेन बहुवचनस्थाने एकवचनं त प्रत्ययः । टेः एत्वम् । लोपस्त आत्मनेपदेषु (पा. सू. ७-१-४१) इति प्रत्ययतकारस्य लोपः । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति ।
- सप्रथः — प्रथ प्रख्याने इति धातुः । सर्वधातुभ्य असुन् इति असुन् प्रत्ययः । प्रथसा सह वर्तत इति सप्रथः । तेन सहेति तुल्ययोगे (पा. सू. २-२-२८) इति समासः । वोपसर्जनस्य (पा. सू. ६-३-८२) इति सह इत्यस्य सभावः । गतिकारकोपपदात्‌कृत् (पा. सू. ६-२-१३९) इति कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 248

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि श्र॑यंतामृता॒वृधः॑ प्र॒यै दे॒वेभ्यो॑ म॒हीः ।  
> पा॒व॒कासः॑ पुरु॒स्पृहो॒ द्वारो॑ दे॒वीर॑स॒श्चतः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । श्रयंतां । ऋतवृधः । प्रय्यै । देवेभ्यः । महीः ।  
> पावकासः । पुरुस्पृहः । द्वारः । देवीः । असश्चतः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्वारो देवीर्यज्ञस्य द्वाराभिमानिन्नो देवता वि श्रयंतां । विविधं
> श्रयंतु । विवृता भवंतु । किमर्थ० । देवेभ्यः प्रयै प्रयातुं देवानां प्रापणाय ॥ प्रयै रोहिष्यॆ अव्यथिष्यॆ । पा. ३-४-१० । इति निपातितः ॥ कीदृश्यस्ताः । ऋतवृधो यज्ञस्य वर्धयित्र्यः पूज्याः महीर्महत्यः पावकास शोधयित्र्यः पुरुस्पृहो बहुस्वहणीयाः असश्च तोर्ऽसज्यमानाः परस्परविप्रकृष्टा इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The doors (द्वारो देवीः (*dvāro devīḥ*)), the deities who preside over the doors of the sacrifice, are to open out in various ways (वि श्रयंतां (*vi śrayaṃtāṃ*)), that is, to stand open. For what purpose? प्रयै (*prayai*) means "in order to go", that is, to bring the gods to the sacrifice; it is a form irregularly accepted. What are they like? They increase the sacrifice (ऋतवृधः (*ṛtavṛdhaḥ*)), they are worshipful, great (महीः (*mahīḥ*)), purifying (पावकासः (*pāvakāsaḥ*)), desired by many (पुरुस्पृहः (*puruspṛhaḥ*)), and असश्चतः (*asaścataḥ*), that is, not clinging to one another, standing far apart from each other.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतवृधः (*ṛtavṛdhaḥ*)—those who make the sacrifice grow
- मही (*mahī*)—those of great form
- पावकासः (*pāvakāsaḥ*)—those having the power of purifying
- पुरुस्पृहः (*puruspṛhaḥ*)—those desired by many
- असश्चतः (*asaścataḥ*)—those who stand apart, not clinging
- द्वारोदेवी: (*dvārodevī:*)—the deities presiding over the doors (of the sacrificial house)
- देवेभ्यः (*devebhyaḥ*)—for the gods
- प्रयै (*prayai*)—for entering
- विश्रयंतां (*viśrayaṃtāṃ*)—let them move apart, far away, and make a way

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the door-deities of the sacrificial house, who are the cause of the sacrifice's growth, of great form, having the power of purifying, desired by many, and standing apart without clinging to one another, move apart and far away and make a way for the gods to enter.

##### Printed English

Let the bright, separable doors, the augmenters of sacrificial, the purifiers of rites, the desired of many, be set open for the gods to enter.

### Printed p. 249

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

द्वारो देवीः (*dvāro devīḥ*)—Sāyaṇa explains this as the deities presiding over the doors of the sacrificial house. There is a difference of opinion whether these doors are really the doors of the sacrificial house or whether this is merely an ornamental usage. If the word does not mean house-doors here, then in the matter of the deities of the Āprī hymns too, points different from those just stated would have to be assumed. From the standpoint of ritual procedure, only two things, the barhis and the svāhākṛti, are without attribution of divinity; though merely earthly, they are described with qualities of supernatural purity because they are connected with the sacrifice. In तिस्रो देवीः (*tisro devīḥ*) all are called deities together, because Sarasvatī has a pure divine element and the other two rivers are mentioned along with that river. In the same way in दैवौ होतारौ (*daivau hotārau*) the earthly hotṛ is also meant as a deity, because he is with Agni, who is of divine nature. But in देवी द्वारः (*devī dvāraḥ*) there is no room for this. If these doors are the doors of the sacrificial house, the question arises why they should have a special place that the barhis and svāhākṛti do not have. If only some of the implements that are parts of the sacrifice are to be viewed as divine, then the most important of all, the altar (vedi), is not named at all.

Another point to be noticed here is that the word is in the plural, "doors". In the verse of an Āprī hymn,
सप्त होत्राणि मनसा वृणाना इन्वंतो विश्वं प्रति यन्नृतेन । नृपेशसो विदथेषु प्र जाता अभीमं यज्ञं वि चरंत पूर्विः ॥ (*sapta hotrāṇi manasā vṛṇānā invaṃto viśvaṃ prati yannṛtena | nṛpeśaso vidatheṣu pra jātā abhīmaṃ yajñaṃ vi caraṃta pūrviḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-4-5)
the adjective पूर्विः (*pūrviḥ*) is also in the plural. And if we examine the duties of these door-deities:
1) "Open out, ye sacrifice-increasing divine doors, unclinging" (वि श्रयंतां ऋतावृधो द्वारो देवीरसश्वतः (*vi śrayaṃtāṃ ṛtāvṛdho dvāro devīrasaśvataḥ*)), meaning "take them apart for the convenience of the sacrificer", in the verse Ṛ. 1-13-6;
2) दुरो धृतान्यक्षरन् (*duro dhṛtānyakṣaran*), "they poured out waters", in 1-188-5;
3) प्र प्र यज्ञं प्रणीत न (*pra pra yajñaṃ praṇīta na*), "they fill the sacrifice with fruit", in 5-5-5;
4) इन्वंतो विश्वं प्रति यन्नृतेन (*invaṃto viśvaṃ prati yannṛtena*), "they quicken the whole world with water", in 3-4-5.

### Printed p. 250

Thus their duties are indicated in many other ways. Some hold that many of these actions are attributed to them merely ornamentally and figuratively, and that there is no truth in them. Moreover,
समिद्दो अस्य मनुषो दुरोणे देवो देवान् यजसि जातवेदः । आ च वह मित्रमहश्चि कित्वान्त्वं दूतः कविरसि प्रचेताः ॥ (*samiddo asya manuṣo duroṇe devo devān yajasi jātavedaḥ | ā ca vaha mitramahaści kitvāntvaṃ dūtaḥ kavirasi pracetāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-1)
In this verse, since it says मनुषो दुरोणे (*manuṣo duroṇe*), "in the house of man", this figurative language applied to merely worldly house-doors may even look like an excessive flight of fancy. Some critics also hold that adjectives such as नृपेशसः (*nṛpeśasaḥ*), ऋतावृधः (*ṛtāvṛdhaḥ*), पावका (*pāvakā*) are not fitting. Here, whether to support or to refute these opinions, it is good to learn the views of the Vedic scholars who are the foundation for keeping up the Vedic tradition.

In the verse of the Āprī hymn
स्वाध्यो३ वि दुरो देवयंतोऽशिश्रयू रथयुर्देवताता । पूर्वि शिशुं न मातरा रिहाणे समग्रुवो न समनेष्वंजन् । (*svādhyo3 vi duro devayaṃto 'śiśrayū rathayurdevatātā | pūrvi śiśuṃ na mātarā rihāṇe samagruvo na samaneṣvaṃjan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-2-5)
they state clearly, यज्ञगृहद्वाराणि इत्यर्थः (*yajñagṛhadvārāṇi ityarthaḥ*), that it means the doors of the sacrificial house. Likewise some other scholars explain that for the verb व्यशिश्रयुः (*vyaśiśrayuḥ*) the subject is यजमानाः (*yajamānāḥ*), and that the construction is that they stood taking shelter at the door of the sacrificial house. The English scholars Oldenberg, Grassman and Ludwig also approve this explanation, and in the verse
विराट्संम्राड्विभ्रीः प्रभ्वीर्बह्वीश्च भूयसीश्च याः । दुरो धृतान्यक्षरन् । (*virāṭsaṃmrāḍvibhrīḥ prabhvīrbahvīśca bhūyasīśca yāḥ | duro dhṛtānyakṣaran |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-188-5)
they say the words विराट् (*virāṭ*) and संम्राट् (*saṃmrāṭ*) are the names of two door-leaves. The scholar Max Muller says these are the gates of heaven by which the gods descend from heaven (History of Ancient Sanskrit Literature, p. 245). In the Praiṣa hymn,
होतायक्षद्दुर ऋष्वाः कवष्टोऽकोषधावनीरुदाताभिर्जिहतां वि पक्षोभिः श्रयंतां । सुप्रायणा अस्मिन्यज्ञे विश्रयंतामृतावृधो वियंत्वाज्यस्य होतर्यज । (*hotāyakṣaddura ṛṣvāḥ kavaṣṭo 'koṣadhāvanīrudātābhirjihatāṃ vi pakṣobhiḥ śrayaṃtāṃ | suprāyaṇā asminyajñe viśrayaṃtāmṛtāvṛdho viyaṃtvājyasya hotaryaja |*) (Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-2)

### Printed p. 251

— what is said in these sentences the commentator explains as दुरो द्वारसंज्ञकाः पंचमप्रयाजदेवतास्तद्रूपमग्निं दैवो होता यजतु (*duro dvārasaṃjñakāḥ paṃcamaprayājadevatāstadrūpamagniṃ daivo hotā yajatu*). In these sentences the words आताभिः (*ātābhiḥ*) and उज्जिहतां (*ujjihatāṃ*) are important; this to some extent opposes the opinion above. From this one view is that it can be said that "door" meaning the doors of the sacrificial house is ornamental. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa,
दुरो यजति वृष्टिर्वै दुरो वृष्टिमेव तत्प्रीणाति वृष्टिमन्नाद्यं यजमाने दधाति । (*duro yajati vṛṣṭirvai duro vṛṣṭimeva tatprīṇāti vṛṣṭimannādyaṃ yajamāne dadhāti |*) (Ai. Brā. 2-4) **NOTE**
the meaning "rain" is given in place of "door". How this meaning arises is not made clear. The Nirukta author says द्वारो जवतेर्वा प्रवतेर्वा पारयतेर्वा (*dvāro javatervā pravatervā pārayatervā*): that it is formed either from the root जु (*ju*) meaning "press forward", or from the root द्रु (*dru*) meaning "advance", or from the root वृ (*vṛ*) meaning "separate". Quoting the verse
व्यचस्वतीरुर्विया वि श्रयंतां पतिभ्यो न जनयः शुंभमानाः । देवीर्द्वारो बृहतीर्विश्वमिन्वा देवेभ्यो भवत सुप्रायणाः । (*vyacasvatīrurviyā vi śrayaṃtāṃ patibhyo na janayaḥ śuṃbhamānāḥ | devīrdvāro bṛhatīrviśvaminvā devebhyo bhavata suprāyaṇāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-5)
and explaining the meaning of the word द्वार (*dvāra*), he states a difference of opinion: गृहद्वार इति कात्थक्यः । (*gṛhadvāra iti kātthakyaḥ |*) according to Kātthakya it is a house-door, and अग्निरिति शाकपूणिः । (*agniriti śākapūṇiḥ |*) according to Śākapūṇi it is Agni (Ni. 8-10). In the Bṛhaddevatā,
द्वारस्तु देव्यो याः प्रोक्ता विश्वेषां तास्तु पत्नयः । अग्नायीमनुवर्तंते तथाग्नाय्क्यग्निमेव च ॥ अग्नौ ध्रुवं स्थितास्तास्तु संस्तूयंतेऽग्निना सह । प्राधान्यं तासु चैवाग्ने स्तुतिष्टेव हविष्णु च ॥ (*dvārastu devyo yāḥ proktā viśveṣāṃ tāstu patnayaḥ | agnāyīmanuvartaṃte tathāgnāykyagnimeva ca ‖ agnau dhruvaṃ sthitāstāstu saṃstūyaṃte 'gninā saha | prādhānyaṃ tāsu caivāgne stutiṣṭeva haviṣṇu ca ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 3-6 to 7)
it says that these doors are wives of the gods; that they follow Agnāyī, who follows Agni; and that, being firmly established in Agni, they are praised together with Agni. This explanation is reasonable.

असश्चतः (*asaścataḥ*)—असज्यमानाः परस्परं विप्रकृष्टाः । (*asajyamānāḥ parasparaṃ viprakṛṣṭāḥ |*) It means those who stand apart, not clinging to one another.

### Printed p. 252

##### Grammar

- श्रयंताम् — श्रिञ् सेवायां धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वान्निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- ऋतावृधः — ऋतं वर्धयंति इति ऋतवृधः । वृधु वृद्धौ धातुः । प्रयोजकव्यापारदर्शनादंतर्भावितण्यर्थको धातुः । क्विप् च (पा. सू. ३-२-७६) इति क्विप् प्रत्ययः । उपपदसमासः । अन्येषामपि दृश्यते (पा. सू. ६-३-१३७) इति पूर्वपदस्य संहितायां दीर्घो भवति । गतिकारकोपपदात्‌कृत् इति कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- प्रयै — या प्रापणे इति धातुः । प्रयै रोहिष्यै अव्यथिष्यै (पा. सू. ३-४-१०) इति निपातितम् । कृन्मेजंतः इति अव्ययसंज्ञा भवति ।
- महीः — महतीः इत्यत्र छांदसत्वेन अतः लोपः । विभक्तौ परस्यां सुपां सुलुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-३९) इति पूर्वसवर्णदीर्घः ।
- पावकासः — जसि परे आज्जसेरसुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-५०) इति तस्य असुक् आगमः ।
- पुरुस्पृहः — स्पृह ईप्सायां धातुः । क्विप् प्रत्ययः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- असश्चतः — ग्लुञ्च षस्ज गतौ धातुः । जकारस्य व्यत्ययेन चकारः । अस्मात् लडर्थे शतृ प्रत्ययः । न विद्यंते सश्चंतः गच्छंतः येषु असश्चतः । नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा. ६-२-१७२) इति उत्तरपदांतोदात्तस्वरो भवति ।

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ भंद॑माने॒ उपा॑के॒ नक्तो॒षासा॑ सु॒पेश॑सा ।  
> य॒ह्वी ऋ॒तस्य॑ मा॒तरा॒ सीद॑तां ब॒र्हिरा सु॒मत् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । भंदमाने इति । उपाके इति । नक्तोषसा । सुपेशसा ।  
> यह्वी इति । ऋतस्य । मातरा । सीदतां । बर्हिः । आ । सुमत् ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 253

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भंदमाने सर्वैः स्तूयमाने उपाके वरस्परं सन्निहिते सुपेशसा शोभनरूपे । पेश इति रूपनाम । यह्वी महत्वा अपत्यरूपे वा प्रतिदिनं जायमानत्वात् । यहुरित्यपत्यनाम यहुः सूनुरिति तन्नामसु पाठात् । ऋतस्य मातरा
> यज्ञस्य निर्मात्र्यौ । ताभ्यामागमनाद्यज्ञस्य संपूर्तेयज्ञमातृत्वमुपचर्यते ।
> यद्वा । अतिरात्रादीनामहोरात्रसाध्यत्वात् । नक्तोषासा उक्तगुणविशिष्टे अहोरात्रदेवते सुमत् स्वयमेव । आस्माभिरप्रार्थिते इत्यर्थः । यद्वा । सुमद्बर्हिरास्कृतं दर्भमा समंतादा सीदतां । उपविशतां । यद्वा । ऋतस्यॆति कर्मणि
> षष्ठी । यज्ञ मा सीदतां । आसाद्य च बर्हिरास्कृतं दर्भं स्वयनेवा सीदतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

भंदमाने (*bhaṃdamāne*) means praised by all; उपाके (*upāke*) means near each other; सुपेशसा (*supeśasā*) means of beautiful form, पेश (*peśa*) being a name for form. यह्वी (*yahvī*) means either great, or of the form of offspring because they are born every day; he supports this from the list of names, where यहु (*yahu*) is a name for offspring ("*yahuḥ sūnuḥ*"). ऋतस्य मातरा (*ṛtasya mātarā*) means the two who produce the sacrifice; because the sacrifice is completed by their coming, the sacrifice is figuratively said to have them as mothers. Or else, because rites such as the atirātra are accomplished by day and night, नक्तोषासा (*naktoṣāsā*) are the deities of day and night, endowed with the stated qualities. सुमत् (*sumat*) means of their own accord, unasked by us. Or else *sumat barhiḥ* is the prepared darbha grass: let them sit down upon it all around, let them be seated. Or else ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*) is a genitive in the sense of the object: let them come to the sacrifice, and having come, let them of their own accord sit on the prepared darbha grass.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आ छंदमाने (*ā chaṃdamāne*)—those who are called upon by all **NOTE**
- उपाके (*upāke*)—those who are close to each other
- सुपेशसा (*supeśasā*)—those of charming form
- यह्वी (*yahvī*)—those of great form; or (because born daily) those of the form of offspring
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the sacrifice
- मातरा (*mātarā*)—the two who are the cause of making
- नक्तोषासा (*naktoṣāsā*)—the deities of night and day
- सुमत् (*sumat*)—(even if we do not pray) of your own accord
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—upon the darbha seat
- आ सीदतां (*ā sīdatāṃ*)—sit down facing it
- अथवा सुमत् (*athavā sumat*)—widely spread
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—upon the darbha seat
- आ सीदतां (*ā sīdatāṃ*)—sit down facing it

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O deities of day and night, praised by all, near each other, of charming form, of great form, and makers of the sacrifice: even though we have not prayed, of your own accord and with kindly regard, be pleased to sit upon our widely spread darbha seats.

##### Printed English

Beautiful night and morning, ever hymned, ever associated, progeny (of time), parents of sacrifice, sit down of your own good-will on the sacred grass.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

(आ) भंदमाने (*(ā) bhaṃdamāne*)—सर्वैः स्तूयमाने । (*sarvaiḥ stūyamāne |*) "Praised by all": an adjective of the deities called नक्तोषासा (*naktoṣāsā*). Since it is read among the names of the act of worship, अर्चति (*arcati*), गायति (*gāyati*) and so on (Ni. 3-19), भंदमाना (*bhaṃdamānā*) means the object of worship. भंदनाः । (*bhaṃdanāḥ |*)

### Printed p. 254

The Nirukta says that भंदना भंदतेः स्तुतिकर्मणः । (*bhaṃdanā bhaṃdateḥ stutikarmaṇaḥ |*) (Ni. 5-2): the word is formed from the root भंद् (*bhaṃd*) and means an act of praise. The Nirukta cites two Ṛks:

आ विवेश रोदसी भूरिर्वपसा पुरुप्रियो भंदते धामभिः कविः । (*ā viveśa rodasī bhūrirvapasā purupriyo bhaṃdate dhāmabhiḥ kaviḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-3-4)

स भंदना उदियर्ति प्रजावतीर्विश्वायुर्विश्वाः सुभरा अहर्दिवि । (*sa bhaṃdanā udiyarti prajāvatīrviśvāyurviśvāḥ subharā ahardivi |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-86-41.)

and thereby shows that the word is fitting in contexts where excellent praise is made. (Ni. 5-2)

सुमत् (*sumat*)—स्वयमेव । अस्माभिरप्रार्थिते इत्यर्थः। (*svayameva | asmābhiraprārthite ityarthaḥ|*) That is, "even if we do not pray, of your own accord"; it indicates the generosity and the self-respect of Uṣāsānaktā. सुमत्स्वयमित्यर्थः (*sumatsvayamityarthaḥ*) (Ni. 6-22): the Nirukta explains the word सुमत् (*sumat*) as स्वयं (*svayaṃ*), that is, "of itself". It is also understood that this is not the only meaning of the word; it is also a synonym of the words कल्याण (*kalyāṇa*), शोभन (*śobhana*) or भद्र (*bhadra*). Since many words such as सुमद्रथः (*sumadrathaḥ*), सुमद्गणः (*sumadgaṇaḥ*), सुमदंशुः (*sumadaṃśuḥ*) are in use, one looks at the meaning of those words. In the Ṛk

अमेव नः सुहवा आ हि गंतन नि बर्हिषि सदतना रणिष्टन । (*ameva naḥ suhavā ā hi gaṃtana ni barhiṣi sadatanā raṇiṣṭana |*)
अथा मंदस्व जुजुषाणो अंधसस्त्वष्टर्देवेभिर्जनिभिः सुमद्गणः । (*athā maṃdasva jujuṣāṇo aṃdhasastvaṣṭardevebhirjanibhiḥ sumadgaṇaḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-36-3)

the word सुमद्गणः (*sumadgaṇaḥ*) is explained as शोभनगणः (*śobhanagaṇaḥ*); and in

विभावा देवः सुरणः परि क्षितीरग्निर्बभूव शवसा सुमद्रथः । (*vibhāvā devaḥ suraṇaḥ pari kṣitīragnirbabhūva śavasā sumadrathaḥ |*)
तस्य व्रतानि भूरिपोषिणो वयमुप भूषेम दम आ सुवृक्तिभिः । (*tasya vratāni bhūripoṣiṇo vayamupa bhūṣema dama ā suvṛktibhiḥ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-3-9)

सुमद्रथः (*sumadrathaḥ*) is explained as शोभनरथः (*śobhanarathaḥ*). In all these places the word सुमत् (*sumat*) is a synonym of शोभन (*śobhana*) and कल्याण (*kalyāṇa*). Likewise here too, since the coming of the deities Uṣāsānaktā is excellent and auspicious, the fitness of this word can be understood.

उपाके (*upāke*)—this word is read among the words for "near" (Ni. 3-9); परस्परं सन्निहिते । (*parasparaṃ sannihite |*) It means those who are close to each other.

सुपेशसा (*supeśasā*)—पेश इति रूपनाम । पिंशतेर्विपिशितं भवति । (*peśa iti rūpanāma | piṃśatervipiśitaṃ bhavati |*) The Nirukta, citing this very Ṛk, shows that the word is formed from the root पिश् (*piś*), which gives the sense "refined, polished", and means "possessing beauty" (Ni. 8-11). The purport is: those whose form is exceedingly beautiful.

### Printed p. 255

यह्वी (*yahvī*)—महत्यौ अपत्यरूपे वा । (*mahatyau apatyarūpe vā |*) It means: those of a very great nature, or those of the form of children. In the Nirukta this word is read among the words for "great" (Ni. 3-13) and also among the words for offspring, तुक् (*tuk*), तोकं (*tokaṃ*) and so on (Ni. 3-1). Both kinds of nature can be seen in the description of Uṣas. For example:

उप व एषे वंद्येभिः शूष्यैः प्र यह्वी दिवश्चि तयद्भिरर्कैः । (*upa va eṣe vaṃdyebhiḥ śūṣyaiḥ pra yahvī divaści tayadbhirarkaiḥ |*)
उषासानक्ता विदुषीव विश्वमा हा वहतो मर्त्याय यज्ञं । (*uṣāsānaktā viduṣīva viśvamā hā vahato martyāya yajñaṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-41-7)

उत योषणे दिव्ये मही न उषासानक्ता सुदुघेव धेनुः । (*uta yoṣaṇe divye mahī na uṣāsānaktā sudugheva dhenuḥ |*)
बर्हिषदा पुरुहूते मघोनी आ यज्ञये सुविताय श्रयेतां । (*barhiṣadā puruhūte maghonī ā yajñaye suvitāya śrayetāṃ |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-2-6)

In these and similar Ṛks the great nature of the deities Uṣāsānakta is described. And in

द्वे विरूपे चरतः स्पर्धे अन्यान्या वत्समुप धापयेते । (*dve virūpe carataḥ spardhe anyānyā vatsamupa dhāpayete |*)
हरिरन्यस्यां भवति स्वधावाञ्चुक्रो अन्यस्यां ददृशे सुवर्चाः ॥ (*hariranyasyāṃ bhavati svadhāvāñcukro anyasyāṃ dadṛśe suvarcāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-1)

पुनः पुनर्जायमाना पुराणी समानं वर्णमभिशुंभमाना । श (*punaḥ punarjāyamānā purāṇī samānaṃ varṇamabhiśuṃbhamānā | śa*)
श्वघ्नीव कृत्नुर्विज आमिनाना मर्तस्य देवी जरयंत्यायुः ॥ (*śvaghnīva kṛtnurvija āminānā martasya devī jarayaṃtyāyuḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-10)

and similar Ṛks, the point is also described that Night and Uṣas, being born again and again, are of the form of children. Therefore, for the word यह्वी (*yahvī*) either of the two meanings above may be given.

नक्तोषसा (*naktoṣasā*)—among the other epithets of these deities the epithet ऋतस्य मातरा (*ṛtasya mātarā*) is very important.

द्रवतां त उषसा वाजयंती अग्ने वातस्य पथ्याभिरच्छ । (*dravatāṃ ta uṣasā vājayaṃtī agne vātasya pathyābhiraccha |*)
यत्स्रीमंजंति पूर्व्यं हविर्भिरा वंधुरेव तस्थतुर्दुरोणे ॥ (*yatsrīmaṃjaṃti pūrvyaṃ havirbhirā vaṃdhureva tasthaturduroṇe ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-14-3)

In this Ṛk the constant relation between the sacrifice and them is described. In all the Ṛks that invite them (there are about twenty such Ṛks) they are prayed to come with the other deities and sit on the sacred-grass seat. Moreover, they have a very close relation with Agni, who is kindled at dawn and at evening. For that very reason the figurative description that they bear and hold Agni between them,

### Printed p. 256

नक्तोषासा वर्णमामेम्याने धापयेते शिशुमेकं समीची । (*naktoṣāsā varṇamāmemyāne dhāpayete śiśumekaṃ samīcī |*)
द्यावाक्षामा रुक्मो अंतर्नि भाति देवा अग्निं धारयन्द्रविणोदां ॥ (*dyāvākṣāmā rukmo aṃtarni bhāti devā agniṃ dhārayandraviṇodāṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-5)

and similar Ṛks, tells that they help Agni, who is of the form of a child, to drink the oblation. Because of this relation they are said to be near the sacrificers. In general this description is repeated in the Āprī-sūktas too. For example:

अ सुष्वयंती यजते उपाके उषासानक्ता सदतां नि योन् । (*a suṣvayaṃtī yajate upāke uṣāsānaktā sadatāṃ ni yon |*)
दिव्ये योषणे बृहती सुरुक्मे अधि श्रियं शुक्रपिशं दधाने ॥ (*divye yoṣaṇe bṛhatī surukme adhi śriyaṃ śukrapiśaṃ dadhāne ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10.118-6)

In this Ṛk of an Āprī-sūkta the same matter is stated.

From this it is understood that the deities Uṣāsānaktā are not given a subordinate place in the sacrificial acts but obtain a most important place. This obtaining of a principal place is indicated in the mantra of the Praiṣa-sūkta,

होता यक्षदुषासानक्ता बृहती सुपेशसा नृग्ं पतिभ्यो योनिं कृण्वाने ॥ (*hotā yakṣaduṣāsānaktā bṛhatī supeśasā nṛgṃ patibhyo yoniṃ kṛṇvāne ‖*)
सग्ंस्मयमाने इंद्रेण देवैरेदं बर्हिः सीदतां वीतामाज्यस्य होतरज ॥ (*sagṃsmayamāne iṃdreṇa devairedaṃ barhiḥ sīdatāṃ vītāmājyasya hotaraja ‖*)
(Ḍvai Brā. 3-6-21)**NOTE**

in which Uṣā-naktā are prayed to sit on the sacred-grass seat not only with Agni but also with the other deities beginning with Indra. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa they are addressed as अहोरात्रे (*ahorātre*). In Ni. 8, 11 the Nirukta-author has described the nature of these deities. What the Nirukta-author says of the derivation of the two words उषस् (*uṣas*) and नक्त (*nakta*) and of the nature of these deities has been told earlier. In the Bṛhaddevatā too they are described in this way:

नक्तोषासौ च ये देव्यावाग्नेय्यावेव ते स्मृते । (*naktoṣāsau ca ye devyāvāgneyyāveva te smṛte |*)
श्याव्याग्नेयी हि कालस्य तस्यैवोषाः कलेव तु॥ (*śyāvyāgneyī hi kālasya tasyaivoṣāḥ kaleva tu‖*)
(Bṛ. Je. 3-8)**NOTE**

It is well known that these two deities, Uṣas and Nakta, are related to Agni. Of these, Śyāvī (the night-deity, 1-71-1) has a close relation with Agni; and from the Nirukta sentence उषः कस्मादुच्छतीति सत्या रात्रेरपरः कालः (*uṣaḥ kasmāducchatīti satyā rātreraparaḥ kālaḥ*) (Ni 2-18) it is known that Uṣas is the later part of that night.

तम उच्छत्युषा नक्तानक्तीमां हिमबिंदुभिः । (*tama ucchatyuṣā naktānaktīmāṃ himabiṃdubhiḥ |*)
अपि नाव्यक्तवर्णेति नइद् पूर्वाइच्चेरिदं भवेत् ॥ (*api nāvyaktavarṇeti naid pūrvāicceridaṃ bhavet ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-9)

### Printed p. 257

Of these two, Uṣas drives away the darkness. Night makes everything wet and pervaded by drops of dew. Or, since she has no distinct colour, the root अञ् (*añ*) joins with the particle नञ् (*nañ*) and gives the sense "of indistinct colour".

सा हि दोषा भवत्यादौ निशीथे सा तमस्वती । (*sā hi doṣā bhavatyādau niśīthe sā tamasvatī |*)
नाम्मा भवत्युषाश्चैव सैषा प्रागुदयाद्रवेः ॥ (*nāmmā bhavatyuṣāścaiva saiṣā prāgudayādraveḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ De. 3-10)

At the beginning this deity is named (Pra)doṣā, at midnight Tamasvatī, and before sunrise Uṣas. दोषा (*doṣā*), तमस्वती (*tamasvatī*), श्यावी (*śyāvī*), नक्ता (*naktā*) are all names of night, as read in the Nighaṇṭu (Nighaṇṭu 1-7).

Elsewhere in the Ṛk-saṃhitā, by epithets such as तंतुं ततं संवयंती (*taṃtuṃ tataṃ saṃvayaṃtī*) (2.3-6), the help of Uṣāsānaktā in the extension of the sacrifice is described.

##### Grammar

- भंदमाने—भवि कल्याणे । सुखे च । धातुः । भ्यादिः । लिटि शानचादेशः । इदितोनुम्धातोः इति सुमागमः । शप् विकरणः । आनेमुक् (पा. सू. ७-२-८२) इति मुकागमः । अदुपदेशात् परं लसार्वधातुकस्यागतत्वात् तास्यनुदात्तेत्—(पा. सू. ६-१-१८६) इति तदनुदात्तं भवति । तेन धातुस्वरः शिष्यते । सप्तम्येकवचनांतरूपम् ।**NOTE**
- उपाके—अंतोदात्तस्वरे प्राप्ते व्यत्ययेन आद्युदात्तं भवति ।
- नक्तोषसा—नक्तं च उषाश्च नक्तोषसा । द्विवचनस्य औकारविभक्तेः सुपां सुलुक्—(पा.सू. ७-१-३९) इति आकारादेशः । पूर्वपदस्य मकारस्य लोपः उत्तरपदे दीर्घश्च छांदसतया भवतः ।
- सुपेशसा—शोभनं पेशः रूपं ययोस्ते । पूर्ववत् आकारादेशः । नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१६२) इति उत्तरपदांतोदात्तस्वरे प्राप्ते तस्यापवादतया आद्युदात्तं द्व्यच्छंदसि (पा. सू. ६-२-११९) इति उत्तरपदमाद्युदात्तस्वरं भवति ।
- मातरा—सुपां सुलुक्—(पा. रू. ७-३९) इति विभक्तेः आकारादेशः ।**NOTE**
- सीदताम्—षद्ल्य विशरणगत्यवसादनेषु । लोट्प्रथमपुरुषद्विवचने लज्ञद्भावत्वात् तामादेशः । पाघ्राध्मा—(पा. सू. ७-३-७८) इति सीदादेशः । अदुपदेशात् परं प्रत्ययस्यागतत्वात् तास्यनुदात्तेत्—सूत्रेण तदनुदात्तं भवति । तेन धातुस्वरः शिष्यते । पादादौ आगतत्वात् निघातस्वरो न भवति ।

### Printed p. 258

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मं॒द्रजि॑ह्वा जुगु॒र्वणी॒ होता॑रा॒ दैन्व्या॑ क॒वी ।  
> य॒ज्ञं नो॑ यक्षतामि॒मं सि॒ध्रम॒द्य दि॑वि॒स्पृशं॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मंद्रजिह्वा । जुगुर्वणे इति । होतारा । दैव्या । कवी इति ।  
> यज्ञं । नः । यक्षतां । इमं । सिद्धं । अद्य । दिविस्पृशं ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मंद्रजिह्वा देवानां मादनज्वाल् जुगुर्वणी भृशं गृणतां स्तुवतां यजमानानां संभक्तारौ ॥ गृणतेर्यज्लुगंतात् क्विपि च्छांदसी रूपसिद्धिः । तस्मिन्नुपदे वनतेश्चंदसि वनसनरक्षिमथामितीन्प्रत्ययः ॥ कवी मेधाविनौ क्रांतदर्शिनौ
> दैव्या होतारा देवसंबंधिनौ होतार् अयं च मध्यमस्थानश्चेत्युभौ सिद्ध्रं फल.
> साधनभूतं ॥ सिधेः स्थायितंचीत्यादिना रक् ॥ दिविस्पृशं द्युलोकनासिदेवान्
> स्पृशंतं । तैर्हनिषः स्वीक्रियमाणत्पादिति भावः । ईदृशं नोऽस्मदीयमिमं यज्ञमद्य यक्षतां । अनुतिष्ठतां पूजयतां ना । सम्यङ्निर्वाहयतामित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

मंद्रजिह्वा (*maṃdrajihvā*): those whose flames gladden the gods. जुगुर्वणी (*jugurvaṇī*): those who bestow (संभक्तारौ (*saṃbhaktārau*)) on the sacrificers who greatly praise and extol; the form is explained as an irregular (Vedic) formation from the root गृण (*gṛṇa*) in the intensive-luk, with *kvip*.**NOTE** कवी (*kavī*): the two who are wise and far-seeing. दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*): the two Hotṛs related to the gods; he says that these two are this (terrestrial) Agni and the one who has the middle region as his place. सिद्ध्रं (*siddhraṃ*): the instrument of the fruit. दिविस्पृशं (*divispṛśaṃ*): touching the dwellers of the heavenly world, the gods; the idea is that the oblations are accepted by them.**NOTE** Let them today perform (यक्षतां (*yakṣatāṃ*)) such a sacrifice of ours: carry it out, worship it, bring it to proper completion.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मंद्रजिह्वा (*maṃdrajihvā*)—those whose flames cause delight (to the gods)
- जुगुर्वणी (*jugurvaṇī*)—those who favour the sacrificers who offer praise
- कवी (*kavī*)—the wise ones
- दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*)—the two forms of Agni, who call the gods (to the sacrifice)
- सिद्धं (*siddhaṃ*)—that which gives fruit
- दिविस्पृशं (*divispṛśaṃ*)—that which reaches (the gods) in the world of the gods
- नः इमं यज्ञं (*naḥ imaṃ yajñaṃ*)—this sacrifice of ours
- अद्य (*adya*)—now
- यक्षतां (*yakṣatāṃ*)—let them perform

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the two forms of Agni, whose flames gladden the gods and who favour the devoted sacrificers, now perform this sacrifice of ours, which gives fruit and reaches the gods in the world of the gods.

### Printed p. 259

##### Printed English

May the two pleasing-tongued receivers of praise, the divine and sage invokers (of the gods), officiate to-day at this our sacrifice, which confers (rewards) and attains heaven.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*)—देवसंबंधिनौ होतारौ। अयं च मध्यमस्थानश्च इत्युभौ। (*devasaṃbaṃdhinau hotārau| ayaṃ ca madhyamasthānaśca ityubhau|*) Sāyaṇa has explained these as the two forms of Agni: the terrestrial Agni and the Agni of the atmosphere, both related to the gods and both Hotṛs. Some modern scholars have given a different meaning and have also shown differences of opinion among themselves. The scholar Goldner has explained them as the Hotṛ who is the sacrificer and his Agni; Hang as the Hotṛ and the Adhvaryu; Oldenberg as the Hotṛ and his assistant, the Maitrāvaruṇa. None of these is reasonable.

That the performing of the sacrifice is the chief duty of these Hotṛs is stated in the Ṛks 1-13-8 and 1-18-7 by यज्ञं नो यक्षतामिमं । (*yajñaṃ no yakṣatāmimaṃ |*) and in the Ṛk 2-3-7 by देवान्यजंतावृतुथा । (*devānyajaṃtāvṛtuthā |*) This reminder of duty should apply to the human Hotṛ, and Agni is addressed figuratively. In the same way, in

देव्या होतारा प्रथमा सुवाचा मिमाना यज्ञं मनुषो यजध्यै । (*devyā hotārā prathamā suvācā mimānā yajñaṃ manuṣo yajadhyai |*)
प्रचोदयंता विदथेषु कारू प्राचीनं ज्योतिः प्रदिशा दिशंता । (*pracodayaṃtā vidatheṣu kārū prācīnaṃ jyotiḥ pradiśā diśaṃtā |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-111-7)

it is the opinion of some scholars that the description "producers of the sacrifice" or "those who measure the sacrifice" also suggests the same idea.

Thus some epithets, though common to both Hotṛs, suggest only the duty of the human Hotṛ; other epithets, again common to both, describe only the duties of Agni, the divine Hotṛ. For example:

देव्या होतारा प्रथमा न्यृंजे सप्त पृक्षासः स्वधया मदंति । (*devyā hotārā prathamā nyṛṃje sapta pṛkṣāsaḥ svadhayā madaṃti |*)
ऋतं शसंत ऋतमित्त आहुरनु व्रतं व्रतसा दीध्यानाः ॥ (*ṛtaṃ śasaṃta ṛtamitta āhuranu vrataṃ vratasā dīdhyānāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-4-7)

In this Ṛk there is no doubt that the epithet प्रथमा (*prathamā*) refers to Agni, the divine Hotṛ.

### Printed p. 260
In 1-13-4 too, होता मनुर्हितः (*hotā manurhitaḥ*) is an epithet of Agni. The precise difference between these two hotṛs is stated in the Praiṣa-sūkta thus:
होता यक्षद्देव्या होतारा मंद्रा पोतारा कवी प्रचेतसा । स्विष्टमिद्यान्यः करदिषा स्वभिगूर्तमन्य ऊर्जा सतवसेमं यज्ञं दिवि देवेषु धत्तां वीतामाज्यस्य होतर्यज । (*hotā yakṣaddevyā hotārā maṃdrā potārā kavī pracetasā | sviṣṭamidyānyaḥ karadiṣā svabhigūrtamanya ūrjā satavasemaṃ yajñaṃ divi deveṣu dhattāṃ vītāmājyasya hotaryaja |*) (Tai. Brā. 3-6-2-2)
In this sentence it is indicated that one hotṛ is to make the sacrifice acceptable with the oblation in the form of food, and the other is to establish this sacrifice in heaven among the gods. This exchange of functions shows the difference between the human hotṛ and Agni, the divine hotṛ. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa:
दैन्या होतारा यजति प्राणापानौ वै दैन्या होतारा प्राणापानावेव तत्प्रीणाति प्राणापानौ यजमाने दधाति । (*dainyā hotārā yajati prāṇāpānau vai dainyā hotārā prāṇāpānāveva tatprīṇāti prāṇāpānau yajamāne dadhāti |*) (Ai. Brā. 2-4)
Thus the identity of these hotṛs with prāṇa and apāna is stated, and it is said that the one who recites this mantra secures for the sacrificer the well-being of prāṇa and apāna. In the Nirukta:
दैन्या होतारा दैन्य होतारावयं चाग्निरस् च मध्यमः (*dainyā hotārā dainya hotārāvayaṃ cāgniras ca madhyamaḥ*) (Ni. 8-12)
Thus, in passages that support Sāyaṇa's meaning, the two are said to be the terrestrial Agni and the Agni of the atmosphere. In the Bṛhaddevatā too, approving the same view:
