The conclusion seems to be warranted, therefore, that Agni and Apam Napat were once distinct Gods—Gods, of the lightning, who were, however, associated with each other and

### Printed p. 295
were much alike in certain particulars, and that this association, and resemblance ultimately resulted, possibly at the close of the Vedic Period, in a confusion and Union of the two, as a result of which Apam Napat was absorbed by Agni."
( Journal of American Orintal Society. Vol. XIX. (2) p. 144. )

तव्यसीं (*tavyasīṃ*)—तवीयसीं अतिशयेन वर्धयित्रीं । तवतिर्वृद्ध्यर्थः । (*tavīyasīṃ atiśayena vardhayitrīṃ | tavatirvṛddhyarthaḥ |*) It means either "giver of vigour" or "causing growth", and is an adjective of धीतिं (*dhītiṃ*), "thought" (matim).

स शेवृधमधि धा द्युम्न मस्मे महि क्षत्रं जनाषाळिंद्र तव्यं । रक्षा च नो मघोनः पाहि सूरीन्राये च नः स्वपत्या इषे धाः ॥ (*sa śevṛdhamadhi dhā dyumna masme mahi kṣatraṃ janāṣāḷiṃdra tavyaṃ | rakṣā ca no maghonaḥ pāhi sūrīnrāye ca naḥ svapatyā iṣe dhāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-54-11)

In this Ṛk, तव्यं क्षत्रं (*tavyaṃ kṣatraṃ*) is प्रवृद्दं बलं (*pravṛddaṃ balaṃ*); it suggests the two meanings "greatly grown" and "endowed with power".

धीतिं (*dhītiṃ*)—यागक्षलणां उक्तगुणकं कर्म । (*yāgakṣalaṇāṃ uktaguṇakaṃ karma |*) Here it means the rite of the kind mentioned, of the nature of a sacrifice. The word dhīti has two meanings, "rite" and "wisdom". [In the Nirukta (Ni. 3-8) it is read among the names of the fingers.] The Nirukta-writers have cited two Ṛks in which the two meanings, rite and wisdom, occur:

इयं शुष्मेभिर्बिसखा इवारुजत्सानु गिरीणां तविषेभिरूर्मिभिः । सारावतघ्नीमवसे सुवृक्तिभिः सरस्वतीमा विवसेम धीतिभिः ॥ (*iyaṃ śuṣmebhirbisakhā ivārujatsānu girīṇāṃ taviṣebhirūrmibhiḥ | sārāvataghnīmavase suvṛktibhiḥ sarasvatīmā vivasema dhītibhiḥ ‖*)
( Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-61-2 )

In this verse they explain धीतिभिः (*dhītibhiḥ*) as कर्मभिः (*karmabhiḥ*) (by rites), and in

ऋतस्य हि शुरुधः संति पूर्वीऋतस्य धीतिर्वृजिनानि हंति । ऋतस्य श्लोको बधिरा ततर्द कर्णा बुधानः शुचमान आयोः । (*ṛtasya hi śurudhaḥ saṃti pūrvīṛtasya dhītirvṛjināni haṃti | ṛtasya śloko badhirā tatarda karṇā budhānaḥ śucamāna āyoḥ |*)
( Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-23-8 **NOTE** )**NOTE**

as "wisdom". In some places the word is also used in the sense of "praise". In the present Ṛk it means the rite in the form of sacrifice.

##### Grammar

- तव्यसीम्— सूत्रमात्रनिर्दिष्टः तनधातुः वृद्ध्यर्थे प्रयुक्तः । तस्मात् ण्वुल् तृचौ (पा. सू. ३-१-१३३) इति तृच् प्रत्ययः । तुश्चंदसि (पा. सू. ५-३-५९) इति तृजंतात् ईयसुन् प्रत्ययः । तुरिष्ठेमेयस्सु (पा. सू. ६-४-१५४) इति ईयसुनि परे तृचो लोपः । यस्येति च इति अकारस्य लोपः । छांदसत्वात् प्रत्ययस्य ईकारस्य लोपः । ईयसुन् नित्त्वात् इत्यादिर्नित्यम् इति आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 296
- नव्यसीम्— अतिशयार्थे ईयसुनि आगते पूर्ववत् अकारलोपः । पूर्ववत् स्वरो भवति ।
- मतिम्— मंत्रे वृषेष— (पा. सू. ३-३-६६) इति क्तिन् उदात्तो भवति । द्वितीया एकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- भरे— भृञ् भरणे धातुः । लट् उत्तमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- अपाम्— ऊडिदं पदादि— (पा. सू. ६-१-१७१) इति विभक्तेरुदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- नपात्— पा रक्षणे धातुः । तस्मात् शतृ आगते पात् इति रूपं भवति । तस्य नञ् सह समासे नभ्राण् नपात्— (पा. सू. ६-३-७५) इति नलोपाभावेन निपातितम् ।
- पृथिव्याम्— पृथिवीशब्दः जीषंतत्वात् अंतोदात्तः । विभक्तौ परायां तस्य यणादेशे जाते तत्परस्य विभक्तेरनुदात्तत्वात् उदात्तयणो— (पा. सू. ६-१-१७४) इति तस्य उदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- असीदत्— षद्ल्य विशरणगत्यवसादनेषु धातुः । छंदसि लुज् लज् लिटः— (पा. सू. ३-४-६) इति वर्तमानार्थे लज् भवति । प्रथमपुरुष एकवचने तिप् प्रत्ययः । इतश्च इति इकारलोपः । शप् विकरणः । पाफ्राध्मा— (पा. सू. ७-३-६८) इति धातोः सीदादेशः । यः इति यच्छब्दसंबंधात् निघातस्वरो न भवति । अडागमस्य उदात्तत्वात् आद्युदात्तो भवति ।
- ऋत्वियः— ऋतुशब्दः । यज्ञर्त्वग्भ्यांघखञ् (पा. सू. ५-१-७१) इति घ प्रत्ययः । आयनेया— (पा. सू. ७-१-२) इति तस्य इयादेशः । यणादेशः । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरेण इकार उदात्तो भवति । ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 297
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स जाय॑मानः पर॒मे व्यो॑मन्या॒विर॒ग्निर॑भवन्मात॒रिश्व॑ने ।  
> अ॒स्य क्रत्वा॑ समिधा॒नस्य॑ म॒ज्मना॒ प्र द्यावा॑ शो॒चिः पृ॑थि॒वी  ॑
> अरोचयत् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । जायमानः । परमे । विओमनि । आविः । अग्निः । अभवत् । मातरिश्वने ।  
> अस्य । क्रत्वा । संइधानस्य । मज्मना । प्र । द्यावा । शोचिः । पृथिवी इति ।  
> अरोचयत् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स पूर्वोक्तोग्निर्जायमानोऽरणीभ्यामुत्पद्यमानः काष्ठेषु ना प्रादुर्भूतः सन् तदानीमेव परम उत्कृष्टे प्रोमनि विविधरक्षणवति वेदिदेशे मातरिश्वनेऽंतरिक्षसंचारिणे नाय्वे प्रथममाविरभवत् । प्रत्यक्षोऽभूत् । त्वमग्ने
> प्रथमो मातरिश्वन आविर्भव । ऋ. १-३१-३। इत्यादिश्रुत्यंतरप्रसिद्धेः । वायुसंयोगात्पज्वलित इत्यर्थः । अथवा मातरि फलस्य निर्मातरि यज्ञॆ श्वसिति चेष्टत इति मातरिश्वा यजमानः । तदर्थं । किंच समिधानस्येंधनैः सम्यग्दर्धमानस्याग्नेर्मज्जना ।
> बलनामैतत् । बलवता क्रत्वा क्रतुना कर्मणा ज्वालनादिव्यापारेण शोचिद्यावा
> पृथिवी द्यां च पृथिवीं च प्रारोचयत् । प्रकर्षणादीपयत् ॥ मध्यॆ शोचिःशब्दश्चांदसः ॥ मज्मना क्रत्वा समिधानस्येति वा योज्यं । प्रबलेन समिंधनादिव्यापारेण समिध्यमानस्येत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Agni spoken of before, being born—arising from the two fire-sticks—and having appeared in the pieces of wood, at that very moment first manifested himself in the supreme, excellent व्योमनि (*vyomani*) (the place of varied protection, i.e. the altar), to मातरिश्वने (*mātariśvane*), the wind moving in the atmosphere. He became visible; this is known from another śruti, त्वमग्ने प्रथमो मातरिश्वन आविर्भव (*tvamagne prathamo mātariśvana āvirbhava*) (Ṛ. 1-31-3). The meaning: through contact with the wind he blazed up. Or else: Mātariśvan is the sacrificer, who breathes (śvasiti), i.e. exerts himself, in the sacrifice, the maker (nirmātṛ) of its fruit; for his sake. Further, by the मज्मना (*majmanā*) (a word for strength) of Agni, well kindled with fuel, by powerful क्रत्वा (*kratvā*), that is, by action, by the work of blazing and the like, the radiance lit up heaven and earth, brightening them intensely. The word शोचिः (*śociḥ*) in the middle is Vedic usage. Or construe मज्मना क्रत्वा समिधानस्य (*majmanā kratvā samidhānasya*): of him kindled by powerful action of kindling and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—the one described before
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- जायमानः (*jāyamānaḥ*)—as soon as born
- परमे (*parame*)—in the excellent
- व्योमनि (*vyomani*)—in the atmosphere
- मातरिश्वने (*mātariśvane*)—facing the wind
- आविः अभवत् (*āviḥ abhavat*)—became visible. [Or व्योमनि (*vyomani*)—on the altar; मातरिश्वने (*mātariśvane*)—facing the sacrificer; आविः अभवत् (*āviḥ abhavat*)—became visible.] (And)
- मज्मना (*majmanā*)—powerful
- क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)—by the action of blazing and the like
- समिधानस्य अस्य (*samidhānasya asya*)—of this Agni kindled by fuel
- क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)—by the action of blazing and the like
- द्यावा (*dyāvā*)—heaven
- पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*)—and earth
- प्र अरोचयत् (*pra arocayat*)—lit up

### Printed p. 298

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as Agni was born, he became visible in the excellent atmosphere, facing the wind. The radiance of Agni, kindled with fuel, lit up heaven and earth by the powerful actions of blazing and the like.

##### Printed English

As soon as born, was that (Agni) manifested to Matarishwan in the highest atmosphere, and his radiance, kindled by vigorous effort, spread through heaven and earth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk it is described that Agni, as soon as born, appeared in the sky to Mātariśvan, and that afterwards the radiance of him, blazing, lit up heaven and earth. Two constructions of the first half are possible here. First: व्योमनि (*vyomani*)—in the atmosphere, मातरिश्वने (*mātariśvane*)—before Mātariśvan, आविरभवत् (*āvirabhavat*). Second: व्योमनि (*vyomani*)—on the sacrificial altar, मातरिश्वने (*mātariśvane*)—facing the sacrificer, आविरभवत् (*āvirabhavat*).
(1) That Agni appeared in the sky to Mātariśvan is well known. The word व्योम (*vyoma*) is read among the names of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10). It is formed from the root व्येञ् (*vyeñ*), meaning to pervade, to cover; the sense is the pervading atmosphere.

त्वमग्ने प्रथमो मातरिश्वन आविर्भव सुक्रतू या विवस्वते । अरेजेतां रोदसी होतृपूर्येऽसघ्नोर्भारमयजो महो वसो ॥ (*tvamagne prathamo mātariśvana āvirbhava sukratū yā vivasvate | arejetāṃ rodasī hotṛpūrye 'saghnorbhāramayajo maho vaso ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-31-3)

In Ṛks of this kind it is well known that the manifestation of Agni, in the form of lightning, took place first to the wind. Moreover,

वह्निं यशसं विदथस्य केतुं सप्राव्यं दूतं सद्योअर्थं । द्विजन्मानं रयिमिव प्रशस्तं रातिं भरद्भृगवे मातरिश्वा ॥ (*vahniṃ yaśasaṃ vidathasya ketuṃ saprāvyaṃ dūtaṃ sadyoarthaṃ | dvijanmānaṃ rayimiva praśastaṃ rātiṃ bharadbhṛgave mātariśvā ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-60-1)

In Ṛks of this kind, Mātariśvan, for the benefit of Bhṛgu and Manu, the performers of sacrifice, brought to the earth Agni who had been hidden in a distant region, and to the earth for the benefit of all creatures

### Printed p. 299
(Continuing) It has also been done in many places. Therefore there are sound grounds for stating the first meaning.

(2) If one examines what propriety there is in stating the second meaning, and whether it has sound grounds in support:

व्योमनि (*vyomani*)—Here the word व्योमनि (*vyomani*) has been explained as वेदिदेशे (*vedideśe*). The form is derived from the root अव (*ava*), meaning protection, joined with the prefix वि (*vi*). As the etymology of this word, विविधरक्षणवति वेदिदेशे (*vividharakṣaṇavati vedideśe*) has been shown: the region of the altar furnished with manifold protections. This word is used in this sense in many places in the Ṛk-saṃhitā. For example:

> ⟦इयं वेदिः परो अंतः पृथिव्या अयं यज्ञो भुवनस्य नाभिः⟧ ।
> ⟦अयं सोमो वृष्णो अश्वस्य रेतो ब्रह्मायं वाचः परमं व्योम⟧ ।

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-35)

In this Ṛk the word for "sky/space" is explained as उत्कृष्टं रक्षकं स्थानं (*utkṛṣṭaṃ rakṣakaṃ sthānaṃ*), "a superior, protecting place"; and in

> ⟦ऋतेन ऋतं धरुणं धारयंत यज्ञस्य शोके परमे प्रोमन्⟧ ।
> ⟦दिपो धर्मन् धरुणो सेदुषो नृञ्चात्यैरजाता अभि ये नसक्षुः⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-15-2)

it is explained as उत्कृष्टे स्थाने उत्तरवेद्यां (*utkṛṣṭe sthāne uttaravedyāṃ*), "in the superior place, on the northern altar", that is, on the excellent uttaravedi. In the same way, if in the present Ṛk too the word व्योम (*vyoma*) is taken to mean the altar, then the word मातरिश्व (*mātariśva*) becomes मातरि फलस्य निर्मातरि यज्ञे श्वसिति चेष्टते इति मातरिश्वा यजमानः (*mātari phalasya nirmātari yajñe śvasiti ceṣṭate iti mātariśvā yajamānaḥ*): since he is active in the sacrifice, the place of origin of every kind of fruit, मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*) means the sacrificer. It can therefore be explained that Agni, as soon as he was kindled, appeared on the altar facing the sacrificer.

The Brāhmaṇa sentence

> ⟦व्योमसदित्येष वै व्योमसदृश्योम वा एतत्सद्मनां यस्मिन्नेष आसन्नस्तपति⟧ ।

(Ai. Brā. 18-20) also supports this meaning.

If, by the first construction, मातरिश्व (*mātariśva*) means the wind, one must ask what relation Agni has to this Mātariśvan. If it is said that Agni appeared facing him, it means the two are different persons. But if the history stated here is examined a little more closely, certain grounds show that the two are identical.

> ⟦उदुष्टुतः समिधा यहो अद्यौद्वर्ष्मन्विवो अधि नाभा पृथिव्याः⟧ ।
> ⟦मित्रो अग्निरीड्र्यो मातरिश्वादूतो वक्षद्यजथाय देवान्⟧ ।

(Khi. Saṃ. 9, 25-9).

### Printed p. 300
In this Ṛk Sāyaṇa explains the word मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*) as मातर्यंतरिक्षे श्वसिति प्राणिति सूर्यरूपेण चेष्टत इति मातरिश्वा (*mātaryaṃtarikṣe śvasiti prāṇiti sūryarūpeṇa ceṣṭata iti mātariśvā*) or मातररण्यां श्वसिति निवसतीति मातरिश्वा (*mātararaṇyāṃ śvasiti nivasatīti mātariśvā*): Agni has this name either because he moves in the atmosphere in the form of the sun, or because he dwells in the mother-like fire-sticks. He has shown support for his explanation by saying that the Nirukta-writers too show this same etymology and take it to mean Agni. Likewise,

> ⟦तं शुभ्रमग्निमवसे हवामहे वैश्वानरं मातरिश्वानमुक्थं⟧ ।
> ⟦बृहस्पतिं मनुषो देवतातये विप्रं श्रोतारमतिथिं रघुष्यदं⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-26-2)

in this Ṛk, and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-4 (स मातरिश्वा पुरुवारपुष्टिः (*sa mātariśvā puruvārapuṣṭiḥ*)), the identity of Agni and Mātariśvan is stated. And as is said in the present Ṛk (1-143-2),

> ⟦उदस्तंभीत्समिधा नाकमृष्टोग्नि र्भवन्नुत्तमो रोचमानां⟧ ।
> ⟦यदी भृगुभ्यः परि मातरिश्वा गुहा संतं हव्यवाहं समाधे⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-5-10)

in this Ṛk it is clearly stated that the two are distinct. [But this is the very sentence of Ṛk 3-5-9 above, which states the identity of Agni and Mātariśvan, or says that the word मातरिश्वा (*mātariśvā*) is an epithet of Agni.]**NOTE**

The other qualities and the power of Mātariśvan will be explained later in the contexts suitable to them.

##### Grammar

- जायमानः— जनी प्रादुर्भावे धातुः । लडर्थे शानच् प्रत्ययः । श्यन् विकरणम् । ज्ञाजनोर्जा (पा.सू. ७-३-७९) इति जादेशः । आने मुक् (पा.सू. ७-२-८२) इति सूत्रेण अदंतस्य मुकागमः । अदुपदेशस्य परत्र लसार्वधातुकस्य आगतत्वात् तास्यनुदात्तेत् (पा. सू. ६-१-१४६) इति तदनुदात्तं भवति । श्यन् नित्त्वात् आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- मातरिश्वने— मातरि स्वयतीति मारिश्वा । श्वन्नुक्सन् (उ. सू. १-१५७) इति कनि प्रत्ययांतत्वेन निपातितम् । चतुर्थी एकवचनांतरूपम् । प्रत्ययस्वरेण इकार उदात्तो भवति ।

### Printed p. 301
- क्रत्वा— क्रतु+आ इति सति जसादिषु छंदसि वाचनम् इति वचनेन नाभावः न भवति । यणादेशः ।
- समिधानस्य— इंधी दिप्तौ धातुः । लडर्थे शानच् प्रत्ययः । बहुलंछंदसि इति विकरणस्य लुक् । प्रत्ययस्य जित्त्वात् अनिदितां इति उपधानकारलोपः । शानच् चित्त्वात् चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति । षष्ठीएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- मज्मना— टुमस्टो शुद्ध धातुः । अस्मात् औणादिको मनि प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण मध्योदात्तो भवति ।**NOTE**
- द्यावापृथिवी— दौश्च पृथिवी च द्यावापृथिव्यौ दिवोद्यावा (पा.सू. ६-३-२९) इति पूर्वपदस्य द्यावादेशः । छांदसत्वेन मध्ये शोचिः इति शब्दः आगतः । सुपां सुलुक् (पा. सू. ७-३-३९) इति द्वितीयाविभक्तेः पूर्वसवर्णदीर्घः भवति ।
- अरोचयत्— रुच दीप्तौ धातुः । हेतुमति च (पा. सू. ६-१-३९) इति प्रयोजकव्यापारे द्योत्यमाने णिच् । पुगंतलघूपधस्यच इति लघूपधगुणः । लङ् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति । ॥ २ ॥

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्य त्वे॒षा अ॒जरा॑ अ॒स्य भा॒नवः॑ सुसं॒दृशः॑ सु॒प्रती॑कस्य सु॒द्युतः॑।  
> भात्व॑क्षसो॒ अत्य॒क्तुर्न सिंध॑वो॒ऽग्ने रे॑जंते॒ अस॑संतो  ॒॑
> अजराः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । त्वेषाः । अजराः । अस्य । भानवः । सुसंदृशः । सुप्रतीकस्य । सुद्युतः ।  
> भात्वक्षसः । अति । अक्तुः । न । सिंधवः । अग्नेः । रेजंते । अससंतः । अजराः ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 302

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्य स्तूयमानस्याग्नेस्तेषा दीप्तयोऽजरा जरारहिता अजीर्णाः ।
> अविरता इत्यर्थः ॥ नञ्चो जरमरेत्यादिनोत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥ तथा सुप्रतीकस्य शोभनमुखस्यास्याग्नेर्भानवो रश्मयः । दीप्तेरुक्तत्वादत्र विस्फुलिंगा अवगंतव्याः । ते च सुसंदृशः सुष्टु सम्यग्रष्टारः । सर्वतो व्याप्ता इत्यर्थः ।
> सुद्युतः सुष्टु सर्वतो द्योतमानाः । तथास्याग्नेर्भात्वक्षसः । त्वक्ष इति बलनाम
> त्वक्षः शर्ध इति बलनामसु पाठात् । भासमानबलाः । अक्तुरिति रात्रिनामैतत् अक्तुरूर्म्योति तन्नामसूक्तत्वात् । द्वितीयार्थे प्रथमा । आक्तुं जगदंजकं नैशं
> तमोऽत्यतिक्रम्य सिंधवः स्यंदमानाः सर्वत्र व्याप्नुवंतोऽससंतः स्वव्यापारेष्वस्वपंतोऽविरताः अत एवाजरा न रेजंते । न कंपंते । दाहपाकादिषु न चलंति
> न चाल्यंते वानैः । यद्वा । नशब्दो दृष्टांतवचनः । भात्वक्षसो भा एव
> त्वक्षो बलं यस्य तादृशस्यादित्यस्य सिंधवो न रश्मय इव । ते यथा स्यंदनशीला
> व्याप्तिमंतोऽक्तुरति अंजकं तमोऽतिक्रम्याससंतो रेजंते तद्वद्दात्वक्षसोs
> स्याग्नेरुक्तलक्षणा दीप्तयोऽपि सर्वत्र रेजंते । कंपंते । व्याप्नुवंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of this Agni who is being praised, the radiances (त्वेषाः (*tveṣāḥ*)) are अजराः (*ajarāḥ*): free of old age, undecaying, that is, unceasing. The accent on the first syllable of the second member follows the rule beginning नञ्चो जरमर (*nañco jaramara*). Likewise the rays (भानवः (*bhānavaḥ*)) of this Agni of fair face (सुप्रतीकस्य (*supratīkasya*)); since radiance has been mentioned, sparks are to be understood here. They are सुसंदृशः (*susaṃdṛśaḥ*), well and fully seen, that is, spread everywhere; and सुद्युतः (*sudyutaḥ*), shining well in all directions. Likewise of this Agni who is भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*): त्वक्ष (*tvakṣa*) is a name for strength, since त्वक्षः शर्ध (*tvakṣaḥ śardha*) is read among the names of strength; thus "of shining strength". अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*) is a name of night, as it is recited among its names (अक्तुरूर्म्योति (*akturūrmyoti*) etc.); the nominative stands in the sense of the accusative. Passing beyond the night-darkness that smears the world, the सिंधवः (*siṃdhavaḥ*), flowing and pervading everywhere, अससंतः (*asasaṃtaḥ*), not sleeping in their own activities, unceasing, and therefore undecaying, न रेजंते (*na rejaṃte*) do not tremble; in burning, cooking and so on they do not waver, nor are they moved by winds.

Or else: the word न (*na*) expresses comparison. Like the rays of the sun, whose strength is light itself (भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*)), which, flowing and pervasive, pass beyond the smearing darkness and without sleep रेजंते (*rejaṃte*) shine tremulously, so the radiances of this Agni, of the stated character, tremble, that is, pervade, everywhere.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Agni
- त्वेषाः (*tveṣāḥ*)—the radiances
- अजराः (*ajarāḥ*)—free of decay
- सुप्रतीकस्य (*supratīkasya*)—of him with excellent face
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Agni
- भानवः (*bhānavaḥ*)—rays (sparks)
- सुसंदृशः (*susaṃdṛśaḥ*)—pervading so as to be visible to all
- सुद्युतः (*sudyutaḥ*)—very bright
- अग्नेः (*agneḥ*)—of Agni
- भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*)—the powers of the nature of light
- अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*)—the darkness that pervades (the world)
- अति (*ati*)—pushing through (piercing)
- सिंधवः (*siṃdhavaḥ*)—flowing ones
- अससंतः (*asasaṃtaḥ*)—without interruption (in their activities)
- अजराः (*ajarāḥ*)—not wasting
- न रेजंते (*na rejaṃte*)—do not hold back (in their tasks)
- (Or) भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*)—of the sun, whose strength is of the nature of light
- सिंधवो न (*siṃdhavo na*)—like rays
- अक्तुः अति (*aktuḥ ati*)—piercing the darkness
- अससंतः (*asasaṃtaḥ*)—without the least interruption (in their duties)
- रेजंते (*rejaṃte*)—pervade in the form of tremor

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The radiances of Agni are undecaying. The sparks, the instruments of this Agni of excellent face, pervade so as to be visible to all and are exceedingly bright. The brilliance of this Agni, like the rays of the sun whose strength is of the nature of light, pierces the darkness that envelops the world and, with uninterrupted activity, unwasting, pervades the world.

### Printed p. 303

##### Printed English

His radiance is undecaying ; the rays of him who is of pleasing aspect, are everywhere visible and bright : the intensely shining, all-pervading, unceasing, undecaying (rays) of Agni, desist not (from their functions).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the radiance of Agni, and his power of the nature of light, destroying the black darkness and ever intent on its duty, are described.

सुप्रतीकस्य (*supratīkasya*)—प्रतीकं मुखं प्रकाशाख्यं दर्शनं वा (*pratīkaṃ mukhaṃ prakāśākhyaṃ darśanaṃ vā*). The word प्रतीक (*pratīka*) means either face or a radiant appearance. The Nirukta-writer, quoting

> ⟦यावन्मात्रमुषसो न प्रतीकं सुपर्ण्यो३वसते मातरिश्वः⟧ ।
> ⟦तावद्दधात्युप यज्ञमायन् ब्राह्मणो होतुरवरो निषिदन्⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-19)

has taken it in the sense of radiant appearance. Here the word is used so as to include both meanings: the shining face of the Agni who has appeared.

भानवः (*bhānavaḥ*)—The word भानु (*bhānu*) can mean both the sun and rays.

> ⟦उदपप्तन्नरुणा भानवो वृथा स्वायुजो अरुषीर्गा आयुक्षत⟧ ।
> ⟦अक्रन्नुषासो वयुनानि पूर्वथा रुशंतं भानुमरुषी रशिश्रयुः⟧ ।

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-2)

In this Ṛk the golden-hued radiances of the Ushas goddesses darted upward .... .... .... .... then, joined with their splendour, they united with the bright-hued sun; thus both meanings of भानु (*bhānu*) appear in one Ṛk. In the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa,

> ⟦कविशस्तो बृहता भानुनागा हव्या जुषस्व⟧ ।

(Ai. Brā. 2-12)

Agni is entreated: "Come with your great splendour and be gracious." Likewise here too the meaning is the radiant rays of Agni.

सुसंदृशः (*susaṃdṛśaḥ*)—सुष्टु सम्यग्रष्टारः । सर्वतो व्याप्ता इत्यर्थः । (*suṣṭu samyagraṣṭāraḥ | sarvato vyāptā ityarthaḥ |*) The meaning is: pervading so as to be visible to all.

### Printed p. 304
अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*)—This word is read among the 23 names of night, श्यावी, क्षपा, शर्वरी (*śyāvī, kṣapā, śarvarī*) and so on (Ni. 1-18). In many places अक्तु (*aktu*) means just night. The word is also used in the senses of darkness, ray, weapon and so on. Since the root अंज् (*aṃj*) has the meanings cover, conceal, smear, the meanings arise: darkness that covers with shade; a ray that envelops all around; a weapon that covers the body. For example:

> ⟦घनेव विष्वग्वि जह्यराष्ट्रपुर्जंभ यो अस्मध्रुक्⟧ ।
> ⟦यो मर्त्यः शिशीते अत्यक्तुभिर्मा नः स रिपुरीशत⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-36-16)

In this Ṛk the word अक्तुभिः (*aktubhiḥ*) is explained as अवरकैरायुधैः (*avarakairāyudhaiḥ*). But in this meaning the word is used rarely. Its use in the meanings darkness, and night enveloped by darkness, is more frequent.

> ⟦उत्तानायामजनयन्तुषूतं भुवदग्निः पुरुषेशासु गर्भः⟧ ।
> ⟦शिरिणायां चिदक्तुना महोभिरपरीवृतो वसति प्रचेताः⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-10-3)

In such Ṛks, अक्तुना (*aktunā*) is explained as अंजनादक्तुस्तमः (*aṃjanādaktustamaḥ*): darkness is अक्तु (*aktu*) because it pervades. So in the present Ṛk too the meaning is the darkness that pervades the world. The radiance of Agni drives out such black darkness.

अससंतः (*asasaṃtaḥ*)—स्वव्यापारेषु अस्वसंतः अविरताः (*svavyāpāreṣu asvasaṃtaḥ aviratāḥ*). The meaning is: in their own activities, without sloth, with unbroken devotion to work. The Nirukta-writer, quoting

> ⟦अति वायो ससतो याहि शश्वतो यत्र ग्रावा वदति तत्र गच्छतं गृहमिंद्रश्च गच्छतं⟧ ।
> ⟦वि सूनृता ददृशे रीयते धृतमा पूर्णया नियुता याथो अध्वरमिंद्रश्च याथो अध्वरं⟧ ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-135-7)

explains ससतः (*sasataḥ*) as अलस्यादिना निद्रां कुर्वतः (*alasyādinā nidrāṃ kurvataḥ*). The meaning is: without such sloth and sleep, ever engaged in their activities.

भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*)—त्वक्ष इति बलनाम, 'त्वक्षः, शर्धः' इति बलनामसु (नि३-९) पाठात् । भासमानबलाः । (*tvakṣa iti balanāma, 'tvakṣaḥ, śardhaḥ ' iti balanāmasu (ni3-9) pāṭhāt | bhāsamānabalāḥ |*) The word त्वक्ष (*tvakṣa*) is read among the names of strength. The meaning is: powers of the nature of light.

### Printed p. 305

*Special Topics (continued)*

इंद्रियं वीर्यमात्मनत्ते तेजो ना अग्निरिंद्रियं वीर्यमिंद्र उभे वीर्ये । ( (*iṃdriyaṃ vīryamātmanatte tejo nā agniriṃdriyaṃ vīryamiṃdra ubhe vīrye | (*) (Śa. Brā. 5-2-3-9). As described in such passages, Agni's powers in the form of radiance are described here too.

Sāyaṇa has shown that the second half of this Ṛk admits two constructions. First: the words सिंधवः (*siṃdhavaḥ*), अससंतः (*asasaṃtaḥ*) and अजराः (*ajarāḥ*) are adjectives of भात्वक्षसः (*bhātvakṣasaḥ*). The radiant powers are flowing, unbroken in their operations, and undiminished; and न रेजंते (*na rejaṃte*) is taken in negative form: they do not shrink back from their works.

Second: न शब्दो दृष्टांतवचनः । भात्वक्षसो भा एव त्वक्षो बलं यस्य तादृशस्यादित्यस्य सिंधवो न रश्मय इव । ते यथा स्यंदनशीला व्याप्तिमंतः अक्तुरति अंजकं तमः अतिक्रम्य अससंतो रेजंते तद्वत् भात्वक्षसोऽस्याग्नेरुक्तलक्षणा दीप्तयोऽपि सर्वत्र रेजंते कंपंते व्याप्नुवंतीत्यर्थः । (*na śabdo dṛṣṭāṃtavacanaḥ | bhātvakṣaso bhā eva tvakṣo balaṃ yasya tādṛśasyādityasya siṃdhavo na raśmaya iva | te yathā syaṃdanaśīlā vyāptimaṃtaḥ akturati aṃjakaṃ tamaḥ atikramya asasaṃto rejaṃte tadvat bhātvakṣaso 'syāgneruktalakṣaṇā dīptayo 'pi sarvatra rejaṃte kaṃpaṃte vyāpnuvaṃtītyarthaḥ |*). Here the word न (*na*) expresses a simile. Just as the rays of the sun, which has strength in the form of radiance, pierce the darkness, and without the least break in their duties pervade the world with a quivering motion, so too the rays of Agni pervade; this, Sāyaṇa explains, is the meaning.

##### Grammar

- तेषाः — त्रिष दीप्तौ इति धातुः । पचादिषु पाठाद् अच् प्रत्ययः । तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोर्लघूपधस्य गुणः । चित्त्वात् अंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति । **NOTE**
- अजराः — न विद्यते जरा येषां ते अजराः । नञ्स्वरं बाधित्वा नञ्योजरमः (पा. सू. ६-२-११६) इति उत्तरपदस्य अमृदात्तस्वरो भवति । **NOTE**
- अस्य — ऊडिदंपदादि (पा. सू ६-१-१७१) इति विभक्तेरुदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- भानवः — भा वीप्त धातुः । दाभाभ्यांनुः (उ. सू. २-३०-२) इति नु प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति ।
- सुसंदृशः — दृशिर् प्रेक्षणे धातुः । क्विप् प्रत्ययः । गतिकारकोपपदात् कृत् (पा. सू. ६-२-१३९) इति कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- सुप्रतीकस्य — शोभनं प्रतीकं यस्य सः सुप्रतीकः तस्य । क्रत्वादयश्च (पा. सू. ६-२-११८) इति उत्तरपदस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 306

**Grammar** (continued)
- भात्वक्षसः — भाः एव त्वक्षः बलं यस्य सः भात्वक्षाः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- रेजंते — रेज्य कंपने धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- अससंतः — न ससंतः अससंतः । षस स्वष्टे धातुः । लडर्थे शतृ प्रत्ययः । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् । तत्पुरुषे तुल्यार्थ (पा. सू. ६-२-२) इति नञ्प्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यमे॑रि॒रे भृग॑वो वि॒श्ववे॑दसं॒ नाभा॑ पृथि॒व्या भुव॑नस्य  ॒॑
> मज्मना ।  
> अ॒ग्निं तं गी॒र्भिर्हि॑नुहि॒ स्व आ दमे॒ य एको॒ वस्वो॒ ॒॑ ॑
> वरुणो न राजति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । आईरिरे । भृगवः । विश्ववेदसं । नाभा । पृथिव्याः । भुवनस्य । मज्मना ।  
> अग्निं । तं । गीःभिः । हिनुहि । स्वे । आ । दमे । यः । एकः । वस्वः । वरुणः । न ।  
> राजति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> विश्ववेदसं सर्वधनं । वेद इति धननाम वेदो वरिव इति तन्नामसु
> पाठात् । तादृशं यमग्निं भृगवो भृगुगोत्रोत्पन्नाः पापस्य भर्जकाः पृथिव्या
> वेद्याः । एकदेशे कृत्स शब्दः । यद्वा । एतावती वै पृथिवीत्यादिश्रुतेर्वेद्याः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

विश्ववेदसं (*viśvavedasaṃ*) means "possessing all wealth"; वेदः (*vedaḥ*) is a word for wealth, since "veda, variva" are read among the names of wealth. Agni, such as he is, was placed by the Bhṛgus—those born in the Bhṛgu lineage, the burners of sin—on the "navel" of the earth, that is, of the altar (the word कृत्स (*kṛtsa*) being used for a part of it). Or else, on the authority of the śruti beginning एतावती वै पृथिवी (*etāvatī vai pṛthivī*), the altar has the nature of the earth. (The sentence continues on the next page.)

### Printed p. 307

> पृथिवीत्वं । तस्या नाभावुत्तरवेद्यां भुवनस्य भूतजातस्य मज्मना बलेन निमित्तेना अभिमुख्यनेरिरे ईरितवंतः स्थापितवंतः तमग्निं स्वे स्वकीये दमे गृह
> उत्तरवेद्यां गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिरा हिनुहि । प्राप्नुहि ॥ हि गतौ वृद्धौ च । उतश्च
> प्रत्ययाच्चंदसि नावचनमिति हेर्लुगभावः ॥ योऽग्निरेको मुख्यः सन् एक
> एव वा वसो वसुनो गवादिधनस्य राजति ईश्वरो भवति । प्रदातुमिति शेषः ।
> राजतीत्यैश्वर्यकर्मा क्षियति राजतीति तन्नामसु पाठात् । तत्र दृष्टांतः । वरुणो
> न वारक आदित्य इव । स यथा सर्वस्येष्टे तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On the navel of that earth, the northern altar (uttaravedi), by the strength (मज्मना (*majmanā*)) of all beings (भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)) as the cause, the Bhṛgus set Agni before them (ईरिरे (*īrire*): they set him up, established him). "That Agni, in your own house (दमे (*dame*)), on the uttaravedi, bring (हिनुहि (*hinuhi*): reach, obtain) with songs of praise." The root हि (*hi*) means going and growth; the non-elision of the हि (*hi*) follows the rule उतश्च प्रत्ययाच्छंदसि ना वचनम् (*utaśca pratyayācchaṃdasi nā vacanam*). The Agni who, being the chief, or being alone, rules over wealth (वसो (*vaso*)), cows and the like—he is lord, to bestow it, is the supplied remainder. राजति (*rājati*) is a word of lordship, since "kṣiyati, rājati" are read among those names. The comparison: वरुणो न (*varuṇo na*) is "like the restrainer, Āditya"; as he rules over everything, so does Agni.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Agni
- वरुणो न (*varuṇo na*)—like Āditya
- एकः (*ekaḥ*)—being the sole lord
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—of all wealth such as cows
- राजति (*rājati*)—is the master
- विश्ववेवसं (*viśvavevasaṃ*)—possessing all wealth
- यं (*yaṃ*)—whom Agni
- भृगवः (*bhṛgavaḥ*)—the sages born in the lineage of Bhṛgu
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—of the altar
- नाभा (*nābhā*)—on the northern part
- भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)—of all beings
- मज्मना (*majmanā*)—by the strength
- आ (*ā*)—facing
- ईरिरे (*īrire*)—have established
- तं अग्निं (*taṃ agniṃ*)—that Agni
- स्वे दमे (*sve dame*)—in your (Agni's) own house (on the northern altar)
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises
- आ हिनुहि (*ā hinuhi*)—bring and establish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Agni who, like Āditya, is the sole lord and master of all wealth such as cows, and whom all the sages born in the lineage of Bhṛgu have established, with all their strength, on the northern part of the altar, that possessor of all wealth: bring him with praises to the northern altar of your own house and establish him.

##### Printed English

Bring to his own abode with hymns that Agni, the possessor of all riches, whom the descendants of Bhrigu placed by the strength of all beings upon the navel of the earth : for, like Veruna, he reigns sole (monarch) over (all) treasure.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk, after praising Agni as the sole lord and master of all wealth, the sacrificer is told to bring such an Agni, possessor of all wealth and established on the northern part of the altar.

### Printed p. 308

वरुणो न (*varuṇo na*)—here the word Varuṇa is explained as Āditya the restrainer. Agni's lordship is compared to the sole lordship of Āditya.

राजति (*rājati*)—"kṣiyati, rājati" and so on are read among the names of verbs of lordship (Ni. 2-21). ईश्वरः भवति (*īśvaraḥ bhavati*)—he is lord of all wealth. In the Ṛk सप्त क्षरंति शिशवे मरुत्वते पित्रे पुत्रासो अप्यवीवतन्नृतं । उभे इदस्योभयस्य राजत उभे यतेते उभयस्य पुष्यतः । (*sapta kṣaraṃti śiśave marutvate pitre putrāso apyavīvatannṛtaṃ | ubhe idasyobhayasya rājata ubhe yatete ubhayasya puṣyataḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 18-13-5) **NOTE**, the Nirukta authors show as an example that the word राजतः (*rājataḥ*) gives the sense ईशाते (*īśāte*); the same sense results here too.

भृगवः ईरिरे (*bhṛgavaḥ īrire*)—भृगुगोत्रोत्पन्नाः स्थापितवंतः (*bhṛgugotrotpannāḥ sthāpitavaṃtaḥ*): this points to the old story that the sages born of the Bhṛgu line first established Agni in the house of sacrifice. In such Taittirīya Saṃhitā passages as अयमिह प्रथमो धायि धातृभिर्होता यजिष्ठो अध्वरेष्वीड्यः । यमप्नवानो भृगवो विरुरुचुर्वनेषु चित्रं विभुवं विशेषिविशे ॥ (*ayamiha prathamo dhāyi dhātṛbhirhotā yajiṣṭho adhvareṣvīḍyaḥ | yamapnavāno bhṛgavo virurucurvaneṣu citraṃ vibhuvaṃ viśeṣiviśe ‖*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 1-5-1) **NOTE**, the matter of the Bhṛgus first establishing Agni as hotṛ is told. In the Ṛk Saṃhitā too, in Ṛks such as दधुष्टा भृगवः मानुषेष्वा रयिं न चारुं सुहवं जनेभ्यः । होतारमग्ने अतिथिं वरेण्यं मित्रं न शेवं दिव्याय जन्मने । (*dadhuṣṭā bhṛgavaḥ mānuṣeṣvā rayiṃ na cāruṃ suhavaṃ janebhyaḥ | hotāramagne atithiṃ vareṇyaṃ mitraṃ na śevaṃ divyāya janmane |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-58-6) and द्विता यदीं कीस्तासो अभिद्यवो नमस्यंत उपवोचंत भृगवो मथ्यंतो दाशा भृगवः । अग्निरीशे वसूनां शुचियोर्ण धर्णिरेषां । प्रिया अपिधीर्वनिषीष्ट मेधिर आ वनिषीष्ट मेधिरः ॥ (*dvitā yadīṃ kīstāso abhidyavo namasyaṃta upavocaṃta bhṛgavo mathyaṃto dāśā bhṛgavaḥ | agnirīśe vasūnāṃ śuciyorṇa dharṇireṣāṃ | priyā apidhīrvaniṣīṣṭa medhira ā vaniṣīṣṭa medhiraḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-127-7), it is praised in many places that the Bhṛgus churned and established Agni in the house of sacrifice, and that Mātariśvan brought Agni from a distant region for these Bhṛgus. That circumstance is hinted at in the present Ṛk also.

### Printed p. 309

भृगवः (*bhṛgavaḥ*)—भ्रष्टारो हविषां पापानां भर्जका वा (*bhraṣṭāro haviṣāṃ pāpānāṃ bharjakā vā*): either those who offer oblations, or those who destroy sins. Sāyaṇa explains that these sages have this name because the word can be understood in either way and because they had both kinds of power.

The Nirukta authors, with अर्चिषि भृगुः संबभूव भृगुर्भृज्यमानो न देहे (*arciṣi bhṛguḥ saṃbabhūva bhṛgurbhṛjyamāno na dehe*) (Ni. 3-17), show the derivation of the word Bhṛgu: Bhṛgu, born of the flame, though inside Agni, was not burnt; hence the name. This undying power helps the great sacrificial work, and likewise the destruction of sin. In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa, अथांगारैरभ्यूहति । भृगूणामंगिरसां तपसा तप्यध्वमित्येतद्वै तेजो यद्भग्वंगिरसाग् सुतप्तान्यसन्निति तस्मादेनमभ्यूहति । (*athāṃgārairabhyūhati | bhṛgūṇāmaṃgirasāṃ tapasā tapyadhvamityetadvai tejo yadbhagvaṃgirasāg sutaptānyasanniti tasmādenamabhyūhati |*) (Ka. Brā. 1-2-1-3) **NOTE**. In such passages, since those born in the Bhṛgu line are direct relations of Agni, they are said to have extremely brilliant radiance. And, citing the Taittirīya Saṃhitā passages अयमिह प्रथमो धायि धातृभिर्होता यजिष्ठो अध्वरेष्टीड्यः (*ayamiha prathamo dhāyi dhātṛbhirhotā yajiṣṭho adhvareṣṭīḍyaḥ*) and so on (Śa. Brā. 2-3-4-14) and Tai. Saṃ. (1-5-5-1); and द्वे अग्नेर्जुहोति । द्विपाद्यजमानो यजमानोऽग्निर्यावानाग्निर्यावत्यस्य मात्रा तावतैवैतद्यज्ञग्ं सग्स्थापयतीष्टो यज्ञो भृगुभिरिष्टोऽग्निराहुतः पिपर्तु न इति । (*dve agnerjuhoti | dvipādyajamāno yajamāno 'gniryāvānāgniryāvatyasya mātrā tāvataivaitadyajñagṃ sagsthāpayatīṣṭo yajño bhṛgubhiriṣṭo 'gnirāhutaḥ pipartu na iti |*) (Pa. Brā. 9-5-1-31) **NOTE**. By such passages the manner in which the Bhṛgus worshipped Agni and performed sacrifice is also explained.

पृथिव्या नाभा भुवनस्य मज्मना ईरिरे (*pṛthivyā nābhā bhuvanasya majmanā īrire*)—by this mantra the manner in which the Bhṛgus established Agni is stated. Here पृथिव्या नाभा (*pṛthivyā nābhā*) is explained as the northern altar. As it is said in Saṃhitā passages such as उत्तरं परिग्राहं परि गृह्णात्येतावती वै पृथिवी यावती वेदिस्तस्या एतावत एव । (*uttaraṃ parigrāhaṃ pari gṛhṇātyetāvatī vai pṛthivī yāvatī vedistasyā etāvata eva |*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 2-6-4-3) **NOTE**, that the earth itself is the altar, so it is explained here also. On such an altar in the form of the earth, gathering together the strength of all beings, the Bhṛgus established Agni.

### Printed p. 310

यद्वैतमत्राहरंति । यान्वै तान्सप्त पुरुषानेकं पुरुषमकुर्वन्नयमेव स योऽयमग्निश्चीयतेऽथ यामेषां तामूर्ध्वाग्ग् श्रियग्ं रसग्ं समुदौहन्नेष स यमेतमत्राग्निमाहरंति तद्यदेतमत्राहरंति यैवैतेषाग्‌ं सप्तानां पुरुषाणां श्रीर्यो रसस्तमेतदूर्ध्वग्ं समुद्वहंति तदस्यैतच्छिर आत्म्यायमग्निश्चित आत्मानमेवास्यैतत्संस्कृत्य शिरः प्रति दधाति ॥ (*yadvaitamatrāharaṃti | yānvai tānsapta puruṣānekaṃ puruṣamakurvannayameva sa yo 'yamagniścīyate 'tha yāmeṣāṃ tāmūrdhvāgg śriyagṃ rasagṃ samudauhanneṣa sa yametamatrāgnimāharaṃti tadyadetamatrāharaṃti yaivaiteṣāgṃ saptānāṃ puruṣāṇāṃ śrīryo rasastametadūrdhvagṃ samudvahaṃti tadasyaitacchira ātmyāyamagniścita ātmānamevāsyaitatsaṃskṛtya śiraḥ prati dadhāti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 9-2-3-51). By such mantras, the matter explained there is hinted here too: that Agni, installed on the altar, is the essence of the seven persons who reflect all beings.

##### Grammar

- ईरिरे — ईर गतौ प्रेरणे च धातुः । लिट्प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचने लिटस्तझयोरेशिरेच् इति इरे आदेशः । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- विश्ववेदसम् — विश्वं वेदः यस्य सः विश्ववेदाः तन् विश्ववेदसम् । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- नाभा — सुपां सुलुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-३९) इति विभक्तेः आकारादेशः ।
- मज्मना — टुमस् जो शुद्ध धातुः । औणादिको मनि प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति । तृतीयैकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- गीर्भिः — गृ शब्दे धातुः । क्विप् च इति क्विप् प्रत्ययः । ऋत इद्दातोः (पा. सू. ७-१-१००) इति धातोरित्वं, गिर् शब्दो भवति । भिस् परे हलि च इति धातोरुपधाया दीर्घः ।
- हिनुहि — हि गतौ वृद्भौ च धातुः । लोट्मध्यमपुरुषैकवचने सिपो हि आदेशः । स्वादिभ्य श्नुः इति श्नुविकरणम् । उतश्च प्रत्ययाच्छंदसि ना वचनम् (पा. सू. ६-४-१०६-१) इति हेर्लुक् न भवति । विकरणस्य सार्वधातुकमपित् इति चित्त्वात् तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोर्गुणो न भवति । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- वस्वः — वसु शब्दः । अस्मात् षष्ठीविभक्तौ परतः जसादिषु छंदसि ना वचनम् इति वचनात् नुम् । गुणादयो न भवंति । यणादेशः ।

### Printed p. 311

##### Grammar

- राजति — राज धातुः ऐश्वर्य क्रियावाची । क्षयति राजति (निरु. २-२१-४) इति पठितम् । लट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । यः इति यच्छब्दसंबंधात् यद्वृत्तान्वित्यम् (पा. सू. ८-१-६६) इति निघातप्रतिषेधो भवति । शप् तिप् पित्तात् धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते ।

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न यो वरा॑य म॒रुता॑मिव स्व॒नः सेने॑व सृ॒ष्टा दि॒व्या  ॒॑
> यथाशनिः ।  
> अ॒ग्निर्जंभै॑स्तिगि॒तैर॑त्ति॒ भर्व॑ति यो॒धो न शत्रु॒न्त्स वना॒  ॑
> न्यृंजते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । यः । वराय । मरुतांइव । स्वनः । सेनाइव । सृष्टा । दिव्या । यथा । अशनिः ।  
> अग्निः । जंभैः । तिगितैः । अत्ति । भर्वति । योधः । न शत्रून् । सः । वना । नि । ऋंजते ।  
> ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> योऽग्निर्वराय वरणाय निग्रहाय न शक्तो न भवति । तत्र दृष्टांतत्रयमुच्यते । मरुतां स्वन इव । स यथाग्राह्यस्तद्वत् । तथा सृष्टा वैरिक्षयार्थं
> प्रबलेनातिसृष्टा सेनेव । सा यथानैरनिरोध्या तद्यत् । तथा दिव्या दिवि भवाशनिर्यथा पतत्येव न निवार्यते तद्वत् । ईदृक् सामर्थ्यमस्तीति दर्शयति । अयमग्निस्तिगितैर्निशितैस्तीक्ष्णीभूतैः ॥ अंत्यविकारश्चांदसः ॥ जंभैर्दंतैर्दंतस्थानीयाभिर्ज्वालाभिरत्ति । अस्मद्विरोधिनो भक्षयति । तथा भर्वति । हिनस्ति ॥ भर्व हिंसायां ॥
> यास्कस्वाह भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मेति । नि ९-२३ । यद्यप्यत्तिभर्वत्योरदनमेवार्थः
> तथापि तदवांतरभेदोऽवगंतव्यः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । योधो न संप्रहर्ता शूर

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Agni who is not able to be checked (वराय (*varāya*) = वरणाय (*varaṇāya*), निग्रहाय (*nigrahāya*), "for restraining"). Three illustrations are given. He is like the roar of the Maruts, which cannot be seized; like an army let loose with great force for the destruction of enemies, which cannot be obstructed; and like the heavenly thunderbolt, which, as it falls, is not warded off. Thus the verse shows his might. This Agni, with sharpened (तिगितैः (*tigitaiḥ*) = *niśitaiḥ*, made keen; the change of the final is Vedic) जंभैः (*jaṃbhaiḥ*), that is, with flames that stand in the place of teeth, eats (अत्ति (*atti*)), that is, devours our adversaries. Likewise भर्वति (*bharvati*), he destroys (root भर्व हिंसायां (*bharva hiṃsāyāṃ*)). Yāska says भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मेति (*bharvatirattikarmeti*) (Ni. 9-23). Although eating is the meaning of both अत्ति (*atti*) and भर्वति (*bharvati*), a difference between them should be understood from their inner sense. The illustration: like a warrior (योधः (*yodhaḥ*)), a fighter, a hero—

### Printed p. 312

> इव । स यथा शत्रुन्भर्वति भक्षयति तद्वत् । किंच सोग्निर्वना वनानि वृक्षादिसमूहान् न्यृंजते । नितरां प्रसाधयति । दहतीत्यर्थः । ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मेति
> यास्कः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

—who devours (भर्वति (*bharvati*)) his enemies, so does Agni. Moreover that Agni न्यृंजते (*nyṛṃjate*) the वना (*vanā*), the masses of trees and the like: he thoroughly "sets them in order", that is, burns them. Yāska: ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मेति (*ṛṃjatiḥ prasādhanakarmeti*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—that very Agni
- मरुतां (*marutāṃ*)—of the Maruts
- स्वनः इव (*svanaḥ iva*)—like the roar that cannot be obstructed
- सृष्टा (*sṛṣṭā*)—made ready (for the destruction of enemies)
- सेना इव (*senā iva*)—like a powerful army
- दिव्या (*divyā*)—born in the world of heaven
- अशनिः यथा (*aśaniḥ yathā*)—like the thunderbolt that cannot be stopped
- वराय न (*varāya na*)—that which cannot be obstructed (having an unimpeded spread)
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—this Agni
- तिगितैः (*tigitaiḥ*)—sharp
- जंभैः (*jaṃbhaiḥ*)—with flames that are like teeth
- योधः (*yodhaḥ*)—a valiant warrior
- शत्रून् न (*śatrūn na*)—as he harms and destroys enemies
- अत्ति (*atti*)—devours (our adversaries)
- भर्वति (*bharvati*)—harms and destroys
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Agni
- वना (*vanā*)—the trees and so on of the forest
- न्यृंजते (*nyṛṃjate*)—burns completely

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As the roar of the Maruts cannot be obstructed, as a powerful army made ready for destroying enemies is hard to subdue, and as the thunderbolt born in the heavenly world cannot be stopped, so the spreading of this Agni too is unimpeded. As a valiant warrior harms and destroys his enemies, so this Agni, with his sharp flames like teeth, harms and destroys our adversaries. And he completely consumes the trees and the like of the forest. **NOTE**

##### Printed English

Agni, who, like the roaring of the winds, like a victorious host, like the thunderbolt in heaven, is not to be arrested, devours and destroys (our foes) with sharpened teeth, and, as a warrior (annihilates his enemies) he, (Agni), lays waste the woods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the flame of Agni, spreading unimpeded, is compared to the roar of the Maruts, to the speed of a powerful army, and to the thunderbolt that cannot be resisted. And as a valiant warrior harms and destroys his enemies, Agni too devours trees and the like. This is a description that is in one way terrifying and in another attractive. The same description may be noted in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, where it is said in an equally attractive manner.

### Printed p. 313

तव भ्रमास आशुयापतंत्यनु स्पृश धृषता शोशुचानः । तपूगंष्यग्ने जुह्वा पतंगानसंदितो विसृज विश्वगुल्काः । (*tava bhramāsa āśuyāpataṃtyanu spṛśa dhṛṣatā śośucānaḥ | tapūgaṃṣyagne juhvā pataṃgānasaṃdito visṛja viśvagulkāḥ |*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-2-13)

ऋतावा धर्ता कृष्टीनामुत मध्य इद्दः । साध्वीमकर्देवनीतिं नो अद्य यज्ञस्य जिह्वामविदाम गुह्यां । स आयुरागात्सुरभिर्वसानो भद्रामकर्देवहूतिं नो अद्य ! अक्रंददग्निस्तनयन्निव द्यौः क्षामा रेरिहद्वीरुधस्समंजन् ! सद्यो जज्ञानो वि हीमिद्दो अख्यदा रोदसी भानुना भात्यंतः । (*ṛtāvā dhartā kṛṣṭīnāmuta madhya iddaḥ | sādhvīmakardevanītiṃ no adya yajñasya jihvāmavidāma guhyāṃ | sa āyurāgātsurabhirvasāno bhadrāmakardevahūtiṃ no adya ! akraṃdadagnistanayanniva dyauḥ kṣāmā rerihadvīrudhassamaṃjan ! sadyo jajñāno vi hīmiddo akhyadā rodasī bhānunā bhātyaṃtaḥ |*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-14-2) **NOTE**

सश्वितानस्तन्यतूरोचनस्था अजरेभिर्नादद्धिर्यविष्ठः । यः पावकः पुरुतमः पुरूणि पृथून्यग्निरनुयाति भर्वन् । (*saśvitānastanyatūrocanasthā ajarebhirnādaddhiryaviṣṭhaḥ | yaḥ pāvakaḥ purutamaḥ purūṇi pṛthūnyagniranuyāti bharvan |*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-14-4)

In such passages the description—that Agni sounds like the thunder of a cloud in the atmosphere, spreads a flame like a bright garment, devours the trees and creepers met on his way, and pervades the whole atmosphere with his trembling flames as he spreads—shows the form of Agni, both attractive and at the same time terrifying, just as it is described in the present Ṛk.

वराय (*varāya*)—वरणाय (*varaṇāya*), निग्रहाय (*nigrahāya*) (न (*na*)): the meaning is "to stop, to restrain". This word, derived both from the root वृञ् (*vṛñ*) (वरणे (*varaṇe*)), which gives the meaning "to desire, to choose", and from the root वृञ् (*vṛñ*) (आवरणे (*āvaraṇe*)), which gives the meaning "to hold back, to conceal", is used in both senses.

यस्ता चकार स कुह स्विदिंद्रः कमा जनं चरति कासु विक्षु । कस्ते यज्ञो मनसे शं वराय को अर्क इंद्र कतमः स होता ॥ (*yastā cakāra sa kuha svidiṃdraḥ kamā janaṃ carati kāsu vikṣu | kaste yajño manase śaṃ varāya ko arka iṃdra katamaḥ sa hotā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-21-4)

In this Ṛk वराय तव वरणाय कः कीवृशः अर्कः मंत्रः समर्थो भवति (*varāya tava varaṇāya kaḥ kīvṛśaḥ arkaḥ maṃtraḥ samartho bhavati*): which mantra is capable of choosing you, so that you become gracious—the root वृञ् (*vṛñ*) here gives the meaning "choose, select". Similarly in Ṛk 6-44-21 वराय (*varāya*) means श्रेष्ठाय (*śreṣṭhāya*), "excellent, the best".

इषा मंदस्वादु तेऽरं वराय मन्यवे । भुवत्त इंद्र शं हृदे ॥ (*iṣā maṃdasvādu te 'raṃ varāya manyave | bhuvatta iṃdra śaṃ hṛde ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-2) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-82-3) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 314

In such Ṛks वराय शत्रुनिवारकाय मन्यवे क्रोधाय (*varāya śatrunivārakāya manyave krodhāya*) gives the meaning "repelling, holding back". So in the present Ṛk too it gives the meaning "that which cannot be repelled or held back". अशनिः यथा (*aśaniḥ yathā*): as nobody can stop the speed of the thunderbolt, and as the thunderbolt must strike once it is discharged, so is the spread of Agni; thus his power is shown.

तिगितैः (*tigitaiḥ*)—निशितैस्तीक्षैः (*niśitaistīkṣaiḥ*): the meaning is "very sharp". Ordinarily this word is an adjective of a weapon. For example:

यो नः सनुत्य उत ना जिघत्रुरभिख्याय तं तिगितेन विध्य । बृहस्पत आयुधैर्जेषि शत्रून्हुहे रीषंतं परि धेहि राजन् ॥ (*yo naḥ sanutya uta nā jighatrurabhikhyāya taṃ tigitena vidhya | bṛhaspata āyudhairjeṣi śatrūnhuhe rīṣaṃtaṃ pari dhehi rājan ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-30-9)

In this Ṛk it indicates the nature of the weapon, "with a sharp weapon". The adjective is also fitting in the present Ṛk, because it qualifies the word जंभैः (*jaṃbhaiḥ*) and because a tusk is sharp like a weapon, so that the flames of Agni are compared to such tusks as being sharp.

भर्वति (*bharvati*)—आवयति (*āvayati*), भर्वति (*bharvati*) and so on are read among the names of the action of eating (Ni. 3-8) **NOTE**. The meaning is "devours". To give the same sense, the word भर्वन् (*bharvan*) is used in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā passage quoted above (पृथून्यग्निरनुयाति भर्वन् (*pṛthūnyagniranuyāti bharvan*), 1-3-14-4) as well. The purport is that the flame of Agni devours all creepers and trees.

##### Grammar

- वराय — व्रियते इति वर्ण वृञ् वरणे धातुः । ग्रहवृदृसिश्चिगमश्च (पा. सू. ३-३-५८) इति अप् प्रत्ययः । तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोः इकः गुणः । रपरत्वेन भवति । प्रत्ययः पित्तात् धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते । चतुर्थी एकवचनांतरूपम् । **NOTE**
- सृष्टा — सृज विसर्गे धातुः । कर्मणि निष्ठा इति क्त प्रत्ययः । व्रश्चभृष्ट— इति धातुजकारस्य षत्वम् । ष्टुत्वेन प्रत्ययतकारस्य टित्वम् । स्त्रीत्वविवक्षायां अदंतनिबंधनः टाप् ।
- दिव्या — दिवि भवा दिव्या । दंडादिगणपठितत्वात् (पा. सू. ५-१-६६) य प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति । स्त्रीत्वे अदंतसिबंधनः टाप् ।
- अशनिः — अश भोजने धातुः । आर्तिसृधृ— (उ. सू. २-२५९) इति अनि प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरेण मध्योदात्तं पदं भवति ।

### Printed p. 315

- तिगितैः — तिज निशाने (तीक्ष्णीकरण) धातुः । क्त प्रत्ययः । छांदसत्वेन धातोः अंत्यस्य कुत्वम् । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तो भवति । तृतीया बहुवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतपरत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- अत्ति — आद भक्षणे धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतपरत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- भर्वति — भर्व हिंसायां धातुः । यास्कैः भर्वतिरत्तिकर्मा (निरु. ९-२३) इति उक्तम् । लट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतपरत्वात् निघातस्वरो न भवति । शप् तिप् पित्तात् धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते ।
- योधः — युध संप्रहारे धातुः । नंदिग्रहपचादि— (पा. सू. ३-१-१३४) इति कर्तरि अच् प्रत्ययः । तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोः लघूपधस्य गुणः । चितः इति अंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- वना — शसि शि आदेशे सति रूपे शेश्छंदसि बहुलम्— (पा. सू. ६-१-७०) इति शेः लोपः ।
- ऋञ्चिते — ऋञ्चित धातुः प्रसाधनक्रियावाची प्रयुक्तः । लट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतपरत्वात् निघातस्वरो भवति ।

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कु॒विन्नो॑ अ॒ग्निरु॒चथ॑स्य॒ वीरस॒द्वसु॑ष्कु॒विद्वसु॑भिः॒ काम॑मा॒वर॑त् ।  
> चो॒दः कु॒वित्तु॑तु॒ज्जात्सा॒तये॒ धियः॒ शुचि॑प्रतीकं॒ तम॒या ॒ ॑
> धिया गृणे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कुवित् । नः । अग्निः । उचथस्य । वीः । असत् । वसुः । कुवित् । वसुभिः । कामं ।  
> आवरत् ।

### Printed p. 316

> चोदः । कुवित् । तुतुज्यात् । सातये । धियः । शुचिप्रतीकं । तं । अया । धिया ।  
> गृणे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमग्निर्नोऽस्माकमुचथस्योक्तस्य स्तोत्रस्य कुविद्दहुवारं वी कामयितासत् । भवतु ॥ अस्तेर्लेट्यडागमः ॥ यद्वा । उचथस्यॆतन्नामकस्य महर्षेर्गॊत्रप्रभवस्य न इति संबंधः । तथा वसुर्वासयिता सर्वॆषां वसुस्थानीयो
> ना वसुभिर्वासयितृभिर्धनैः काममत्यर्थमभिमतं वा कुविदतिप्रभूतमावरत् ।
> आवृणोतु । अभिमतप्रदानेन कामं निवर्तयत्वित्यर्थः ॥ वृणोतेर्लेट्यडागमः ।
> छांदसो विकरणस्य लुक् ॥ अयमग्निश्चोदोऽस्माकं कर्मसु प्रेरकः सन् धियः
> कर्माणि सातये लाभाय कुविद्दहु तुतुज्यात् । त्वरयतु । प्रेरयत्वित्यर्थः ॥
> तुजिः प्रेरणार्थः । छांदसः शपः श्लुः ॥ शुचिप्रतीकं शोभनावयवं शोभनज्वालं
> तमग्निमया धियानया स्तुतिरूपया प्रज्ञया गृणे । उच्चारयामि ।
> सौमीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Agni, for our उचथस्य (*ucathasya*), our uttered praise, कुवित् (*kuvit*) (many times) may be वी (*vī*), a desirer; "let it be" (असत् (*asat*) is the Lēṭ form of अस् (*as*) with the augment *aṭ*). Or else: उचथस्य (*ucathasya*) is of the great seer of that name, and the connection is "of us, who are born in his gotra". Likewise वसुः (*vasuḥ*), the one who makes dwell, who stands for all as wealth, may आवरत् (*āvarat*) (fulfil) कामं (*kāmaṃ*), our desire, that is, what is greatly wished, कुवित् (*kuvit*) in great abundance, with वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*), wealth that provides dwelling; that is, may he turn our desire back (satisfy it) by granting what is wished (Lēṭ of *vṛṇoti* with *aṭ*; Vedic dropping of the stem-vowel). This Agni, चोदः (*codaḥ*), as the impeller in our rites, may तुतुज्यात् (*tutujyāt*) (hasten, impel) धियः (*dhiyaḥ*), our rites, सातये (*sātaye*) for gain, कुवित् (*kuvit*) abundantly (the root तुजि (*tuji*) means impelling; Vedic श्लु (*ślu*) in place of शप् (*śap*)). शुचिप्रतीकं (*śucipratīkaṃ*) is "of beautiful limbs, of beautiful flame"; that Agni I गृणे (*gṛṇe*), utter, that is, praise, with अया धिया (*ayā dhiyā*), this intellect in the form of praise.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- उचथस्य (*ucathasya*)—praise
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—always
- वीः (*vīḥ*)—as one who desires
- असत् (*asat*)—may he be
- वसुः (*vasuḥ*)—Agni, the giver of wealth or the dwelling-place (of all)
- वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*)—with riches
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—our desired object
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—in great abundance
- अवरत् (*avarat*)—may he fill and satisfy
- चोदः (*codaḥ*)—as the impeller (of us in our rites)
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—our sacrificial rites (to perform)
- सातये (*sātaye*)—for the gain of the fruit
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—greatly
- तुतुज्यात् (*tutujyāt*)—may he impel and cause them to be accomplished
- शुचिप्रतीकं (*śucipratīkaṃ*)—having radiant flames
- तं (*taṃ*)—Agni
- अया धिया (*ayā dhiyā*)—with this intellect (meditation) in the form of praise
- गृणे (*gṛṇe*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Agni always desire our praises. May Agni, the giver of wealth, fill and satisfy our desired object abundantly with all riches. Being our impeller in our rites, may he impel us to accomplish the sacrifice for the fruit of the sacrifice. I praise Agni of radiant flames with this meditation in the form of praise.

##### Printed English

May Agni be ever desirous of our praise ; may the giver of wealth satisfy our utmost expectation with riches; may the inspirer (of our devotion)

### Printed p. 317

hasten our rites fruition. I glorify him, the radiant-limbed (Agni), with this laudation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

उचथस्य (*ucathasya*)—उकथस्य स्तोत्रस्य (*ukathasya stotrasya*). Or यद्वा एतन्नामकस्य महर्षेर्गॊत्रप्रभवस्य नः। (*yadvā etannāmakasya maharṣergòtraprabhavasya naḥ|*). Sāyaṇa has explained that it means either "of the praise" or "of us, born in the family of the great seer named Ucatha". Here the first meaning fits better.

कुवित् (*kuvit*)—this word is used repeatedly in this very Ṛk, in two senses. For the first occurrence he has given बहुवारं (*bahuvāraṃ*), "many times", that is, "always"; for the second अतिप्रभूतं (*atiprabhūtaṃ*). For the third the sense is also "abundant". The Nirukta authors have read कुवित् (*kuvit*) among the names for "much" (Ni. 3-13).

उपेमसृक्षि वाजयुर्वचस्यां चनो दधीत नाद्यो गिरो मे । अपां नपादाशुहेमा कुवित्स सुपेशसस्करिति जोषिसद्धि ॥ (*upemasṛkṣi vājayurvacasyāṃ cano dadhīta nādyo giro me | apāṃ napādāśuhemā kuvitsa supeśasaskariti joṣisaddhi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-5-2)

In such Ṛks the meaning is बहुलं (*bahulaṃ*), "abundantly"; and in the Ṛk quoted by the Nirukta authors—

ता आ रुद्रस्य मीळ्हुषो विवासे कुविन्नसंते मरुतः पुनर्नः। यत्सस्वर्ता जिहीळिरे यदाविरव तदेन ईमहे तुराणां ॥ (*tā ā rudrasya mīḷhuṣo vivāse kuvinnasaṃte marutaḥ punarnaḥ| yatsasvartā jihīḷire yadāvirava tadena īmahe turāṇāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-58-5)

in such Ṛks the meaning is बहुकृत्वः (*bahukṛtvaḥ*), पुनःपुनः (*punaḥpunaḥ*), "many times, again and again".

धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—कर्माणि (*karmāṇi*): "(to perform) our sacrificial rites" is the meaning. In the Nirukta this word is read both among the names of action (Ni. 3-1) and among the names of intelligence. Quoting the Ṛk

महो अर्णः सरस्वती प्रचेतयति केतुना । धियो विश्वा विराजति ॥ (*maho arṇaḥ sarasvatī pracetayati ketunā | dhiyo viśvā virājati ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-3-12)

the Nirukta authors explain सर्वाणि प्रज्ञानानि (*sarvāṇi prajñānāni*) (Ni. 11-27) in the sense of all our meditations endowed with intelligence; and in the Ṛks

फथस्पथः परिपतिं वचस्या कामेन कृतो अभ्यानळर्कं । स नो रासच्चुरुधश्चंद्राग्रा धियंधियं सीषधाति प्र पूषा ॥ (*phathaspathaḥ paripatiṃ vacasyā kāmena kṛto abhyānaḷarkaṃ | sa no rāsaccurudhaścaṃdrāgrā dhiyaṃdhiyaṃ sīṣadhāti pra pūṣā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-49-8)

पावीरवी तन्यतुरेकपादजो दिवो धर्ता सिंधुरापः समुद्रियः । विश्वे देवासः शृणवन् वचांसि मे सरस्वती सह थीभिः पुरंध्या । (*pāvīravī tanyaturekapādajo divo dhartā siṃdhurāpaḥ samudriyaḥ | viśve devāsaḥ śṛṇavan vacāṃsi me sarasvatī saha thībhiḥ puraṃdhyā |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-65-8 **NOTE**) **NOTE**

they have explained it as rites such as sacrifices and offerings.

### Printed p. 318
The remainder of the Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 6 stands here.

शुच्रिपतीकं (*śucripatīkaṃ*)—having a beautiful flame, having beautiful limbs; the meaning is Agni, who is both delightful and excellent. Among the epithets that belong to Agni this is one of the chief ones.
तुतुज्यात् (*tutujyāt*)—let him urge, let him impel. The purport is: let him impel us and bring our rite to completion.

##### Grammar

- वीः — वी गत्यादिषु धातुः । कर्तरि क्विप्‌च इति क्विप् प्रत्ययः । धातुस्वरेण उदात्तं भवति ।
- असत् — अस भुवि धातुः । लेट् प्रथमपुरुषएकवचने तिप् प्रत्ययः । इतश्च लोपः (पा सू ३-४-९७) इति इकारलोपः । लेटोडाट् (पा. सू. ३-४-९९) इति तस्य अडागमः । असत् इति रूपं भवति ।
- वसुः — वस निवासे धातुः । श्यस्वस्निहि (उ. सू. १-१०) इति उ प्रत्ययः । व्यत्ययेन आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- आ वरत् — वृञ् वरणे धातुः । आज् उपसर्गः । लेट् प्रथमपुरुषएकवचने पूर्ववत् इकारलोपः तस्य अडागमः । बहुलं छंदसि इति विकरणस्य लुक् । प्रत्ययनिमित्ते धातोः इकि गुणः । रपरत्वेन भवति । वरत् इति रूपं भवति । निपातैरद्यदि (पा सू. ८-१-३०) इति अत्र कुनित् इत्यस्य संबंधत्वात् निघातप्रतिषेधः भवति । प्रत्ययस्य पित्त्वात् धातुस्वरः शिष्यते । असत् इत्यत्रापि एवमेव स्वरः भवति । तिजिचोदात्तवति इति गतेः (आ) अनुदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- चोदः — चुद प्रेरणे धातुः । नंदिग्रहपचा इति अच् प्रत्ययः । तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोः लघूपधस्य गुणः । चितः इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- तुतुज्यात् — तुज प्रेरणे धातुः । लिज् प्रथमपुरुषएकवचने ति प्रत्ययः । इतश्च इति इकारलोपः । यासुट् पर— इति तस्य यासुटागमः । बहुलं छंदसि इति श्लु विकरणम् । तं निमित्तीकृत्य धातोः द्वित्वादि । पूर्ववत् कुनित् योगस्य सत्त्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । प्रत्ययस्य अकारः उदात्तः भवति ।
- सातये — ऊतिजूति— इति उदात्तेन क्तिन् प्रत्ययेन निसातितम् ।
- शुचिप्रतीकम् — शुचिः प्रतीकम् यस्य सः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१) इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति । द्वितीयाविकवचनांतरूपम् ।

### Printed p. 319
- धिया — तृतीयाविकवचनांतरूपम् । सावेकाचस्तृतीयादिः इति विभक्तेः उदात्तस्वरः भवति ।

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> घृ॒तप्र॑तीकं न ऋ॒तस्य॑ धू॒र्षद॑म॒ग्निं मि॒त्रं न स॑मिधा॒न  ॑
> ऋंजते ।  
> इंधा॑नो अ॒क्रो वि॒दथे॑षु॒ दीद्य॑च्चु॒क्रव॑र्णा॒मुदु॑ नो ॒ ॑
> यंसते धियं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> घृतप्रतीकं । वः । ऋतस्य । धू-सदं । अग्निं । मित्रं । न । संइधानः । यंजते ।  
> इंधानः । अक्रः । विदथेषु । दीद्यत् । शुक्रवर्णां । उत् । ऊं इति । नः । यंसते । धियं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> धृतप्रतीकं धृतोपक्रमं प्रयोजादिष्वाज्यैर्हूयमानत्वात् । यद्वा ।
> प्रतीकमंगं । दीप्तज्वालमित्यर्थः । किंच वो युष्मत्संबंधिन ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य
> धूर्षदं धुरि निर्वहणे सीदंतं यज्ञनिर्वाहकमग्निं मित्रं न मित्रमिव समिधान
> इद्धैर्दिपयमान ऋंजते । प्रसाधयति । ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मा । इंधानः
> सम्यग्दीप्यमानोऽक्रो ज्वालासमिदादिभिराक्रांतोऽन्यैरनाक्रांतो वा ॥ क्रमेश्कांदसो डः ॥ विदथेषु यज्ञेषु वेदयत्तु स्तोत्रेषु निमित्तभूतेषु दीद्यत्
> स्वयं दीप्यमानोऽस्मदीयां धियं प्रज्ञानं यागादिविषयां शुक्रवर्णां शुभ्रवर्णां निर्मलां ज्योतिष्टोमादि कर्म वोदु यंसते । उद्योजयत्येव ॥
> यमेर्लॆट्यडागमः । सिप् ॥ उशब्दोऽवधारणे । धीरिति कर्मनाम । धीः शमीति
> तन्नामसु पाठात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

धृतप्रतीकं (*dhṛtapratīkaṃ*) means "whose beginning is ghee", since in the prayājas and other rites he is offered oblations of ghee; or else प्रतीकं (*pratīkaṃ*) is the limb, so the sense is "of blazing flame". Further, वः (*vaḥ*) is "belonging to you"; ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*) is "of the sacrifice". He is धूर्षदं (*dhūrṣadaṃ*), one who sits (settles) at the yoke in the carrying-through, that is, the accomplisher of the sacrifice. Agni, मित्रं न (*mitraṃ na*) like a friend, the kindler समिधान (*samidhāna*), kindling with fuel, ऋंजते (*ṛṃjate*) brings to perfection; the root ऋंजति (*ṛṃjati*) has the sense of accomplishing. इंधानः (*iṃdhānaḥ*) is the one well kindled; अक्रः (*akraḥ*) is the one assailed (filled) by flames, fuel and so on, or one not assailed by others. विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*) means in sacrifices; he explains the word as that which makes known, in praises that are the occasion. दीद्यत् (*dīdyat*): himself shining, धियं (*dhiyaṃ*) our understanding concerned with sacrifice, शुक्रवर्णां (*śukravarṇāṃ*) of bright, pure colour, that is the pure rite such as Jyotiṣṭoma, उदु यंसते (*udu yaṃsate*) he indeed brings up (carries to completion). The उ (*u*) is a particle of emphasis. धीः (*dhīḥ*) is a name for rite, since it is read among the names of rite (धीः शमी (*dhīḥ śamī*)).

### Printed p. 320

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समिधानः (*samidhānaḥ*)—the priest who makes Agni blaze (with fuel)
- धृतप्रतीकं (*dhṛtapratīkaṃ*)—glistening with the offering of ghee
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the sacrifice
- धूर्षदं (*dhūrṣadaṃ*)—who takes the foremost place and carries it through
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- मित्रं न (*mitraṃ na*)—like a friend
- ऋंजतॆ (*ṛṃjatè*)—adorns and worships
- इंधानः (*iṃdhānaḥ*)—the kindled one
- अक्रः (*akraḥ*)—the one filled (with fuel and so on)
- विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—in sacrifices
- दीद्यत् (*dīdyat*)—shining (of himself)
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—understanding (concerned with sacrifice)
- शुक्रवर्णां (*śukravarṇāṃ*)—the pure rite such as Jyotiṣṭoma
- उदु यंसते (*udu yaṃsate*)—surely brings to completion and makes bright

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The priest adorns and worships Agni, who glistens with the offering of ghee and bears and accomplishes the burden of the sacrifice, as one would a friend. Agni, kindled, filled with fuel and the like, and shining in the sacrifices, surely brings to completion and makes bright our understanding concerned with sacrifice and also the pure rite such as Jyotiṣṭoma.

##### Printed English

The kindler (of the sacrificial fire) propitiates Agni, of glistening form; the upholder of your ceremony, like a friend ; well kindled and well supplied (with fuel); blazing brightly at holy rites, he illumines our pure and pious observances.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

समिधानः (*samidhānaḥ*)—इध्मैर्दीपयमानः (*idhmairdīpayamānaḥ*). "One who makes (Agni) shine with fuel": it is an epithet of the priest. Ordinarily this word, elsewhere everywhere, as an epithet of Agni gives the passive sense. For example:
स नो रेवत्समिधानः स्वस्तये संददस्वान्रयिमस्मासु दीदिहि । आ नः कृणुष्व सुविताय रोदसी अग्ने हव्या मनुषो देव वीतये ॥ (*sa no revatsamidhānaḥ svastaye saṃdadasvānrayimasmāsu dīdihi | ā naḥ kṛṇuṣva suvitāya rodasī agne havyā manuṣo deva vītaye ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-2-6)
In this Ṛk, and likewise in Ṛks 3-4-11; 5-8-4; 5-8-6 and so on, it is an epithet of Agni in the sense अस्माभिः सम्यगिध्यमानः सन् (*asmābhiḥ samyagidhyamānaḥ san*), "being well kindled by us, the priests". But in the present Ṛk alone it gives the active sense and is an epithet of the priest.

### Printed p. 321
धूर्षदं (*dhūrṣadaṃ*)—धुरि निर्वहणे । सीदंतं यज्ञनिर्वाहकं अग्निं । (*dhuri nirvahaṇe | sīdaṃtaṃ yajñanirvāhakaṃ agniṃ |*) "Capable in the work of accomplishing the sacrifice": this epithet shows Agni's power and his chief part in the sacrificial work. It gives, in each setting, a meaning suited to the context.
यज्ञेन वर्धत जातवेदसमग्निं यजध्वं हविषा तना गिरा । समिधानं सुप्रयसं स्वर्णरं द्युक्षं होतारं वृजनेषु धूर्षदं ॥ (*yajñena vardhata jātavedasamagniṃ yajadhvaṃ haviṣā tanā girā | samidhānaṃ suprayasaṃ svarṇaraṃ dyukṣaṃ hotāraṃ vṛjaneṣu dhūrṣadaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-2-1)
In this Ṛk the meaning is बलस्यकर्तारमित्यर्थः । अग्निपाटवे सति शारीरं बलं भवतीति प्रसिद्धं । (*balasyakartāramityarthaḥ | agnipāṭave sati śārīraṃ balaṃ bhavatīti prasiddhaṃ |*); as producer of strength, it shows Agni's power. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-132-7, धूर्षदं धुर्यश्वयोर्वहनदेशे सीवंतं । (*dhūrṣadaṃ dhuryaśvayorvahanadeśe sīvaṃtaṃ |*) it is an epithet of the chariot, "resting on the necks, the place where the horses bear the yoke". In the present Ṛk the meaning is "one capable of carrying through".

धृतप्रतीकं (*dhṛtapratīkaṃ*)—Although the word प्रतीक (*pratīka*) ordinarily is a synonym of "face", here it means limb. प्रतीकमंगं दीप्तज्वालमित्यर्थः (*pratīkamaṃgaṃ dīptajvālamityarthaḥ*): Agni with a shining flame. The epithet is there because he shines from the offering of ghee.
The word धृत (*dhṛta*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24); the Ṛks in which the word is used in the sense of water are cited by the Nirukta-writers, and this will be told in the respective contexts.

अक्रः (*akraḥ*)—ज्वालासमिदादिभिराक्रांतः, अन्यैः अनाक्रांतः । (*jvālāsamidādibhirākrāṃtaḥ, anyaiḥ anākrāṃtaḥ |*) One pervaded by flames, fuel and so on; or, because his spread is unobstructed, one not subject to assault by other things. The Nirukta-writers say अक्र आक्रमणात् (*akra ākramaṇāt*) (Ni. 6-17): the rampart is called अक्र (*akra*) because enemies come and assail it, and
अक्रो न बभ्रिः समिथे महीनां दिवृक्षेयः सूनवे भायजीकः । उदुस्रिया जनिता यो जजानापां गर्भो नृतमो यहो अग्निः ॥ (*akro na babhriḥ samithe mahīnāṃ divṛkṣeyaḥ sūnave bhāyajīkaḥ | udusriyā janitā yo jajānāpāṃ garbho nṛtamo yaho agniḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-1-12)
they cite this Ṛk and compare Agni's power to the power of a rampart. Or, as Sāyaṇa explains, if the rampart is called अक्र (*akra*) because others cannot assail it, the purport is, as said above, a spreading joined with Agni's unobstructed power.

विदथेषु (*vidatheṣu*)—यज्ञेषु (*yajñeṣu*). "In sacrifices", that is, in sacrificial places. By वेदयत्तु स्तोत्रेषु निमित्तभूतेषु (*vedayattu stotreṣu nimittabhūteṣu*) he shows the explanation of the word विदथ (*vidatha*). Here, of the Nirukta-writers'

### Printed p. 322
explanations, only one is followed. It is read among the names of sacrifice, यज्ञः, वेनः (*yajñaḥ, venaḥ*) and so on (Ni. 3-19), and विदथानि वेदनानि (*vidathāni vedanāni*), to show that the word विदथ (*vidatha*) means knowledge (Ni. 6-7).
होता देवो अमर्त्यः पुरस्तादेति मायया । विवथानि प्रचोदयन् ॥ (*hotā devo amartyaḥ purastādeti māyayā | vivathāni pracodayan ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-28-7)
Citing this Ṛk, they show that it is formed from the root विद (*vida*) meaning knowledge. Here Sāyaṇa has explained विदथानि वेदितव्यानि कर्माणि (*vidathāni veditavyāni karmāṇi*), "rites to be known". Some scholars have tried to state a definite meaning of this word, and all have given their personal opinions. Since each has shown a different derivation and made a different meaning, it is understood that all those meanings fit according to context. The scholar Max Muller, in explaining the Ṛk
अमादेषां भियसा भूमिरेजति नौर्न पूर्णा क्षरति व्यथिर्यती । दूरेदृशो ये चितयंत एमभिरंतर्महे विवथे येतिरे नरः । (*amādeṣāṃ bhiyasā bhūmirejati naurna pūrṇā kṣarati vyathiryatī | dūredṛśo ye citayaṃta emabhiraṃtarmahe vivathe yetire naraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-29-2)
explained the word विदथे (*vidathe*) as "(sacrifice) assembly (the great sacrificial assembly)". And, giving the explanation, after the meanings other German scholars have given for this word and their opinions on its root, he finally says: "Vidatha may have been the name of a sacred act, as Veda is of sacred knowledge. But the fact remains that it is best translated by assembly, particularly an assembly for sacrificial purposes." (Sacred Books of the East. Vol. 32 P 319-50). Some have said that, as sacred knowledge is the meaning of the word Veda, a sacred act is the meaning of विदथ (*vidatha*); but he concluded that there is no doubt that the sacrificial assembly is the chief meaning of this word.
The scholar Hermann Oldenberg, in his book Vedic Hymns, explaining the meaning of the word विदथे (*vidathe*) in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-31-6, stated his opinion thus. His words are cited, and afterwards his opinions are stated.
"On the derivation and meaning of विपथ (*vipatha*) Various opinions have been pronounced in the last years which have been collected by prof. Max Muller in his note on R. V. V. 592 (S. B.

### Printed p. 323
E. Vol. 32 P. 349 sq.) without traying to discuss here all different theories, I proceed to state my own opinion ; I propose to derive विदथ (*vidatha*) from वि+था (*vi+thā*) ; The Verb वि+था (*vi+thā*) means 'to distribute, to arrange, to Ordain :' thus the original meaning of विदथ (*vidatha*) must be like the meaning of विधान (*vidhāna*), 'distribution, disposition, ordinance ; In V. 3-6, we read विदधेषु अह्नां (*vidadheṣu ahnāṃ*) : this phrase receives its explanation by VII. 66-11 we may call attention also to VI. 51-2 within the sphere of the Vedic poets ' thoughts, the most prominent example of something most artificially विहित (*vihita*) was the sacrifice. यज्ञ (*yajña*) and विदथ (*vidatha*), 'Sacrifice' and 'ordinance' became nearly synonymous. (III 3-3) .... .... .... .... finally विदथ (*vidatha*) seems to mean 'the act of disposing of any business ' or the like ; this meaning appears, I believe, in some passages like the well-known phrase, बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ*)—may we with Valiant men mightily raise our voice a the determining of ordinances. Thus the word विदथ (*vidatha*) and सभा (*sabhā*) approach each other in thei meaning ; a person influential in council is called both विदथ्य (*vidathya*) and सभेय (*sabheya*) ". (Sacred Books of the East. Vol. 46 P. 27).
The main substance of this passage is as follows. The word विदध (*vidadha*) is formed by adding the prefix वि (*vi*) to the root धा (*dhā*). विधा (*vidhā*) means "distribute, ordain"; it can also mean observance of rule. That the meanings of the two words विदध (*vidadha*) and विधा (*vidhā*) are the same is understood from the equal sense of the words विदधेष्वह्नां (*vidadheṣvahnāṃ*) in the Ṛk
वयमग्ने वनुयाम त्वोता वसूयपो हविषा बुध्यमानाः । वयं समर्ये विदथेष्वह्नां वयं राया सहसस्पुत्र मर्तान् ॥ (*vayamagne vanuyāma tvotā vasūyapo haviṣā budhyamānāḥ | vayaṃ samarye vidatheṣvahnāṃ vayaṃ rāyā sahasasputra martān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3-6)
and the words वि-दधुः (*vi-dadhuḥ*) in the Ṛk
वि ये दधुः शरदं मासवादहरज्ञमक्तुं चादृचं । अनाप्यं वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा क्षत्रं राजान आशत । (*vi ye dadhuḥ śaradaṃ māsavādaharajñamaktuṃ cādṛcaṃ | anāpyaṃ varuṇo mitro aryamā kṣatraṃ rājāna āśata |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-66-11)
And further, the meanings of the words विहित (*vihita*) and विदथ (*vidatha*) being the same, the sacrifice ordained by the śāstra came to be called by the name विदथ (*vidatha*); the meaning "sacrifice or sacrificial assembly" remained at the end. So this scholar has explained.
The scholar Bloomfield, showing the faults in the meanings given by both Max Muller and Oldenberg, says that विदथ (*vidatha*) never comes to mean an assembly and that the meaning "private house" is the correct one. His opinion is as follows—

### Printed p. 324
##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The word विदथ (*vidatha*) contains the root विद् (*vid*) in its ordinary sense of 'get, acquire'. Lustead of this, the radical idea underlying the word was generally felt to be 'come to-gether,' 'assemble'. Again, the false start from the notion of 'assemblage' resulted in an approximation of the word to other words for assemblage, especially to सभा (*sabhā*) with which विदथ (*vidatha*) was almost identified. We shall see that there are decided points of contact between the words विदथ (*vidatha*) and सभा (*sabhā*) but the contact is that of extreme opposites in a way. विदथ (*vidatha*) refers to home matters; सभा (*sabhā*) generally to public matters.

In the interpretation of a term that figures prominently in the mystic-hieratic sphere of the Veda it is peculiarly necessary to search for its uses outside of that sphere, if there be such uses. In X-S5-26, the bride after the wedding ceremony proper, as the newly married couple are about to travel to their new home is addressed with the words "May Pushan taking thee by the hand lead thee home, may the Asvis lead thee forth on their car Go to your house in order that thou mayst be mistress of the house".

Now विदथ (*vidatha*) here, can but have one meaning, 'household' or something very like it. It certainly cannot refer to anything in the nature of a public gathering similar to the सभा (*sabhā*). In the phrase बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-16), the word सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*) refers to family relations not to public life.

Thus far, it may not be doubted, the word appears in the language of ordinary life. But it is found to a very much greater extent in the sacerdotal sphere: indeed, as all interpreters from Yaska on, have agreed, it often has a meaning very close to Yagna 'Sacrifice' itself. This is as it should be. In as much as the sacrifice is a private, home affair, it is very natural विदथ (*vidatha*) the house the premises the establishment, should figure prominently in connection with the sacrifice.

The gist of this article is: it is not correct that some have given the word विदथ (*vidatha*) the meaning सभा (*sabhā*) (assembly); the meaning of this word is a private home. The use of the word in this very sense can be noticed in many places. For example—

पूषा त्वेतो नयतु हस्तगृह्याश्विना त्वा प्रवहतां रथेन । गृहान् गच्छ गृहपत्नि यथासो वशिनी त्वं विदथवा वदासि ॥ (*pūṣā tveto nayatu hastagṛhyāśvinā tvā pravahatāṃ rathena | gṛhān gaccha gṛhapatni yathāso vaśinī tvaṃ vidathavā vadāsi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-85-26) **NOTE**

In this verse the meaning that Pūṣan and the Aśvin deities should lead you safely to the husband's house is clear. Moreover,

### Printed p. 325

ये स्तोतृभ्यो गोअग्रामश्वपेशसमग्ने रातिमुपसृजंति सूरयः । अस्माञ्च तांश्च प्र हि नेषि वस्य आ बृहद्वदेम विवर्थे सुवीराः ॥ (*ye stotṛbhyo goagrāmaśvapeśasamagne rātimupasṛjaṃti sūrayaḥ | asmāñca tāṃśca pra hi neṣi vasya ā bṛhadvadema vivarthe suvīrāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-16) **NOTE**

in this verse too, since सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*) is explained as शोभनपुत्रादिसहिताः (*śobhanaputrādisahitāḥ*), the meaning is "house". But since the sacrificial work is done in the house, in the present context it becomes "sacrificial house". The same meaning lies in what Yāska and others say, that it is a synonym of "sacrifice". Thus many Western scholars have given their own individual opinions. Since the different meanings house, sacrifice and assembly all fit, none of them can be called wrong. All these meanings are found in Sāyaṇa's commentary itself: for example in the Ṛks 10-85-26 and 27, 7-84-3 etc. he has explained it as "house", and in many Ṛks such as 10-100-6 and 10-111-7 as "sacrifice". It also comes to mean "sacrificial house" and "sacrificial assembly".

##### Grammar

- धृतप्रतीकम्—धृतात् प्रतीकाः यस्य स तम् । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति । द्वितीयाएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- धूर्षदम्—धुरि सीदति इति धूर्षद् । षद्लृ विशरण गत्यवसादनेषु धातुः । सत्तूद्विषद्रुह— (पा. सू. ३-२-६१) इति सुबंते उपपदे सति क्विप् प्रत्ययः । गतिकारकोपपदात् कृत्— (पा. सू. ६-२-१३९) इति कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- सं इधानः—इांधी नीप्त् धातुः । ताच्छिल्ये चानत् प्रत्ययः । अनिदितांहल— इति उपधानकारलोपः । चितः (पा. सू ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति । समासे कृमत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति । **NOTE**
- ऋज्जते—ऋंज भूषणे धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- इंधानः—इंधी दीप्तौ धातुः । रुधादिः । लडर्थे शानच् प्रत्ययः । रुधादिभ्यःक्षम्—(पा. सू. ३-१-७८) इति क्षम् विकरणः । अनुदात्तेत्ताद धातोः परे लसार्वधातुकं आगतत्वात् तास्यनुदात्तेत् (पा. सू. ६-१-१५६) इति अनुदात्तत्वात् धातुस्वरः अवतिष्ठते ।

### Printed p. 326

- अक्रः—क्रमु पादविक्षेपे धातुः । अन्यत्रापिदृश्यते इति वचनात् डप्रत्ययः (छांदसत्वात् ड प्रत्ययः) । डित्वसामर्थ्यात् धातोः टेः लोपः । नक्रः अक्रः कुदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- विदथेषु—विद ज्ञाने धातुः । अस्मात् औणादिकः अथ प्रत्ययः । जित्त्वविधानात् लघूपधगुणः न भवति । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । सप्तमीबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- शुक्रवर्णाम्—शुक्रः वर्णः यस्याः सा । ताम् । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- यंसते—यम उपरमे धातुः । लेट् प्रथमपुरुष एकवचने तप्रत्ययः । टितआत्मनेपदानां इति एत्वम् । लेटोडाटौ (पा. सू. ३-४-९४) इति तस्य अडागमः । सिब्बहुलं लेटि (पा सू. ३-१-३४) इति सिप् । धातुमकारस्य अनुस्वारः । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति । ॥ ७ ॥

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अप्र॑युच्छ॒न्नप्र॑युच्छद्भिरग्ने शि॒वेभि॑र्नः पा॒युभिः॑ पाहि  ॒
> शग्मैः ।  
> अद॑भेभि॒रदृ॑पितेभिरि॒ष्टेनि॑मिषद्भिः॒ परि॑ पाहि नो॒ जाः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अप्रयुच्छन् । अप्रयुच्छत्भिः ! अग्ने । शिवेभिः । नः । पायुभि । पाहि । शग्मैः ।  
> अदभेभिः । अदृपितेभिः । इष्टे । अनिमिषत्भिः । परि । पाहि । नः । जाः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> '। सः यज्ञभाष्यः ।'
> हे अग्ने अप्रयुच्छन्नस्मास्वप्रमाद्यन् ॥ युच्छ प्रमादे ॥ अविच्छिन्नप्रवृत्तिः
> सन्नप्रयुच्छद्भिरप्रमावृद्ध्भिरनवधानरहितैः शिवेभिर्मंत्रकल्याणैः शग्मैः सुखकरैः
> पायुभी रक्षणप्रकारैनोऽस्मान्साहि । रक्ष । किंच हे इष्टे सर्वैरेषणीयाग्ने जा
> जायमानोऽस्माभिर्दिर्प्यमानः सन्नदब्बेभिरहिंसित्यैरदृपितेभिः केनचिदप्यपरिभूतैः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Yajña-bhāṣya (Sāyaṇa's heading). "O Agni, अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*): not being heedless towards us" (root युच्छ (*yuccha*) in the sense of heedlessness). Being of unbroken activity, with अप्रयुच्छद्भिः (*aprayucchadbhiḥ*) (cares) free of heedlessness and lapses of attention, शिवेभिः (*śivebhiḥ*) (auspicious, as the mantra's blessings), शग्मैः (*śagmaiḥ*) (happiness-giving), पायुभिः (*pāyubhiḥ*) (modes of protection), पाहि (*pāhi*): guard us. Further, इष्टे (*iṣṭe*): O Agni who art sought by all; जाः (*jāḥ*): being born, kindled by us, with अदब्बेभिः (*adabbebhiḥ*) (unharmed) and अदृपितेभिः (*adṛpitebhiḥ*) (overcome by none) (cares). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 327

> दृप द्वन्न उत्केशे तौदादिकः ॥ आनिमिषद्भिर्निमेषरहितैरनलसस्वभावैः ईद्रशैललक्षणैर्नोऽस्मान्परि परितः पाहि । पालय । यद्वा । उपर्युपरि जायंत इति जाः । नो
> जा अस्मत्संबंधिनीः पुत्रपौत्रादिरूपाः प्रजाः परि पाहि । परितो रक्ष । न केवलमस्मान् किंत्वस्मत्पूत्रपौत्रादीनपि रक्ष ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

दृप (*dṛpa*) in the sense of exuberance, of the Tudādi class. अनिमिषद्भिः (*animiṣadbhiḥ*): free from winking, of unslothful nature; with such characteristics protect us all round. Or else: those who are born upon and upon (again) are जाः (*jāḥ*); so "नो जाः (*no jāḥ*)": protect all round our offspring in the form of sons, grandsons and so on. Not only us but also our sons and grandsons protect.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- अप्रयुच्छ (*aprayuccha*)—without being heedless (in our matter)
- अप्रयुच्छद्भिः (*aprayucchadbhiḥ*)—not heedless
- शिवेभिः (*śivebhiḥ*)—conducive to welfare
- शग्मैः (*śagmaiḥ*)—conducive to happiness
- पायुभिः (*pāyubhiḥ*)—by modes of protection
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—protect
- इष्टे (*iṣṭe*)—O Agni, desired by all
- जाः (*jāḥ*)—born from us
- अदभीभिः (*adabhībhiḥ*)—unobstructed
- अदृपितेभिः (*adṛpitebhiḥ*)—not subject to another (**NOTE**)
- अनिमिषद्भिः (*animiṣadbhiḥ*)—not of sluggish nature; with protections (that are such)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- परि (*pari*)—all round
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—protect
- [Or नः (*naḥ*)—belonging to us; जाः (*jāḥ*)—our sons, grandsons and so on; परि पाहि (*pari pāhi*)—protect all round.]

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, being very watchful in our matter, protect us with protections that are not heedless, auspicious and happiness-giving. O Agni, desired by all, as soon as you are born from us, protect all round both us and our sons, grandsons and so on, with protections that are unobstructed, that do not become subject to another, and that are not sluggish.

##### Printed English

Agni, never heedless (of us), guard us with never heedless, auspicious, and joy-bestowing cares : do you, who are desired (by all), protect us, and those born of us, with unobstructed, unovercome, and never-slumbering (vigilance).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the protection of Agni is prayed for. The manner of that protection is told by the epithets beginning with अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*).

अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)—युच्छ प्रमादे । अप्रयुच्छन् । अस्मद्रक्षणे जागरूकस्सन् । (*yuccha pramāde | aprayucchan | asmadrakṣaṇe jāgarūkassan |*) Formed from the root युच्छ (*yuccha*), which gives the meaning heedlessness, being careless, and joined with the prefixes, it gives the sense of not being heedless.

### Printed p. 328

As in Ṛks such as

त्वं दूतस्त्वमु नः परस्सास्त्वं वस्य अ वृषभ प्रणेता । अग्ने तोकस्य नस्तने तनूनामप्रयच्छन्वीद्यद्भोधि गोपाः । (*tvaṃ dūtastvamu naḥ parassāstvaṃ vasya a vṛṣabha praṇetā | agne tokasya nastane tanūnāmaprayacchanvīdyadbhodhi gopāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-9-2)

where Agni is prayed to protect with watchfulness, so it is told here too.

शग्मैः (*śagmaiḥ*)—The word शग्म (*śagma*) is read among the names of happiness (Ni. 3-13). Sāyaṇa has generally everywhere given this word the meaning happiness or happiness-giving. But in

तं शग्मासो अरुषासो अश्वा बृहस्पतिं सहवाहो वहंति । सहश्चिद्यस्य नीलवत्सधस्थं नभो न रूपमरुषं वसानाः । (*taṃ śagmāso aruṣāso aśvā bṛhaspatiṃ sahavāho vahaṃti | sahaścidyasya nīlavatsadhasthaṃ nabho na rūpamaruṣaṃ vasānāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-97-6)

and in the Ṛk

एह हरी ब्रह्मयुजा शग्मा वक्षतः सखायं । गीर्भिः श्रुतं गिर्वणसं ॥ (*eha harī brahmayujā śagmā vakṣataḥ sakhāyaṃ | gīrbhiḥ śrutaṃ girvaṇasaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-2-27)

he has given the meaning "conducive to happiness" or "able". Bhaṭṭa-bhāṣya (Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara) has shown that the word शग्म (*śagma*) used in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā sentence

पर्वतादा सरस्वती यजता गंतु यज्ञं । हवं देवी जुजुषाणा घृताची शग्मां नो वाचमुशती शृणोतु ॥ (*parvatādā sarasvatī yajatā gaṃtu yajñaṃ | havaṃ devī jujuṣāṇā ghṛtācī śagmāṃ no vācamuśatī śṛṇotu ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-8-22-2)

and in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa sentence

तपसास्यानुवर्तये । शिवेनास्कोपवर्तये । शग्नेनास्काभिवर्तये । (*tapasāsyānuvartaye | śivenāskopavartaye | śagnenāskābhivartaye |*) (Tai. Brā. 1-5-5-2) **NOTE**

has the meaning "capable" or "a special power". From these explanations it can be said that this word is formed from the root शक् (*śak*) in the sense of power; it gives the meaning "able" or "capable". But

रथवाहनं हविरस्य नाम यत्रायुथं निहितमस्य वर्म । तत्रा रथमुप शग्मं सदेम विश्वाहा वयं सुमनस्यमानाः ॥ (*rathavāhanaṃ havirasya nāma yatrāyuthaṃ nihitamasya varma | tatrā rathamupa śagmaṃ sadema viśvāhā vayaṃ sumanasyamānāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-25-8)

in Ṛks like this alone the meaning must be "conducive to happiness". In the present Ṛk both meanings, "happiness-giving" and "able", fit.

इष्टे (*iṣṭe*)—सर्वैः विषणीयाग्ने (*sarvaiḥ viṣaṇīyāgne*). Agni is addressed as one desired by all, that is, by all, gods, men and the rest.

### Printed p. 329

अदब्बेभिः (*adabbebhiḥ*)—अहिंसितैः (*ahiṃsitaiḥ*), "not harmed by anyone"; it is an epithet of the word पायुभिः (*pāyubhiḥ*). Uses of words formed from the root दभ् (*dabh*), meaning to harm, to destroy, are mostly Vedic only. The use of the word अदब्ब (*adabba*), giving the meaning "unharmed, not to be destroyed", is also mostly Vedic. Generally when Agni is prayed to for protection, the epithet and the qualified, अदब्बेभिः पायुभिः (*adabbebhiḥ pāyubhiḥ*) or अदब्बेभिः गोपाभिः (*adabbebhiḥ gopābhiḥ*), are used together. In Ṛks such as

अदब्बेभिस्तव गोपाभिरिष्टेऽस्माकं पाहि त्रिषधस्थ सूरीन् । रक्षा च नो वदुषां शर्धो अग्ने वैश्वानर प्र च तारीः स्तवानः । (*adabbebhistava gopābhiriṣṭe 'smākaṃ pāhi triṣadhastha sūrīn | rakṣā ca no vaduṣāṃ śardho agne vaiśvānara pra ca tārīḥ stavānaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-8-7)

it is an epithet of गोपाभिः (*gopābhiḥ*); and as in the present Ṛk, in Ṛks such as
